- [x] `MapGenerator.generate()` took 7 positional parameters, 4 of them booleans, after the four AST-driven features each appended one — every call site had become unreadable and trivially easy to mis-order. Collapsed params 2-7 into a single `GenerateOptions` object (`{ bonusLevel, hasRocketLauncher, missingWeaponIndices, maxPlayers, hasSmg, hasGas }`), with a `Required<GenerateOptions>` defaults record so a future option can't silently default to `undefined` inside the body. All 4 `main.ts` call sites, 3 verify/telemetry scripts and 11 test call sites converted. Purely a call-shape change: verified byte-identical by digesting every `demo-campaign/` map across 6 option shapes (102 SHA-256s) before and after — all identical, so no layout and no recorded replay moved

- [x] Deploy from CI action, when tag is pushed/created (allow manual starting for testing [must still use most recent tag then]). FTP creds live in github secrets `DEPLOY_HOST/_USER/_PASS` — `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, triggers on any tag push or manual `workflow_dispatch`; a manual run pins itself to the most-recently-created tag (`git describe` on `git rev-list --tags --max-count=1`) before building, so a test run always deploys what a real tag push would. Builds via `npm run build` (same as the verify workflow) and uploads `dist/` via `SamKirkland/FTP-Deploy-Action@v4.4.0`

- [x] tsc/build/lint CI gate — `verify (no browser)` now runs `npm run build` (`tsc --noEmit && vite build`) right after `npm ci`. Prompted by the TypeScript 5.9.3→7.0.2 dependabot bump (#4): the new DOM lib types widened `HTMLElement.hidden` to `boolean | "until-found"`, breaking a `classList.toggle` call in `src/ui/fileTree.ts` — Vitest (esbuild-transpiled, no type-check) and the Playwright job (dev server, no build) both stayed green and the PR merged clean, so the break only surfaced on the next local `npm run build`. Fixed by coercing to `Boolean(open)` at the call site; CI now catches this class of break before merge.

- [x] enhance weapon "sprites", they cause eyecancer, and they weren't aligned to the actual position ammo is fired from — the viewmodel was drawn 2% right of true screen-center (`cx = w * 0.52 + bobX`) while `RaycasterEngine.fire()`'s tracer/flame-stream have always launched from a fixed, simulation-adjacent point at exact screen-bottom-center; first attempt wrongly moved the tracer to chase the gun's drawn position, but the correct direction is the reverse — the gun is the cosmetic element, so it's the one that should conform to the fixed shot origin. Fixed by recentering the viewmodel (`WEAPON_CENTER_BIAS` 0.52 → 0.5 in `viewmodel.ts`) instead of touching `effects.ts`/`engine.ts`'s tracer origin at all. Every weapon's Canvas-2D silhouette also got a polish pass (dark outline strokes + a bottom-shadow band to match the existing top-highlight, giving flat fills some bevel) while keeping the existing primitive-shape style rather than a full redesign

- [x] enhance soundfx, esp. weapon fire — every weapon's fire sound was previously the same shared `audio.playShoot()` blip; each of the 7 weapons now has its own procedurally-synthesized voice dispatched off `weapon.viewKind` (`src/engine/audio.ts`): pistol keeps the original snap, shotgun layers a distorted thump with a filtered noise burst, gdb gets a cheap high-pitched tick, ghidra gets a rising launch whoosh (distinct from its existing impact boom), Friday Hotfix gets a continuously-blended rumble+hiss jet, the knife/Toolchain get an airy whoosh / gritty revving buzz respectively — gdb/Friday Hotfix/Toolchain's rapid auto-fire retriggers add a small `Math.random()` pitch jitter so a burst doesn't sound clone-stamped

- [x] flamethrower must burn 2.5x gas considering its dmg — Friday Hotfix's `ammoPerShot` raised from `1` to `2.5` (`src/engine/weapons.ts`), same flat cost every other weapon pays otherwise; HUD's gas readout now floors the value (`src/engine/hud.ts`) since the pool can land on a half-unit

- [x] antialiasing for wall edges — the top/bottom silhouette against ceiling/floor is now sub-pixel blended per column instead of floored to whole pixels (which caused the classic raycaster "staircase" jaggies), by proportionally alpha-blending the one boundary row at each edge with whatever's already painted underneath (`renderScene` in `src/engine/raycaster.ts`); zBuffer/sprite-occlusion resolution is untouched; verified headlessly by bundling `raycaster.ts` and rendering a synthetic wall in Playwright/Chromium, confirming the boundary pixel is a proportional blend of wall and background color [Task 71]

- [x] console hints keep coming when game is paused, spamming away previous output — engine now fires an `onFreezeChange` edge event on the paused/lore-frozen ↔ running transition, which the console sidebar uses to suspend its ambient hint timer while frozen (resumes once unpaused)

- [x] "Suggested repos" section at GitHub loading — four quick-pick buttons (Easy: FizzBuzzEnterpriseEdition, Medium: DOOM, Hard: webpack, Nightmare: magento2) below the repo input fill it in and load immediately, sharing the existing fetch-and-launch flow

- [x] Progress indicator for github loads — the recursive tree fetch (the one genuinely slow step for a big repo) now streams and shows a running "X MB received" counter, and the "Scanning file tree…" fallback pass (each extensionless file costs a real network round-trip) shows a "(checked/total)" counter, instead of one static message

- [x] Highscore table dialog wordwrapped every column — widened dialog (640px→960px) and made only the long-text columns (Campaign, Ended On) wrap; # / Score / Lines / Complexity / Levels / Hash / Replay stay on one line now

- [x] to few enemies — complexity packs (1 extra enemy per 10 pts) [Task 18]

- [x] increase aggro distance — 5 → 7.5 tiles [Task 18]

- [x] enemies sometimes invisible when very close to the player — sprite near-clip fix [Task 18]

- [x] enemies shouldnt be melee only — ranged plasma bolts [Task 19]

- [x] enemies should roam around in their rooms, not wait statically (without leaving the room) [Task 18]

- [x] enemies dont attack when shot from outside aggro distance — damage aggro [Task 18]

- [x] return and enemy placed at the same location — exit tile kept clear of spawns [Task 18]

- [x] to less ammo — defeated enemies drop heap (ammo) pickups [Task 11]

- [x] strafing — A/D strafe perpendicular to the camera [Task 20]

- [x] running — Shift sprints at 2x move speed [Task 20]

- [x] floors to long / paths boring — long corridors now jog/turn instead of one straight sightline; large rooms get 1-3 pillars to break up empty space [Task 21]

- [x] goto usage should result in a teleport portal, like they exist in e.g UT — `goto`/label pairs are now parsed (C + PHP) and turned into linked, glowing violet teleporter pad pairs; stepping on one warps to the other [Task 22]

- [x] multi-level / jump to next level on end of level — reaching `return` now auto-loads the next parsable file in the tree (depth-first, dirs-first) instead of ending the run, carrying health/ammo across; only shows the win screen on the last file [Task 22]

- [x] add languages: asm, js, ts, python, java, c++, objc (and other realworld languages, as long as supported by our libs - we dont want to use multiple libs for that) — added JS, TS/TSX, Python, Java, C++, Go, Rust, Ruby, C#, Bash, Scala, Objective-C via one generic Tree-sitter-driven adapter (still just tree-sitter, no second parsing lib); no ASM (no ABI-compatible prebuilt grammar found), Kotlin/Lua skipped (no prebuilt wasm shipped by their packages) [Task 23]

- [x] ensure code parser only acts on actual supported code, in case some troll loads his meme folder or whatever. ensure that loaded code is never executed. — strict extension whitelist (only registered adapters' extensions reach a parser at all), plus a size cap + binary-content sniff on top; loaded text is only ever fed to `Parser.parse()` (builds an AST), never eval'd/compiled/run; any parse failure now logs a warning and skips the file instead of crashing the map generator or game loop [Task 23]

- [x] map top left should be semi-transparent. player and direction should be more clearly visible, same for the exit. enemies shouldnt be show until the room was entered. — always-on minimap now uses a semi-transparent dark rgba backing panel, a bright solid triangle for the player's exact position/facing, a pulsing high-contrast exit marker, and enemies stay hidden until the player's collision box AABB-intersects their room (sticky once discovered) [Task 24]

- [x] more traps (kinds, but also placement) — added timed spike traps (tile toggles safe/damaging on a per-trap cycle) and invisible proximity mines (reveal + detonate for AoE damage if you linger, timer resets if you back off in time), both scattered procedurally at 1-tile-wide corridor choke points rather than open room floor. Not exhaustive — more kinds/placement variety is still open for later [Task 24]

- [x] if the AST has a detectable entrypoint (like `main()` for c, index.php for PHP etc), use that a starting level after loading the workspace — workspace load now searches for a standard entrypoint filename (main.c/cpp/py/go/rs, index.php, index.js, Program.cs, etc.) across the whole tree, falling back to a content-based `main()` function scan for the C family, and auto-launches it immediately; falls back further to the first parsable file in tree order if nothing matches [Task 24]

- [x] mines need to be visible way earlier, its almost impossible to avoid them — sight radius (visible) and fuse-arming radius (actually dangerous) are now separate: mines reveal from 4.5 tiles away but only start their fuse within 1.8, giving real reaction room. Also added the ability to shoot a spotted mine to disarm it outright — safe from beyond its blast radius, but still hurts point-blank — since spotting one you can't do anything about wasn't useful

- [x] drop, enemies, exit etc should be in front based on drawing distance from POV - for example: sometimes a dropped item next to exit "disappears" behind the exit — every world billboard (enemies, projectiles, keys, ammo drops, exit marker, teleporters, decorations, mines) is now collected into one list and drawn in a single depth-sorted pass (furthest-to-nearest), instead of each category drawing in a fixed order regardless of actual distance [Task 25]

- [x] canvas looses focus on level change - bug — `canvas.focus()` is now called right after the canvas is attached to the DOM on every level (re)load, so WASD works immediately without an extra click first [Task 25]

- [x] ensure the player spawns far enough outside the aggro range of any enemy, except that's not possible without leaving the room — spawn now picks whichever corner of the first room has the greatest minimum distance to any enemy-bearing room's center, instead of a fixed corner; best-effort (small/dense levels may still have no fully safe corner), but strictly better than before [Task 25]

- [x] pause game if canvas not focussed / ESC was pressed — window blur now forces a pause (and Escape toggles one explicitly), freezing the sim and drawing a "PAUSED" overlay over one frozen frame; click or a second Escape resumes. Distinct from the existing Tab automap pause [Task 25]

- [x] fullscreen support for the canvas — F toggles the Fullscreen API on the canvas, called directly from the keydown handler (required for browsers to accept it as a real user gesture) [Task 25]

- [x] level start screen - showing stats about AST and the resulting lvl (enemy, room etc count). if there is a detectable project name of the loaded workspace: use that as "campaign" name, if not: use directory name except its `src`, in that case use the upper level directory name. use the filename as level name. game should be paused till the player ack's that screen. Also add a "Commit Summary" (Level End Screen) showing stats like "X Lines refactored, Y Bugs squashed". — both overlays added, blocking play until acknowledged. Campaign name = workspace root name; the "parent dir if named src" fallback isn't reachable in a browser sandbox (the File System Access API only grants a handle to the picked directory itself, no way to walk up to its parent) so a root literally named "src" just uses "src" as-is — documented in code. Commit summary shows the cleared file's line count and a new per-level kill counter [Task 26]

- [x] save & quit (campaign persistence) — save the current campaign state (current file, HP, ammo, score, weapons) to `localStorage`. add a "Continue Run" button in the main menu for navigating massive repositories over multiple sessions. — autosaves (throttled during play, immediately on level transitions) to localStorage; "Continue Run" button re-picks the workspace (file handles can't be persisted across sessions) and resumes at the saved path with carried-over health/ammo/weapon, falling back to a fresh entrypoint launch if the saved file isn't found. Cleared on death or campaign completion [Task 26]

- [x] enemy stuck in or on a wall — first-in-pack spawns and random pack-member/roam positions could land exactly on a tile boundary (room-center math wasn't floored) or, in labyrinth/maze rooms, anywhere in the room's bounding rectangle including wall tiles; only the single tile under the raw position got force-cleared, so a straddling collision box could still overlap an uncleared neighbor. Every candidate spawn/roam position is now snapped to its containing tile's exact center first, guaranteeing the enemy's collision box stays fully inside the one tile that does get cleared

- [x] aggro picks up through wall, should be line of sight only (or when shot) — proximity aggro now requires an unobstructed line of sight (reusing the same ray-sampling check ranged enemy fire already used) in addition to being in range; damage aggro (getting shot) still bypasses it entirely, since being hit at all proves you've been spotted [Task 27]

- [x] player needs an unlimited meelee weapon ("SIGKILL Knife" / "Refactoring Crowbar") to be able to continue when out of ammo until dead or looting ammo. should feature a small lifesteal effect (+1 HP) or force a guaranteed ammo drop on kill to encourage aggressive play — added as weapon slot 3: 0 ammo cost, only connects within 1.5 tiles regardless of what's aimed at further away, heals +1 stability per kill. (Every kill already dropped ammo unconditionally, so the "guaranteed drop" half was already covered game-wide.) [Task 27]

- [x] weapons should have a range determined by their type (or realistic equivalent) — hitscan weapons should use a "Cone of Fire" (spread angle that widens with distance) instead of a hard drop-off to 0 damage — ranged pellets (pistol/shotgun) now get a random column deviation scaled to the z-buffer depth of whatever's downrange, so a shot lined up on something far away can still miss, while point-blank shots stay accurate; melee is exempt (it can't reach that far to begin with). No weapon previously had any range cutoff at all, so this replaces "unlimited perfect accuracy" rather than an actual drop-off [Task 27]

- [x] acid pools look almost the same as the exit on the minimap, needs differantiaton — acid tiles recolored to a hot amber/orange (`#ff9d1f`) on both the always-on HUD minimap and the full Tab automap (which, it turned out, wasn't rendering hazard tiles distinctly at all — they fell through to the plain-floor case), so neither can be confused with the green pulsing exit marker [Task 27]

- [x] more weapons, to be randomly dropped by enemies. Like a rocket-like-launcher for AoE damage, or an MP. — added the MP (fully automatic, high fire rate/low damage) and Rocket Launcher (slow real projectile, AoE splash, self-damage included) as weapon slots 4/5. Neither is owned from the start — only an Elite kill's guaranteed drop unlocks one, so "randomly dropped by enemies" is scoped to Elites specifically rather than any kill (a deliberate call: making them freely available from the start would make the drop meaningless) [Task 28]

- [x] each weapon should use unique ammo, also resulting in a slightly but noticeable different tracer — ammo split into two pools (Bullets: pistol/shotgun/MP, Rockets: launcher only); every weapon now has its own tracer color (yellow pistol, orange-ish shotgun, silver knife slash, blue MP, orange rocket) [Task 28]

- [x] ammo should also be randomly placed across maps for player pickup, only as a sparse backup - primary source should be spawn + loot. — `placeAmmoPickups` scatters a sparse handful of bullets/rockets pickups across non-spawn rooms at generation time, mostly bullets with rockets rarer [Task 28]

- [x] weapon should be rendered differently, depending on their type/strength — every weapon (including the pre-existing pistol/shotgun/knife) now has its own distinct bottom-of-screen silhouette instead of one generic reused blaster shape: boxy single-barrel pistol, wider double-barrel shotgun, an actually-connected blade+handle knife, a slim long-barreled MP with a stick mag, and a big shoulder-mounted rocket tube [Task 28]

- [x] collectible health and armor drops (randomly dropped on top of ammo by enemies) — regular enemy kills now roll a weighted random drop (bullets/rockets/health/armor); armor is a new player stat, absorbed 1:1 before health on any hit, capped at 100 [Task 28]

- [x] elite enemies / bosses — instead of just spawning *more* enemies (complexity packs), functions with extreme cyclomatic complexity (e.g., massive legacy controllers) spawn a single Elite Enemy (different tint/sprite, 4x HP, high damage, guaranteed weapon drop). — functions at/above complexity 40 (exactly where a pack would otherwise hit its biggest size) spawn one Elite instead: 1.5x sprite scale, a gold tint/HP-bar/label, 4x a single enemy's HP at that complexity, 2x melee/ranged damage. Death guarantees either a still-unowned MP/Rocket Launcher or a large (50) health pack once both are owned [Task 28]

- [x] secret rooms (Secrets) — parse AST for "Dead Code" (unreachable code, blocks after returns) or large comment blocks. generate these as hidden rooms off the main map, accessible via fake walls, containing high-value loot (mega-health, rocket ammo). — unreachable statements after a `return` (C/PHP/generic adapters) each carve a small room behind a fake wall tile that looks and blocks exactly like a normal wall; opening one drops a guaranteed mega-health or fat rockets pickup inside [Task 29]. Follow-up: made purely 100% indistinguishable was too obscure to ever realistically find — fake walls now carry a very slight cool hue nudge off the real wall color (3D scene, minimap, and automap all use the same tiny tint) so a player who looks closely has a real shot at spotting one

- [x] lore terminals — parse code comments (`//` or `/* */`) into interactable glowing wall textures. pressing 'E' opens a text popup with the original code comment. — large comments become glowing wall tiles near the room they came from; interacting nearby pauses the game and shows the raw comment text. Uses R, not E — E is already the turn-right key in this game's Q/E-turn scheme (see [[feedback-control-scheme]]) [Task 29]. Follow-up: a long comment could overflow the overlay box with no way to read the rest — the box now caps its own height and holding W/S scrolls the wrapped text, with a scrollbar shown only when it actually overflows

- [x] by accident, header files (.h) make nice bonus levels for restocking ammo. lets visually differentiate them from normal source levels. loot chances / drops should be slightly higher as well, since its a single closed room with at least one enemy — `.h` files now generate with a distinct cool steel/teal wall+floor theme, a boosted static-pickup rate/amount, and enemy kills lean toward scarcer/higher-value drops [Task 29]

- [x] if not fullscreen: show console log near to the canvas, randomly drop hints about traps being near and similar — a DOM sidebar next to the canvas mirrors `console.log` and occasionally drops a random in-character hint while a level is running; hidden automatically while the canvas is fullscreen (a DOM sibling can't render in that state) [Task 29]. Follow-up: raw object dumps (e.g. logging a whole `ParsedFile`/`GameMap`) ruined the effect and readability — the sidebar now only ever mirrors an actual string message (an object-only call is dropped entirely, a trailing object after a string message is ignored, not stringified), and any surviving line is length-capped. Second follow-up: the per-level `[map] ...` log line printed the exit tile's exact coordinates in plain text — a spoiler the sidebar then mirrored verbatim; removed from the printed string (the full map object is still passed through for devtools inspection, which takes a deliberate click to expand, not a passive read)

- [x] avoid pixelation at fullscreen — `.scene-canvas` and `.scene-canvas:fullscreen` both now declare `image-rendering: crisp-edges` (fallback) then `pixelated` explicitly, rather than relying purely on cascade inheritance into the `:fullscreen` state [Task 30]

- [x] mousewheel weapon switching (shoulder keys on gamepad) — the mousewheel now cycles through owned, non-melee weapons (wrapping), bound to the canvas so it never hijacks scrolling the file-tree/console sidebar. Gamepad shoulder-key support not implemented at the time — no gamepad support existed in this engine yet at all (see the general gamepad/joystick support entry below, added in [Task 33]) [Task 30]

- [x] melee weapon can be used with LEFT CTRL, dont add it to the weapon list / number hotkeys — the SIGKILL Knife is now unreachable via number keys or the mousewheel (excluded by its own `meleeRange` field, not a hardcoded slot) and is bound exclusively to Left-Ctrl as an instant "quick-melee" swing: a pure viewmodel overlay independent of whatever ranged weapon is equipped — `weaponIndex`, `ownedWeapons`, and the HUD ammo label never change [Task 30]

- [x] option for gorelevel, which controls the amount of blood effects. normal/default is current state, add "no gore", "more gore" and "extreme gore" options on top. — a Gore dropdown in the sidebar (None/Normal/More/Extreme = 0x/1x/3x/10x) persisted to localStorage, scaling blood-particle count, rendered size, and how long a landed particle lingers as a floor stain (a genuinely new concept — previously every particle vanished under a second regardless of whether it had landed) [Task 30]. Follow-up: Extreme (10x) reads as over-the-top in practice — hidden from the dropdown for now behind `EXTREME_GORE_ENABLED = false` in effects.ts (same disable-behind-a-flag pattern as `DECORATIONS_ENABLED`); a previously-saved "extreme" preference downgrades to "more" instead. Also found and fixed a real, previously-shipped bug while verifying this: `GORE_KEY`'s `const` was declared below the point where `loadGoreLevel()` was first called, so every read hit its temporal-dead-zone, threw, got silently swallowed by an empty `catch`, and the Gore setting never actually persisted across a reload — it always silently reset to Normal regardless of what was saved. Second follow-up (re-enabling Extreme, 2026-07-17): root-caused the "over-the-top"/"mountain of blood" complaint — `renderBlood`'s particle size formula (`tilePx * 0.05 * sizeMultiplier`) had no upper clamp, and `tilePx` (pixels per world tile) grows unbounded as depth approaches the close-range render cutoff, so a single old-extreme (10x) point-blank particle could render at ~800px, nearly twice the real gameplay canvas's own height — that's the literal mountain, not particle accumulation. Fixed three ways: retuned `GORE_MULTIPLIERS.extreme` to `{count:16, size:4, stainDuration:4}` (size/stainDuration cut well below a naive 10x, since size scales rendered area quadratically and old extreme had count×size×stainDuration all maxed together, a ~1000x "visual mass" jump over `more`'s 27x; count bumped from an initial 10 to 16 once the size fix made more particles safe to add back — user-requested follow-up, same day); added an unconditional hard pixel clamp in `renderBlood` (`BLOOD_MAX_SIZE_FRACTION = 0.06`, 6% of canvas height — applies to every gore tier, not just extreme, since `more` was technically unclamped too); added a hard cap on live particle count (`MAX_BLOOD_PARTICLES = 300`, FIFO-evict-oldest in `spawnBlood`) as defense-in-depth against the newly-shipped Multi/Ultra Kill feature chaining several enemies' hits within a few seconds. `EXTREME_GORE_ENABLED` flipped back to `true`. Verified: full Vitest suite (1543 tests) at 100% coverage including new clamp/eviction tests in `effects.test.ts` and rewritten/added `main.test.ts` cases for the now-enabled dropdown option and a mocked-disabled-flag branch-coverage case; live check driving the real `renderBlood`/`spawnBlood`/`updateBlood` functions against a real canvas in a real browser (headless Playwright's pointer-lock support turned out too unreliable for genuine in-game combat navigation — confirmed via `getPlayerState()` showing near-zero movement/shots fired despite held input) confirmed a point-blank multi-hit burst renders as small uniform clamped squares, and a simulated 6-kill chain produces a dense-but-bounded spatter pattern confined to a modest fraction of the screen, not a solid mass climbing toward enemy-sprite height

- [x] fps (if possible: also gpu/cpu usage) display, toggable - default off. use RIGHT_CTRL as hotkey (for now) — Right-Ctrl toggles a top-right FPS + frame-time readout (averaged twice a second, from the real unclamped frame delta so a stutter doesn't get invisibly floored by the sim's own dt clamp), visible across every render state including the automap/pause/lore overlays. GPU/CPU usage not implemented — no standard browser API exposes that to page JS in a sandboxed context [Task 30]

- [x] name mp5 "gdb" and rocket launcher "ghidra" — renamed in weapons.ts's WEAPONS array (only the display `name` field; array indices/ammo/behavior unchanged), plus every doc comment, hint caption, and console-sidebar hint that referenced the old names [Task 31]

- [x] cone of fire / distance spread is a bit extreme — the aim-deviation formula is now quadratic in range (scaled by `(range/FOG_FAR)²`) instead of linear, so medium-range shots stay noticeably tighter and only the true maximum visual range (where the world fades to black anyway) spreads as much as before [Task 31]

- [x] multikill score bonus, sfx and a text-gfx in the canvas (ut like) (starting at 3 kills in 3sec, becomes an ultrakill yielding even more points at 6 kills in 6 secs) — a rolling-window kill-streak check in `engine.ts`'s new `registerKillForStreak()` (called from `damageEnemy` right after each kill) compares the in-window kill count before/after adding the new kill, so each tier fires exactly once per streak: 3 kills within 3s triggers a "MULTI KILL!" banner (amber, 300 bonus points), 6 kills within 6s an "ULTRA KILL!" banner instead (red/bigger, 750 bonus points) — Ultra is checked first since 6-in-6 implies 3-in-3 already fired earlier in the same streak. New procedurally-synthesized SFX pair (`playMultiKill`/`playUltraKill` in `audio.ts`, modeled on the existing `playExplosion`/`playRocketExplosion` "smaller vs. bigger" precedent — Ultra adds two more arpeggio notes plus a noise-burst layer). New `drawKillStreakToast` in `hud.ts` — a bold, UT-style banner in the upper third of the screen, frame-counted fade like the existing cheat-activation toast, sized/colored by tier. `scoring.ts`'s `computeScore` gained a flat `multikillBonus` field (count × per-tier bonus, same pattern as the existing lore/secret-room bonuses). A temporary `demo-campaign/stage99_multikill_playtest.c` level (13 trivial low-complexity functions, ~97 enemies) was added for manually playtesting/hearing it in a real browser (audio can't be verified by any automated/headless run by design — `AudioManager.isSilenced()` mutes all SFX under browser automation on purpose) — user confirmed it works (2026-07-17), level removed afterward.

- [x] full favicon/icon set generated from a user-supplied "CODE" logo image — `public/` now has `favicon.ico` (multi-size), standalone 16/32/48/96px PNGs, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `android-chrome-{192,512}.png` plus maskable variants, `mstile-*.png` + `browserconfig.xml`, and `site.webmanifest`, all wired into `index.html`. Generated by cropping the source to its real alpha-channel content bbox (not the full padded canvas) and compositing onto transparent (browser/Android) or opaque `#1a1a1f` (iOS/Windows, which render transparency as solid black) backgrounds as appropriate — see `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s Favicons entry for the full detail.

- [x] SEO optimize the page for dist build / hosted gameplay — static-meta SEO (no SSR/prerendering, out of scope — pure client-side SPA): `meta description`, canonical link, Open Graph + Twitter Card tags, a minimal schema.org `VideoGame` JSON-LD block, and a one-line `<noscript>` notice added to `index.html`; `public/robots.txt` + `public/sitemap.xml` (single-URL, this is a one-page app); `package.json` gained `description`/`homepage`. Canonical URL: `https://codeenstein3d.mcdope.org/` (intended hosting domain — no live deploy exists yet). New `public/og-image.png` (1200x630, opaque) generated from the same CODE logo source as the favicons, tagline text overlaid. See `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s SEO entry for the full detail.

- [x] balancing telemetry should track score reached — score was already threaded through the engine's `getTelemetrySnapshot()` (`stats.score`, `RaycasterEngine.buildStats()`) and per-level averaged in `scripts/run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`'s `aggregateLevelRuntime`, but nothing surfaced *the score a run actually ends at* — the per-level figure pools every level's own end score together (mixing early-level low scores with late-level cumulative ones), which answers "what does a typical level-end score look like," not "how far did a run's score climb by the time it stopped." Added `computeFinalScoreReached(qualifyingRuns)`: takes each qualifying run's *last* level snapshot's `score` (already the correct running-campaign-total semantics per `EngineStats.score`'s doc comment) and runs it through the same `spread(..., "mean")` helper every other per-run stat (`weaponAcquisitionRate`, etc.) already uses — new `finalScoreReached` field on `buildComboOutput`'s per-combo (profile×difficulty) output, alongside `qualifyingRunCount`/`weaponAcquisitionRate`.

- [x] Forced weapon unlock for MP at the 4th level, 8th for rocket launcher (in a campaign only) — a 1-based campaign level counter (persisted in the campaign save) force-adds gdb/ghidra to `ownedWeapons` once reached, regardless of whether an Elite ever dropped them; never removes anything, so an already-looted weapon is unaffected. Only advances via the auto-chain (`advanceToNextLevel`), not manual sidebar picks [Task 31]

- [x] code smell detection to increase complexity and/or difficulty — functions with >5 parameters or >3 nesting levels now get bonus complexity points (scaled by how far over the threshold, not a flat penalty), added on top of the normal decision-point score in all three parser paths (C, PHP, generic). A smelly function ends up tougher (or spawns as an Elite at extreme complexity) same as genuinely complex control flow already did [Task 31]

- [x] option for difficulty — an Easy/Normal/Hard sidebar dropdown (persisted to localStorage) scales enemy HP (0.7x/1x/1.5x, rescaled in-place on the map's Enemy objects at engine construction), enemy-dealt damage (melee/ranged only, not traps/hazards/self-splash), and ammo/health/armor pickup amounts (1.3x/1x/0.7x, inverse of HP — harder difficulty is scarcer too, not just tougher) [Task 31]

- [x] scoring (HUD has a Score slot since Task 20, always 0 — no scoring logic yet), extra points for health, ammo left, path length (shorter = better) and time needed (faster = better) at end of level, plus points for every kill (depending on enemy). score malus at end of level for health below 100% — `src/engine/scoring.ts`: points per kill scaled by the killed entity's `complexityScore` (tripled for an Elite), plus bonuses for remaining health/ammo (against what the level started you with), completion speed (maxed at/under 90s, decaying to 0 by 180s), and route efficiency (actual distance walked vs. a new BFS-shortest spawn→exit tile distance on `GameMap`), minus a malus scaled to how far below full health you finished. Recomputed live every frame (cheap, pure function) so the HUD's Score readout is a running projection, final the instant the exit is reached [Task 32]

- [x] persisted highscore list — top-10 board in `localStorage` (`src/engine/highscores.ts`), best score first, viewable via a new sidebar "Highscores" button opening a `<dialog>` (Score/Campaign/Level/Hash table) [Task 32]. Follow-up: recording on *every* level clear was too noisy for a multi-level campaign — an entry is now only written once a run actually ends, on death (`onGameOver`, which now also carries final stats, same as `onWin` already did) or on finishing the whole campaign (`advanceToNextLevel`'s "no more files" branch), never on an ordinary mid-campaign level clear. Each entry also now records `levelsCleared` (how many levels were actually cleared before the run ended — `campaignLevelIndex - 1` on death since the level died on wasn't cleared, `campaignLevelIndex` on completion since it was), shown as its own "Levels" column

- [x] user pickable bgm folder — needs a simple settings menu for volume control (Master, SFX, BGM) so custom music doesn't overpower game audio — `audio.ts`'s single master gain split into independent SFX and BGM buses under one master; three sidebar sliders (persisted to localStorage) control all three. `src/engine/bgm.ts` picks a local folder via the File System Access API and plays its `.mp3`/`.ogg`/`.wav` files back as a shuffled, looping playlist through an `<audio>` element routed into the BGM bus (streamed, not fully decoded up front, so a large file doesn't have to load whole) [Task 32]

- [x] scoresystem should also store a hash (sha256 or similar) of the AST, or code scanned. this should also be displayed in the highscore list, for comparison. part of that hash should be based on the campaign/project name. — each recorded run hashes `JSON.stringify(parsed AST) + campaignName` with `crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', ...)`; the truncated hex digest is shown in the Highscores dialog (full hash in the cell's tooltip) [Task 32]

- [x] canvas should scale to viewport in windowed mode — `.scene-canvas` now also caps `max-height: 100vh` (previously only bounded by width) with `object-fit: contain`, so a short/narrow window can no longer derive a height taller than the viewport and clip the bottom of the scene; letterboxed instead of clipped [Task 33]

- [x] compass pointing to the exit — a small dial below the always-on minimap (`drawCompass` in `hud.ts`) with a needle computed from the angle between the player's position and the exit tile, rotated relative to the player's own facing so it reads correctly no matter which way they're looking — same convention as the automap's player marker [Task 33]. Follow-up: disabled again behind `COMPASS_ENABLED = false` in `hud.ts` — the needle read as inverted on at least one axis, and the dial didn't fit visually next to the minimap; code is intact, just gated, pending a fix/redesign. Second follow-up: root cause was the needle's rest (bearing-zero) geometry pointing along local +X ("east"/3 o'clock) instead of -Y ("up"/12 o'clock) — a target dead ahead should read as "up", not sideways, and basing it on +X put the whole left/right sweep 90° off from what a glance expects. Fixed the geometry (verified against a synthetic map in headless Chromium: dead-ahead→up, behind→down, right→right, left→left, screenshot confirmed) and redesigned placement — `renderMinimap` now returns its panel's bounding rect and the needle draws directly in its top-right corner instead of as a separate floating dial. Re-enabled (`COMPASS_ENABLED = true`) [Task 34]

- [x] bonus score points for reading lore panels + exploring >95% of the map — `scoring.ts` gains two new bonuses: a flat 100pts per unique lore terminal read (tracked in a `Set` keyed by tile), and a 750pt "100% Clear" bonus once unique walkable tiles visited crosses 95% of the level's total walkable tiles (counted once at level start; secret rooms stay excluded from that denominator, so finding them is a bonus, not a requirement) [Task 33]

- [x] ammo to scarce on normal difficultiy — static map ammo pickups bumped ~40-50% (bullets 8→11, rockets 2→3, all difficulties), and Normal specifically now rolls enemy loot kind against a slightly ammo-favoring weight table (Easy/Hard already have their own scarcity curve via the existing per-drop amount multiplier, so only Normal's drop-*kind* odds changed) [Task 33]

- [x] gamepad/joystick support — `InputController.pollGamepad()` polls the HTML5 Gamepad API once per frame (no axis/button change events exist to listen for instead): left stick moves/strafes, right stick turns, RT/R2 fires (edge-triggered like a click, plus held for automatic weapons), bumpers cycle weapons (reusing the existing mousewheel-cycle queue), R3 or B triggers quick-melee. All blended additively with existing keyboard/mouse input, so both work at the same time [Task 33]

- [x] dont close end-game dialog on fire, happens by accident all the time in a fight — every blocking overlay (`GameHud.show()`: level-start briefing, commit summary, Kernel Panic, Build Successful) now ignores its own dismiss triggers (Enter/Space/Escape, mousedown, and any gamepad button) for 1.2s after it appears, so mashing the fire button in the fight that triggered it can't instantly close it before it's even readable. Also added a gamepad dismiss path (polled directly via `requestAnimationFrame`, since no engine `InputController` is running while these overlays are up) — previously a gamepad-only player had no way to dismiss them at all [Task 34]

- [x] console hints often come twice in a row, shouldnt happen. each hint should be different to the previous — `consoleSidebar.ts` now tracks `lastHintIndex` and re-rolls (in a `while` loop) whenever the random pick matches it [Task 34]

- [x] cone-of-fire still to extreme — reduced the base max deviation (56px → 38px) and changed the range falloff from quadratic to cubic (`(range/FOG_FAR)³`), so medium-range pistol/gdb shots stay reliable and only the last stretch before `FOG_FAR` spreads noticeably [Task 35]

- [x] compass shouldnt overlay the minimap, add a little cutout in the border to contain it in — `renderMinimap` now reserves a dedicated, separately-bordered notch in the panel's bottom-right gutter, entirely outside the grid-drawing area; the compass needle draws centered in that notch instead of overlapping the grid corner. Verified in headless Chromium against both a large and a small map [Task 35]

- [x] optional replay storage at end of level (even possible?), and a replay player obv. highscores should include a replay as well that can be replayed — deterministic replay system: `Math.random()` replaced with a seeded mulberry32 PRNG (`src/prng.ts`, extracted from the map generator's existing seeded approach) everywhere gameplay-outcome-relevant randomness happens — enemy AI roam/fire-cooldown timing, loot rolls, weapon cone-of-fire spread, elite-loot coinflip — while purely cosmetic randomness (blood particles, SFX pitch, BGM shuffle, console hints) stays on `Math.random()` since it never feeds back into simulation state. Each level launch records a fresh gameplay seed plus every frame's digested input (`InputController.captureSnapshot()`, a non-destructive peek) via `ReplayRecorder`, capped at ~6 minutes of frames; whichever level's recording is live when a run ends gets attached to that run's highscore entry. A new "Watch Replay" button per highscore entry (only shown for entries that have one) re-picks the workspace (same pattern as Continue Run), verifies the re-parsed file's AST hash still matches before trusting it to regenerate the map, then drives a fresh `RaycasterEngine` with the recorded seed and a `ReplayPlaybackInput` standing in for live input, frame-by-frame. Scoped to a single level (the one the run ended on), not a full multi-level campaign replay — an R&D feature, not a complete solution. Verified deterministic (same seed + inputs → byte-identical end stats) against the real engine/enemyAi/loot code in headless Chromium [Task 35]. Follow-up: expanded to cover the *whole* campaign run, not just the level it ended on — `ReplayRecorder` replaced with `CampaignReplayRecorder`, which records one `ReplayLevelSegment` per level actually played (own file path/bonus flag/gameplay seed/AST hash/difficulty/gore/carryover/frames) and stays the same instance across `advanceToNextLevel`'s auto-chain, only starting fresh on a genuinely new run (no carryover, or none in memory e.g. right after a reload). `ReplayPayload` bumped to `version: 2` (a `levels[]` array) — old single-level (`version: 1`) entries are deliberately left unsupported rather than migrated, and defensively hidden from the "Watch Replay" button rather than trusted blindly, since that shape round-tripped through `localStorage`/JSON and can't be trusted from the type alone. "Watch Replay" now walks the whole level sequence, re-locating/re-verifying/regenerating each in turn and carrying recorded state forward exactly like a live run. Verified in headless Chromium: a scripted 2-level recording produces correct per-level segments/carryover/hashes, and replaying it through fresh engines reproduces byte-identical final stats [Task 36]

- [x] replay just silently stopping was surprising, needs an "end of replay" dialog; also expose play/pause/seek/playback-speed controls — added `GameHud.showReplayEnded()`, shown (via a new `endReplay()` helper) for every non-win/death termination path: manual Escape stop, a file that can't be relocated/re-verified/parsed, and the (shouldn't-happen) frames-exhausted safety net; natural win/death still show their own Build Successful/Kernel Panic overlay as before. Fixed a real latent race while doing this: the drive loop could keep ticking (and, at a mid-campaign death, wrongly try to advance to the next level) between a level's engine firing onGameOver/onWin and the player actually dismissing that dialog — a new `levelEnded` flag now stops frame consumption the instant either fires, checked every tick and mid-burst. Also added a DOM transport bar (play/pause, seek back/forward ~5s, speed stepper 0.25x-4x) alongside the hint caption: pausing just stops consuming frames without cancelling the poll loop (so unpausing resumes on its own), speed changes how many recorded frames run per real tick via a fractional accumulator (each frame's own recorded `dt` is never altered, so the simulated trajectory itself never changes — only how fast it's watched), seeking forward bursts through recorded frames synchronously, and seeking backward — since simulation isn't reversible — rebuilds the current level's engine from scratch and bursts forward to the target instead. Verified in headless Chromium: restarting and re-bursting to the same target frame reproduces byte-identical state every time [Task 37]

- [x] ability to load remote git repos instead of local folder + fix the "has been externalized for browser compatibility" build warnings — `src/fs/github.ts` fetches a public repo's default branch and full recursive tree from the GitHub REST API and reconstructs it into the exact same `TreeNode` shape `readDirectoryTree` builds from a local `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`; file content is fetched lazily (only once a file is actually opened/parsed) from raw.githubusercontent.com rather than the REST API, since that host doesn't count against the much stricter unauthenticated REST rate limit. `TreeNode.handle` widened to a union with a new minimal `RemoteFileHandle` interface (just the `getFile()` shape `readFileText` actually calls) so every existing consumer — file tree rendering, `flattenParsableFiles`, entrypoint detection, level launching, replay — works completely unchanged against a GitHub-sourced tree, no special-casing anywhere. New sidebar "owner/repo" input + "Load from GitHub" button parallel `Select Workspace`; campaign autosave is skipped while a GitHub-sourced run is active (a `workspaceIsRemote` flag) since "Continue Run" re-picks a *local* folder and has no way to re-fetch a remote repo. Separately, `web-tree-sitter`'s single browser bundle statically references Node's `fs/promises`/`module` for a Node-only code path that's dead in a real browser (gated behind a runtime `ENVIRONMENT_IS_NODE` check) but still had to resolve at build time — `vite.config.ts` now aliases both to a new empty stub module (`src/empty-node-shim.ts`), clearing the warning without pulling in a real Node polyfill for code that never runs. Verified against a real small public repo (`sindresorhus/pify`) in headless Chromium over a real network connection: correct tree/nesting reconstruction, real raw-content fetch, and a real tree-sitter parse of the fetched file all succeeded, plus the actual sidebar button driving the real app end-to-end (file tree populates, entrypoint auto-launches, canvas unhides) [Task 38]. Follow-up: the run's score/replay *were* actually being recorded correctly all along (verified directly — a real GitHub-sourced playthrough left a full highscore entry with an attached replay payload in `localStorage`), but "Watch Replay" unconditionally called `pickWorkspace()` to re-fetch the level files, which only makes sense for a locally-picked run — for a GitHub-sourced one it would prompt a local folder picker that could never match the recorded `owner/repo`-prefixed paths. `HighscoreEntry` gained an optional `source?: "github"` field (set when recording a remote run); `startReplay` now checks it and, when set, re-parses `campaignName` back into an `owner/repo` ref and calls `fetchGithubTree` instead of prompting a local pick. Verified in headless Chromium: a full GitHub-sourced run recorded a `source: "github"` entry, and clicking its "Watch Replay" button re-fetched the same repo (no local-picker prompt) and played the recorded run back correctly. Second follow-up: a genuine multi-level run (recorded highscore/replay for real, `advanceToNextLevel` verified directly through the actual code) still wasn't showing up in the Highscores dialog — the real bug wasn't GitHub-specific at all: a replay payload spanning several levels' worth of recorded frames can run into multiple megabytes, easily blowing a browser's `localStorage` quota; `recordHighscore`'s `setItem` threw, was caught, warned, and silently dropped the *entire* new entry rather than just its replay. `recordHighscore` now retries on a quota failure — first without just the new entry's replay, then (if that alone still doesn't fit) without any entry's replay — so a run's score always survives even when its replay doesn't. Verified directly against the real `recordHighscore`/`loadHighscores` in headless Chromium with a synthetic ~30MB multi-level replay: the score persists to `localStorage` even though the oversized replay gets dropped, and a normal-sized entry recorded alongside it keeps its replay intact

- [x] compass cutout too large and invasive, i want like a circle attached to the corner - not a full bar just for that tiny compass — `renderMinimap`'s panel no longer reserves a bottom strip at all (`COMPASS_NOTCH_SIZE`/`COMPASS_NOTCH_GUTTER` removed, panel height reverts to exactly the grid + 4px); `MinimapPanelRect.notch` replaced with `compassBadge: {cx, cy, r}` — a small (13px radius) circle centered exactly on the panel's bottom-right corner point, so it straddles/overlaps the corner like a notification badge rather than sitting in a dead reserved strip. Its background/border draw moved to the very end of `renderMinimap` (after every grid/wall/enemy marker) so it paints on top of the corner instead of underneath it. `drawCompass`'s needle shrank (9px → 7px half-length) to fit the smaller circle; the bearing/rotation math itself (dead-ahead reads as "up", positive = clockwise) is completely unchanged. Verified in headless Chromium: new panel-height/badge-geometry assertions, plus a bearing-math regression check confirming the geometry refactor didn't perturb the existing (already correct) rotation [Task 39]

- [x] support doom-cheats for the lulz, disable highscore saving when any cheat is active though — classic `IDDQD`/`IDKFA`/`IDCLIP` typed anywhere in-game (a rolling letter buffer in `InputController.onKeyDown`, independent of every existing keybinding — Q/E/F/R etc. still work normally while also feeding the buffer). `IDDQD` (god mode) and `IDCLIP` (no-clip, `Player.noClip` bypasses `collides()`) toggle on/off exactly like real Doom; `IDKFA` is a one-time grant — every weapon unlocked, bullets/rockets set to 999, armor to 100 (no health/keys touched, scope kept to exactly "unlocks all weapons, maxes out ammo and armor"). Cheat state carries across a level transition via new `EngineCarryover.godMode`/`noClip` fields (a fresh engine/player is constructed per level). A small top-center canvas toast confirms activation, fading over ~2s (frame-counted like the existing `flashFrames`/`muzzleFrames` timers, no new dt-based timer needed). `main.ts` tracks a campaign-scoped `cheatsUsed` flag (same reset-only-on-fresh-workspace pattern as `workspaceIsRemote`) that gates the very top of `recordRunHighscore` — a cheated run's score and replay are never written to the leaderboard at all, for the rest of that campaign. Left deliberately undocumented in the README (a genuine Easter egg, not a documented control) per explicit request; full details captured here instead. Verified live in headless Chromium: typed all three codes into a real running level via real dispatched keydown events and confirmed `godMode`/`noClip`/`ownedWeapons`/ammo/armor all changed exactly as expected, re-typing `IDDQD` toggled it back off, and — calling the real `recordRunHighscore` directly — a cheated run's call is a no-op (no `localStorage` write) while an otherwise-identical clean run's call does persist an entry [Task 39]

- [x] TODOs in the code do "something" with the level, to be determined — a `TODO`/`FIXME` marker in a comment (`isTodoFlagged`, exported from `astUtils.ts`) now bypasses the lore-terminal length/multi-line gate entirely, so even a short one-line `// TODO: fix this` gets a terminal (the short one-liner is the most common real form of technical debt, so excluding it would miss most of what this feature is meant to surface). Each flagged terminal also gets a small "technical debt" encounter on the floor tile just inside its room: a per-instance seeded-`rng` roll (equal thirds) picks a timed spike trap, a proximity mine (both reusing `placeTraps`' existing shapes/mechanics and constants), or a weak "Bug" enemy (a synthetic `CodeEntity`, flat 10 HP — well under the normal 25 HP floor — purple-tinted via the existing `EntityKind` fallback color, no `sprites.ts` changes needed). Deliberately a *trap* or *mine*, not a `fillHazards`-style permanent acid pool — the candidate tile is exactly where the player has to stand to read the terminal, and a permanently-damaging tile there would make it painful to ever reach rather than just riskier; a trap/mine keeps a genuine safe approach. Never more than one of the three for the same terminal (occasionally none at all, if no free adjacent floor tile exists — a best-effort placement, same philosophy as every other "extra thing near a room" system in this generator), and normal (non-flagged) long comments are completely unaffected. Verified in headless Chromium against the real parser + `MapGenerator`: a synthetic short `// TODO` comment survives the length gate, produces a terminal, and gets at most one adjacent trap/mine/enemy; regenerating from the same parsed AST is deterministic; a normal long comment still gets a terminal with no extra encounter. Follow-up: added the proximity mine as a third possible outcome (was trap-or-enemy only) — sampled 40 synthetic TODO comments across varying seeds and confirmed all three outcomes actually occur, never more than one per terminal [Task 39]

- [x] dont play gameaudio while running tests (either unit/functional, or improvised by claude) — the existing "no `AudioContext` means every play call is a safe no-op" guard (`audio.ts`) never helped here, since a real or headless Chromium *does* have a working `AudioContext`; sound would actually play there. `AudioManager` now also treats `navigator.webdriver === true` as "unavailable" (`isSilenced()`) — the standard flag every automation tool (Playwright/Puppeteer/Selenium) sets on the browser it controls, so neither a future test suite nor an ad hoc Claude-driven check needs to remember a special flag; a real, manually-used browser is completely unaffected. `bgm.ts`'s raw `<audio>` element playback doesn't route through that guard at all (a `<audio>` element plays on its own even without a Web Audio graph wired up), so it now checks `audio.isSilenced()` explicitly too. Verified in a plain Node harness with stubbed `AudioContext`/`Audio`/`navigator` globals: with `navigator.webdriver` unset, `playShoot()` and BGM playback proceed normally; with it `true`, `isSilenced()` reports true and neither a real `AudioContext` nor `HTMLAudioElement.play()` is ever invoked [Task 40]

- [x] files with shebang and no extension arent recognized as source — `registry.ts`'s `getParserForFilename`/`isParsable` now take an optional `sourceText`; when a filename has no extension, they sniff its leading `#!` line (`shebangExtension`) and map the interpreter basename (`bash`/`sh`/`zsh`/`python`/`python3`/`ruby`/`php`/`node`, trailing version numbers like `python3.11` or `bash5` stripped) onto whichever registered extension already has an adapter for it — this never adds a language, it just lets an existing adapter match a file with no extension of its own. `parseFile` already had the text in hand, so it just forwards it through. Fixed at both places a filename used to get judged on its name alone: `handleFileSelected` (clicking a file in the sidebar) now passes the just-read text, and `flattenParsableFiles` — the campaign-wide list behind "next level", entrypoint detection, and resume-save matching — is now `async` and reads content (only for the extensionless case) via a new `isParsableNode` helper before deciding; every caller in `main.ts` threads the `await` through. An interpreter this doesn't recognize (e.g. Perl, no adapter) still correctly reports not-parsable, and a real extension always wins over any shebang. Verified via the project's Vite-dev-server headless-Chromium recipe (needed because the parser layer imports tree-sitter grammar wasm through `?url`): 11 assertions against the real `registry.ts` covering a bare `#!/bin/bash`, `#!/usr/bin/env python3`, `#!/usr/bin/env ruby`, versioned interpreters, an unrecognized interpreter, a real extension overriding a misleading shebang, and `parseFile` itself succeeding end-to-end for an extensionless Python script — all passed [Task 41]

- [x] lore should ignore license headers — `astUtils.ts`'s `extractLargeComments` now runs a new `isLicenseHeader(text, startLine)` check first, ahead of (and overriding) the existing TODO-bypass/length/multi-line gate, so a comment can never become a lore terminal if it (a) sits within the first 10 lines of the file and (b) matches one of a broad set of license/copyright phrase patterns — this project's own SPDX+Copyright convention, plus common full-header boilerplate (MIT's "Permission is hereby granted", BSD's "Redistribution and use...", the GPL family, Apache). Without this, this repo's own 2-line header (the copyright line alone clears the 60-char lore threshold) would generate a lore terminal in nearly every file if the game were pointed at its own source; the same applies to any third-party repo carrying a standard license header. Deliberately position-gated to the first 10 lines only, so a genuinely long lore-worthy comment deep in a file that happens to mention "copyright" in prose is unaffected. Verified via the project's Vite-dev-server headless-Chromium recipe against the real `parseFile`: this repo's own SPDX+Copyright header, a synthetic MIT-style header, and a TODO-flagged copyright header all correctly produced zero lore comments; a license-y phrase placed past line 10 and an ordinary long comment both still correctly produced a lore comment as before [Task 42]

- [x] dont record highscore when player died in the very first level — `recordRunHighscore` (`main.ts`) gets a second early-return guard, right below the existing `cheatsUsed` one and in the same style: `levelsCleared === 0` (i.e. died while still on campaign level 1, before clearing any level) now skips `recordHighscore` entirely, logging `%c[highscores] Died on the very first level...` instead. `levelsCleared` can only be `0` from the death call site (`onGameOver`, which passes `campaignLevelIndex - 1`) — the campaign-completion call site always passes a `levelsCleared >= 1` since reaching it means the current level was won, not died on — so the guard can't accidentally swallow a legitimate single-level campaign win. `HighscoreEntry.levelsCleared`'s doc comment updated to note it can no longer actually be `0` on a stored entry. Verified in headless Chromium via the project's real DOM scaffold (`index.html`'s markup, since `main.ts` throws if `#select-workspace` etc. are missing) with a temporary window hook exposing the real (unexported) `recordRunHighscore`, removed after verification: a synthetic `levelsCleared: 0` call produced no `localStorage` write (and logged the new skip message), while `levelsCleared: 1` and `2` calls both correctly persisted entries [Task 43]

- [x] highscore table should also store some stats about the codebase, like complexity and lines of code total — `HighscoreEntry` gains optional `codebaseLinesOfCode`/`codebaseComplexity` fields, covering the *whole* picked codebase (every parsable file in the tree), not just the levels a run actually reached. A background, chunked aggregation (`computeCodebaseStats`/`kickOffCodebaseStats` in `main.ts`, yielding to the event loop every 20 files) kicks off the moment a workspace loads — local pick, GitHub load, or Continue Run — reusing the exact same `flattenParsableFiles`/`readFileText`/`parseFile` pipeline every level launch already goes through, and is cached per-workspace so dying and retrying doesn't re-parse the whole tree every time. `recordRunHighscore` awaits it with an 8s bounded timeout so a pathologically large repo can't delay recording a run — it just omits the two fields in that rare case rather than blocking. The Highscores dialog gained "Lines"/"Complexity" columns, falling back to the same muted "—" already used for a missing replay on any entry recorded before this field existed. Verified via this project's Vite-dev-server headless-Chromium recipe (needed since parsing goes through tree-sitter wasm): a real aggregation run against a real public repo (`sindresorhus/pify`) over the network produced correct non-zero totals (784 lines / 113 complexity across its 5 real files), and a second harness confirmed the table renders populated numbers for a new-style entry and "—" for an old one [Task 44]

- [x] avoid or workaround localStorage quota (even by compression if needed) — the actual quota risk was always the highscore board's optional replay payload (can run into the megabytes for a long multi-level run), which previously only got *reacted* to (dropping replay data and retrying) after a `setItem` already threw. New `src/engine/storageCompression.ts` gzip-compresses (`CompressionStream`/`DecompressionStream`, base64-encoded for storage) whatever gets written under the `codeenstein-highscores` key *before* that cascade ever runs, falling back to plain JSON if compression isn't smaller (tiny boards) or isn't supported at all — no new npm dependency, per this project's "keep dependencies minimal" rule, same reasoning as using `crypto.subtle.digest` for the AST hash instead of a bundled hash library. A `"gz1:"` prefix distinguishes compressed values from every already-stored plain-JSON board, so existing players' data keeps loading unchanged. `loadHighscores`/`recordHighscore` are now `async` to accommodate this; the pre-existing drop-replay cascade is otherwise untouched and still serves as the last-resort fallback for boards that don't fit even compressed. Campaign save/gore/difficulty/volume keys were left alone — small fixed-shape values with no realistic quota risk, where gzip+base64 overhead would likely make them bigger, not smaller. Verified in headless Chromium against the real module: small objects round-trip and correctly skip compression (no size win), a large synthetic replay-bearing entry round-trips byte-for-byte and compresses meaningfully smaller, a legacy uncompressed board already in `localStorage` still loads correctly, and — with `localStorage.setItem` temporarily wrapped to simulate a small quota — compression alone now saves a board that previously would've needed its replay dropped, while the drop-replay cascade still correctly kicks in once even compressed data doesn't fit **Follow-up 2026-08-01:** the gzip layer this landed was measured and found to be leaving most of the win on the table — `dt`'s float jitter defeated the compressor's dictionary — and the board is now binary-packed for a further 3.5x. See the replay-codec entry at the top of this file.

- [x] readme barely readable now. add more structure, formatting and maybe rephrase stuff if needed

- [x] smarter entrypoint detection, its quite stupid currently. in example when loading a shared library project (mcdope/pam_usb) — verified the concrete complaint against the real `mcdope/pam_usb` repo (fetched over the network): it's a PAM shared library with no `main.c` and no `main()` anywhere in its actual module logic (`pam.c` implements PAM's own `pam_sm_*` callback convention instead), so the old two-stage cascade fell through to the first C-family file with a literal `main()` in tree order — `evdev-helper.c`, a ~65-line setgid helper for one narrow hardware check, picked purely because `-` sorts before `.` alphabetically. Also found, while designing the fix, that every one of that repo's 11 hand-rolled unit-test files (`tests/unit/c/*.c`) has its own `main()` too and is *more* complex than the real source — ruling out "just parse everything and pick whichever main()-having file is most complex" as a fix on its own, since that would pick a test file instead. Fixed with a layered redesign in `main.ts`: every parsable file is first split into a "real source" and "test/spec fixture" bucket (any path segment matching `test`/`tests`/`spec`/`specs`/`__tests__`, case-insensitive — test files are still perfectly normal playable levels later, just never the auto-launched first one); the old C-family-only `main()`-function scan is replaced by a language-agnostic scan-and-score pass (any language's `main`/`Main` function/method entity now counts, catching C#'s capitalized convention too, which the old check never reached) that sums each file's total complexity and keeps the richest main()-having file, falling back to the richest file overall if none had one; each stage (filename convention, then scan-and-score) tries the real-source bucket first and only falls through to the test bucket if that stage found nothing usable, not merely if the bucket was empty; the whole detection pass is capped by a 4s timeout (mirroring the existing highscore-stats timeout pattern) so a pathologically large repo can't hang the very first level from launching, degrading to the old "first file in tree order" fallback if it fires. Verified end-to-end in headless Chromium: loading the real `mcdope/pam_usb` repo over the network now auto-launches `src/pamusb-check.c` (a legitimate CLI tool shipped with the project) instead of `evdev-helper.c` or any test file; a synthetic workspace with a conventional `main.py` still resolves instantly via the unchanged fast path; a synthetic complex test file with `main()` correctly loses to a plain real source file with none; an all-test-files workspace still resolves via the fallback bucket instead of finding nothing; a capitalized C# `Main` is now detected; and a name-matched file that fails to parse correctly falls through to the scoring scan instead of dead-ending [Task 45]

- [x] rocket ammo should only drop when unlocked, else drop bullets — gated every rocket-ammo source (not just enemy-kill drops) behind actually owning ghidra (`ownedWeapons.has(GHIDRA_WEAPON_INDEX)`): `loot.ts`'s `rollLoot()` now excludes the `"rockets"` weight-table entry entirely when the launcher isn't owned, redistributing its share across bullets/health/armor instead of wastefully rolling into dead loot; `mapGenerator.ts`'s static-pickup and secret-room placement (`placeAmmoPickups`/`placeSecretRooms`) take the same `hasRocketLauncher` flag and roll bullets/health in its place. Threaded through both real call sites in `main.ts` — live level launch and replay playback — by reading `ownedWeapons` off the level's `carryover` (recorded per-segment for replays already, so a replay regenerates the exact same map as the original run rather than diverging once the launcher's unlock state differs from the `true` default) [Task 46]

- [x] score penalties hit to hard, feels like the score is decreasing all the time while playing. seen it happen that i had less score after an level than before it. — two real bugs found in `scoring.ts`/`engine.ts`, both fixed. (1) Health loss was scored twice: `healthBonus` already scaled 0→500 with remaining health fraction, but a separate `hpMalus` also scaled 0→200 *against the same fraction* and got subtracted on top — full-to-zero health swung the total by 700 points off one input instead of 500. Removed `hpMalus` entirely; `healthBonus` alone now covers it. (2) `RaycasterEngine` is a fresh instance every level, and `EngineCarryover` only ever forwarded health/armor/bullets/rockets/weapon — never kill points, lore bonus, or the flat 750pt map-completion bonus — so the live score collapsed back to near-zero at every level transition instead of continuing to grow; that's the direct cause of "less score after a level than before it." `EngineCarryover` gained a `priorScore` field (score banked from every level already cleared), threaded through both real carryover-construction sites (`advanceToNextLevel`'s auto-chain and the "Continue Run" resume handler) and added into `buildStats()`'s live score, so the HUD number is now a genuine running campaign total that only resets on a fresh run. Verified against the real `computeScore`/`RaycasterEngine` in headless Chromium: full-to-zero health now costs exactly 500 points (was 700); a synthetic 2-level run showed the second level's score at its very first frame already sitting at the first level's carried-over total plus its own fresh bonuses, never dropping [Task 47]

- [x] rename "Armor" to "Swap" — renamed throughout: `LootKind`/`LootDrop`, `EngineStats`/`EngineCarryover` fields, the private engine field and its 1:1 damage-absorption logic, loot weight tables, drop-amount/max constants (`ARMOR_DROP_AMOUNT`→`SWAP_DROP_AMOUNT`, `MAX_ARMOR`→`MAX_SWAP`), the HUD label, and the sprite loot-color case; README/comments updated to match. The campaign save's `armor` field also got renamed to `swap` — `loadCampaignSave` now accepts either key (falls back to the legacy `armor` field when `swap` isn't present), so a save written before the rename still loads instead of getting silently treated as invalid. Verified against the real built app in headless Chromium: the HUD renders "SWAP" (not "ARMOR") with the correct value, and a legacy `armor`-keyed save round-trips through the real `loadCampaignSave()` all the way into the actual resume flow, not just a parse check [Task 48]

- [x] where is the "continue run" button? never seen it — found a real, pre-existing bug while verifying the rename above: the button's on-load visibility check (`if (loadCampaignSave()) continueButton.style.display = "";`) runs at module top-level *before* `const SAVE_KEY` was declared further down the file — the same shape of bug as the `GORE_KEY` one from Task 30. `loadCampaignSave` is a hoisted function so the call itself doesn't error, but the instant it reads `SAVE_KEY` it hits the const's temporal dead zone, throws, and the function's own empty `catch` silently swallows it, always returning `null` — so the button never auto-showed on load, for any save, regardless of validity (the *other* call site, inside the button's own click handler, runs after full module init and always worked fine, which is presumably why this went unnoticed). Fixed the same way `GORE_KEY` was: moved `SAVE_KEY`'s declaration up next to `GORE_KEY`/`DIFFICULTY_KEY`/the volume keys, with the same explanatory comment. Verified against the real built app in headless Chromium: reproduced the bug against the pristine pre-fix code first (button stayed hidden even with a valid save seeded into `localStorage`), then confirmed the fix flips it to shown on load; a page with no save correctly still stays hidden [Task 48]. Follow-up: the now-visible button had no dedicated CSS at all and fell back to a bare default `<button>` — small, gray, left-aligned next to the full-width red Select Workspace button. First pass gave it matching full-width/padding/radius but a muted secondary color so it wouldn't compete with the primary CTA. Second follow-up, per explicit request: Select Workspace/Continue Run/Load from GitHub are three different ways to start the same game loop, so they're now grouped into a tab strip (Local/Continue/GitHub) with only the active one's panel shown — the Continue tab itself is hidden entirely (not just disabled) when no save exists, same visibility condition as before, and switches back to Local automatically if the active save gets cleared out from under it (death, campaign completion, a fresh workspace/GitHub pick). Since Continue Run is no longer ever shown stacked next to Select Workspace, it went back to sharing the same bold primary-red styling — each is the sole action of its own tab now, so there's no competing-CTA problem left to soften. Verified in headless Chromium: default state (Local active, Continue tab absent with no save), all three tabs switch panels correctly and only one is visible at a time, Continue tab appears when a save exists and disappears again when cleared [Task 49]. Third follow-up: Load from GitHub was still on the old muted `.settings-btn` style (shared with genuinely secondary actions like Select BGM Folder/Highscores), so it stood out from the other two now-bold tab actions — switched it to the same shared full-width bold-red button rule as Select Workspace/Continue Run, since it's equally the sole primary action of its own tab [Task 49]

- [x] dont drop health packs if the player is at 100%, drop ammo or armor instead — `rollLoot()` (`loot.ts`) gained a `playerAtFullHealth` flag that filters `"health"` out of the weight table entirely (same pattern as the existing `hasRocketLauncher` filter for `"rockets"`), redistributing its share across bullets/rockets/swap instead of re-rolling into it; the regular-kill call site in `engine.ts` now passes `this.health >= MAX_HEALTH`. Elite kills bypass that table with their own guaranteed-health branch, so `dropEliteLoot` gained a matching full-health check that falls back to a new elite-sized bullets/swap drop (`ELITE_BULLETS_DROP_AMOUNT`/`ELITE_SWAP_DROP_AMOUNT`) instead of the guaranteed `ELITE_HEALTH_DROP_AMOUNT` pack. Verified against the real `RaycasterEngine` in headless Chromium: 400 trials of a regular kill at full health never rolled `"health"` (still rolled ammo/swap), 400 trials below full health still could, 400 trials of an elite kill at full health (weapons pre-owned to force past the weapon-drop branch) never rolled `"health"` and consistently fell back to bullets/swap, and an elite kill below full health still guaranteed its health pack unchanged [Task 50]

- [x] user/player docs in `doc/user` — added a six-page player manual (`doc/user/README.md` index plus getting-started/controls/hud-and-ui/mechanics/tips), separate from the project `README.md` (pitch/dev setup) and the still-open `doc/dev` (design docs). Every control label, HUD element, weapon/loot/difficulty number, and mechanic was re-verified directly against current source (`input.ts`, `gameHud.ts`, `hud.ts`, `weapons.ts`, `loot.ts`, `difficulty.ts`, `effects.ts`, `scoring.ts`, `mapGenerator.ts`, `enemyAi.ts`, `engine.ts`, `main.ts`, `index.html`) rather than copied from the README, so it reflects the actual shipped behavior (e.g. Gore's "Extreme" tier exists in code but is hidden from the dropdown). Documented in full detail, no spoiler-hiding, per explicit request — secret-wall tinting, mine disarm mechanics, and TODO/FIXME encounters are all spelled out plainly. `README.md` itself was intentionally left untouched.

- [x] privacy doc, what exactly happens with the workspace selected and what is stored on your machine — added `doc/user/privacy.md`, verified directly against source rather than assumed: grepped the whole codebase for every `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`WebSocket`/`localStorage`/cookie/analytics call. Only remote calls in the entire app are the opt-in GitHub loader (`src/fs/github.ts`): unauthenticated `GET`s to `api.github.com` (repo/tree metadata) and `raw.githubusercontent.com` (lazy per-file content fetch), triggered only by the "Load from GitHub" tab — a locally-picked workspace never touches the network, and neither does a custom BGM folder (played from a local object URL). Documented every `localStorage` key (`codeenstein-campaign-save`, `codeenstein-highscores`, `codeenstein-gore-level`, `codeenstein-difficulty`, the three volume keys) and exactly what each holds — confirmed the campaign save and replay system never store file *contents*, only workspace-relative paths, an AST SHA-256 hash, and recorded player *input*, not source code. No analytics/telemetry/crash-reporting library exists in `package.json` (only dependency is `web-tree-sitter`) or anywhere in `src`. Linked from the `doc/user` index.

- [x] dev docs, GDD, design descisions etc in `doc/dev` — added four files: `doc/dev/README.md` (index), `architecture.md` (the fs→parser→map→engine layering and its hard rules — ParsedFile as the only cross-layer contract, the "Enemy stays data" rule, why difficulty.ts/prng.ts live at src/ root, the determinism/replay constraint — every claim re-verified against current source doc-comments, not assumed), `game-design.md` (developer-facing "why" behind the code→dungeon mapping, enemy/difficulty/economy/scoring intent, distinct from doc/user/mechanics.md's player-facing "how"), and `decisions.md` (a themed, evergreen reference of notable tradeoffs/reversals — determinism, entrypoint detection, pack-vs-elite scaling, storage-quota handling, loot gating, feature reversals, dependency minimalism, doc transparency — citing this file's task numbers for full detail rather than restating them, explicitly not a copy of this log). Root `README.md` gained one line in its Documentation section linking to the new index; `doc/user` and this file were left untouched. Deliberately kept to four files rather than an ADR-per-decision structure, to stay maintainable as this backlog keeps growing.

- [x] fixme triggered mines are sometimes placed so close to the spawn point that it results in insta-damage — root cause: every hazard-placement system in `mapGenerator.ts` (`placeTraps`, `placePillars`, `placeAmmoPickups`) already keeps a minimum distance from spawn, but the TODO/FIXME "technical debt" encounter (`placeTodoEncounter`, spawns a spike trap, proximity mine, or weak "Bug" enemy next to a flagged comment's lore terminal) never checked distance to spawn at all — a comment physically located in the spawn room (room 0) could land its encounter immediately adjacent to the player, and a mine's 0.9s fuse or an already-mid-cycle spike trap could then deal damage before the player could realistically react. Fixed generally, not just for mines, per explicit ask: `spawn` is now threaded through `placeLoreTerminals` → `placeTodoEncounter`, which filters its candidate tiles to those at least `TRAP_SPACING` (3 tiles) from spawn — the exact same constant/`dist()` check `placeTraps`' own `farEnough` already uses — before rolling spike/mine/enemy; if every candidate is too close, the encounter is skipped entirely (matching the function's existing "never a hard failure" contract). Verified directly: a synthetic-`ParsedFile` harness piling TODO/FIXME comments into the spawn room across 300 distinct seeds reproduced the bug constantly pre-fix (dozens of mines/spikes/enemies landing 0-2 tiles from spawn) and confirmed zero violations post-fix across the same 300 seeds (2541 total hazard/encounter placements checked) [Task 51]

- [x] if player collects an unlockable weapon he already has, it should add its ammotype instead of being "wasted" — a weapon drop's `weaponIndex` is only guaranteed unowned at the moment an Elite dies (`dropEliteLoot` only rolls from `UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS.filter` on what's still missing), not at the later moment the player actually walks over the drop — a second Elite's weapon drop can sit on the ground until the player has already unlocked that same weapon from a different one in the meantime. `applyLoot`'s `"weapon"` case (`engine.ts`) now checks `ownedWeapons.has(drop.weaponIndex)` first: already-owned resolves to an elite-sized ammo top-up of that weapon's `ammoType` instead (bullets for gdb, rockets for ghidra, via the same `scaledLootAmount` difficulty scaling every other pickup uses) rather than a no-op re-add-to-set-and-switch-weapon; not-yet-owned keeps the original unlock-and-equip behavior unchanged. Added `ELITE_ROCKETS_DROP_AMOUNT` (`loot.ts`) alongside the existing `ELITE_BULLETS_DROP_AMOUNT`/`ELITE_SWAP_DROP_AMOUNT`, since no elite-sized rockets fallback existed yet. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` [Task 52]

- [x] show proper introscreen when no workspace is loaded instead of the current minimal text, what this is, how it works, etc — replaced the single-line placeholder `<p>` in `#viewport` (`index.html`) with a static `#intro-screen`: a title/lede pulled from the README's pitch, a "How it works" code→dungeon mapping list (folder→level, file→room, function→enemy scaled by complexity, global var→acid pool, private/protected method→locked door, goto/label→teleporter, large comment→lore terminal, dead code→secret room), a "What you'll do" combat/campaign summary, and a "Privacy" card (per explicit follow-up request) stating plainly that a local workspace is parsed entirely client-side via WebAssembly and never leaves the machine, that the only network calls are the opt-in GitHub tab's, and linking out to the existing `doc/user/privacy.md` for full detail — wording double-checked against that doc rather than asserted from scratch, so it doesn't overclaim (e.g. doesn't say "never talks to the network" outright, since GitHub loading genuinely does).
