Pure static markup, no new JS — `launchLevel`'s existing "clear every `#viewport` child but the canvas" logic already removes it the moment a level launches, same as the old placeholder did; `resetToFileTree`'s separate "pick a file" placeholder (shown once a workspace *is* loaded but no file selected yet) was left untouched since the ask was specifically the no-workspace state. New `.intro-*` rules in `style.css` reuse the existing dark/monospace theme (`--bg-panel`/`--border`/`--accent`/`--muted`) with a responsive `auto-fit` two-card grid that collapses to one column on narrow windows, and `overflow-y: auto` so it scrolls within `#viewport` instead of clipping against the pre-existing `overflow: hidden`. Verified visually via Playwright (already a project devDependency) against the real dev server at both a wide (1400px) and narrow (900px) viewport — renders correctly, no console errors, single-column collapse confirmed at the narrow width [Task 53].

- [x] control info in the windowed ui below the canvas looks like shit — the whole thing used to be one long run-on `<p class="map-caption">` sentence mixing the level objective, gameplay tips, and every keybinding into undifferentiated 12px muted text. Split it apart: the caption now only carries the level path/objective and gameplay tips (shorter, no longer interleaved with keybindings), and a new `src/ui/controlsLegend.ts` renders the actual key/mouse/gamepad bindings as a row of keycap-styled chips (bordered, `--bg-panel`-backed `<span>`s that visually read as physical keys) grouped by action, plus — per explicit follow-up request for "even a graphic, showing the keyboard and key mapping" — a small literal WASD/QE cluster graphic (two offset rows of keycaps mimicking the physical keyboard stagger) with its own Move/Strafe/Turn legend, instead of just more prose. New `.controls-legend`/`.key`/`.key-cluster`/`.controls-chip` rules in `style.css` reuse the existing dark/monospace theme tokens (`--bg-panel`/`--border`/`--text`/`--muted`), no new palette colors introduced. Wired into `launchLevel` in `main.ts` only (the replay-viewing hint is a different, purely status-text caption and was left untouched). No keybindings changed, only how they're displayed — `doc/user/controls.md` was already accurate and needed no edit. Verified visually via Playwright against the real dev server: loaded the real `mcdope/codeenstein3d` GitHub repo end-to-end, confirmed the legend renders correctly both on the briefing screen and during actual live gameplay alongside the native canvas HUD, no console errors [Task 54].

- [x] code parsing / world generation should show a loading screen or indicator, for large repos it can take a while (to test: use any linux kernel git like `torvalds/linux`) — a workspace load previously gave no feedback in the main viewport at all: a fetched-but-unparsed tree, the up-to-4s entrypoint content-scan (`findEntrypoint`/`ENTRYPOINT_DETECTION_TIMEOUT_MS`), and the synchronous `MapGenerator.generate` call all ran with nothing but a one-line sidebar status (`#workspace-name`) changing, so a slow remote fetch just looked frozen. Added a `#loading-screen` overlay (spinner + status line, static markup in `index.html` alongside `#intro-screen`) shown via new `showLoadingScreen()`/`setLoadingStatus()` helpers in `main.ts`, wired into all three workspace-load entry points (Select Workspace, Load from GitHub, Continue Run) and `autoLaunchInitialLevel`, with the status text tracking each real phase: "Reading/Fetching…" → "Scanning for entrypoint…" → "Parsing `<file>`…" → "Generating world…". That last transition is followed by one `yieldToMainThread()` tick so the browser actually paints "Generating world…" before the synchronous, CPU-bound map generation call blocks the thread — chunking generation itself into an interruptible/async pass was considered but rejected as out of scope and risky given the project's determinism/replay constraints (see `doc/dev/architecture.md`). Also fixed a latent, unrelated-but-adjacent gap found while wiring the "nothing launched" path: `autoLaunchInitialLevel` finishing with no target (empty workspace, or every candidate failed to parse) previously left the viewport stuck on the stale intro screen instead of falling back to the "select a file" placeholder — factored that placeholder out of `resetToFileTree` into a shared `showFileTreePlaceholder()` so both paths use it. `doc/user/getting-started.md` gained one sentence on the "Picking a workspace" step describing the indicator. Verified visually via Playwright against the real dev server: captured the overlay mid-flight (network-throttled via a CDP session to make the phases observable) showing correct status-text transitions through fetch → scan phases, and confirmed the un-throttled path fully resolves — overlay disappears, canvas un-hides, briefing screen with the Task 54 controls legend renders — end-to-end against the real `mcdope/codeenstein3d` GitHub repo [Task 55].

- [x] reorder weapon hotkeys - 3 is unused because it was the knife once. there should be no "holes" between the hotkeys — root cause: `WEAPONS` (`weapons.ts`) is ordered `[pistol, shotgun, knife, gdb, ghidra]` and number keys used to map straight onto that raw array index, but the knife is structurally excluded from number-key switching (`canWieldViaNumberKey` — it's bound to Left-Ctrl instead), so key `3` (→ index 2, the knife) was permanently dead while gdb/ghidra sat at `4`/`5` instead of `3`/`4`. Fixed without touching `WEAPONS`' own array order (which would've silently corrupted every persisted `weaponIndex`/`ownedWeapons` numeric value in campaign saves, replays, and highscore entries — a real hazard considered and rejected): added `NUMBER_KEY_WEAPONS`, a derived `WEAPONS`-index list filtered to just the non-melee entries in array order (`[0,1,3,4]`), and `RaycasterEngine`'s number-key handling now looks up `NUMBER_KEY_WEAPONS[slot]` instead of treating the pressed digit's slot as a raw array index — so `1`/`2`/`3`/`4` land on pistol/shotgun/gdb/ghidra contiguously, and `5`+ stay harmless no-ops exactly like they always were past the last real slot. Mousewheel cycling (`cycleWeapon`) was already correct (iterates raw `WEAPONS` indices directly, independent of the number-key mapping) and needed no change. Updated `doc/user/mechanics.md`'s weapon-slot table (gdb 4→3, ghidra 5→4) to match. Verified against the real dev server via Playwright: loaded `mcdope/codeenstein3d`, typed the `IDKFA` cheat to own every weapon, then confirmed via captured console fire-logs (turning away from spawn first so a shot reliably misses rather than landing an ambiguous enemy hit) that pressing `3` now fires as `[gdb]` and pressing `4` now fires as `[ghidra] launched` — both previously unreachable at those keys [Task 56].

- [x] gdb should have its own bullets, and their drops should have a suited amount of ammo. 10 bullets in an mp5 doesnt makes sense. should also cause more dmg to the enemy — gdb used to share the `"bullets"` ammo pool with the pistol/shotgun; split into its own `"smg"` pool (new `AmmoType`/`LootKind` member) with its own HUD readout ("SMG AMMO", gdb's blue), starting reserve (`STARTING_SMG_AMMO = 40`, a flat carry-forward reserve like rockets since gdb itself has to be earned), regular drop size (`SMG_DROP_AMOUNT = 30`, a magazine-sized amount instead of the old shared 6-bullet drop that emptied in under a second at gdb's ~11 rounds/sec), and elite-kill-already-owned top-up (`ELITE_SMG_DROP_AMOUNT = 80`). Loot-roll weights (`LOOT_WEIGHTS`/`NORMAL_LOOT_WEIGHTS`/`BONUS_LOOT_WEIGHTS`) rebalanced to add an smg share (gated out entirely until gdb is owned, mirroring the existing rockets/launcher gate); `rollLoot` gained a `hasGdb` parameter for that gate. `damagePerPellet` bumped 7→11 (~57% more) per the explicit ask — still below the pistol's 25/shotgun's per-shot burst, but a meaningful step up given gdb's ~11 shots/sec ceiling. Threaded `smg` through the full ammo lifecycle the same way `bullets`/`rockets` already were: `EngineStats`/`EngineCarryover`, the IDKFA cheat (maxes all three pools now), `fire()`'s ammo check/deduction, `CampaignSave`/`loadCampaignSave` (defaults missing `smg` to 0 for saves written before this field existed — a resumed run with gdb already unlocked just starts it dry, not broken), and `computeScore`'s ammo-remaining bonus (now a three-way average across bullets/rockets/smg instead of two). Deliberately did *not* thread a parallel `hasGdb` gate through `mapGenerator.generate`'s statically-placed `AmmoPickup`s (`placeAmmoPickups`/`placeSecretRooms` stay bullets/rockets-only) — those are explicitly documented as "a backup source, not the primary one," and adding it would have meant changing `generate()`'s signature at both call sites for a source that barely matters next to kill drops; smg ammo is acquired via kill drops only. `doc/user/mechanics.md`, `doc/dev/game-design.md`, and `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s "Ammo/Loot Gating" theme updated to match. Verified against the real dev server via Playwright: loaded `mcdope/codeenstein3d`, typed `IDKFA`, confirmed the HUD switches to a distinct "SMG AMMO" readout when gdb is equipped (separate from `BULLETS`), that holding fire on gdb (auto-fire is `isFireHeld()`-gated, not edge-triggered — a bare `press()` can land entirely between sampled frames, so held it down for real across several frames) drained only the smg pool (999→996 over three ~0.09s-interval shots), and that switching back to the pistol showed `BULLETS` still at its own untouched cheat-maxed value — full pool separation confirmed live, not just by code review [Task 57].

- [x] add "(Build: $date $time)" in title — added a `buildTimestamp()` helper in `vite.config.ts` (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, local to the build machine) injected into the bundle as a `__BUILD_TIME__` global via Vite's `define`, ambient-declared in `vite-env.d.ts`. `main.ts` stamps it onto `document.title` (`🔫 Codeenstein 3D (Build: ...)`) right after imports — mainly so a stale cached bundle after a deploy is obvious from the tab itself rather than silently serving old code. For `npm run dev` this is "when the dev server started," not a live clock, since `define` is evaluated once when the Vite config loads. Verified both `npm run build` completes cleanly with the new `define` block and, via Playwright against the real dev server, that `document.title` actually contains a correctly-formatted `(Build: YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM)` [Task 58].

- [x] canvas should fill as much of the window as possible while keeping aspect ratio — the canvas was capped at `width: min(100%, 960px)`, a fixed max-width that left a lot of a wide monitor unused. Tried a pure-CSS `width:auto; height:auto; max-width:100%; max-height:100%; aspect-ratio:640/400;` fix first and it didn't work: `width:auto` on a replaced element like `<canvas>` falls back to its *intrinsic* size (the 640×400 `width`/`height` attributes) rather than growing to fill available space — `max-width`/`max-height` only ever let it *shrink*, never grow, so the canvas stayed pinned at ~640×400 regardless of window size. Replaced with a JS-driven fit: the canvas is now wrapped in a new `canvasArea` flex-grow `<div>` (absorbs whatever vertical space `#viewport`'s other children — hint caption, controls legend, replay transport bar — don't need) and a `fitCanvasToArea()` function in `main.ts` reads `canvasArea`'s actual content-box size and sets the canvas's inline `width`/`height` in px to the largest 640:400 box that fits inside it, wired up via a `ResizeObserver` on `canvasArea` (fires automatically on window resizes *and* on `canvasArea.hidden` flipping false at a level launch — no explicit call needed at each call site). Also moved `canvas.hidden` toggling to `canvasArea.hidden` throughout (`showLoadingScreen`, `launchLevel`, `showFileTreePlaceholder`, `startReplay`) — hiding an *ancestor* of the Fullscreen API's target element ends fullscreen the same way hiding the element itself does, so this preserves the exact same "leaving the game ends fullscreen, a level transition doesn't" timing the old `canvas.hidden` comment described. `fitCanvasToArea()` is a no-op while `canvas` is actually the fullscreen element (checked via `document.fullscreenElement === canvas`), leaving `.scene-canvas:fullscreen`'s existing CSS to own that state entirely — turned out to be a redundant-but-harmless guard in practice, since the Fullscreen API's UA-level top-layer sizing overrides any inline style regardless, confirmed by direct testing. Verified via Playwright against the real dev server: measured the canvas's actual rendered size at 1920×1080 (1292×808, aspect ratio exactly 1.600 — previously would have capped at 960×600), 1400×900 (772×483), and 1024×700 (396×248) windows, confirmed `canvasArea` genuinely flex-grows via computed-style inspection (not just eyeballing), and confirmed fullscreen enter (fills the full viewport) and exit (cleanly reverts to the fitted size) both still work correctly [Task 59].

- [x] ghidra causes WAY to less dmg - i had to use 2 rockets on some enemies, thats ridicolous considering its a rocket launcher — root cause was purely numeric: a regular (non-pack, non-Elite) enemy's HP scales with its function's complexity (`HP_PER_COMPLEXITY = 25` per point, up to `ELITE_COMPLEXITY_THRESHOLD = 40` before it splits into a pack or becomes an Elite instead), so even a single, unremarkable function with complexity 3-9 already sits at 75-225 HP — routinely above ghidra's old 70 ground-zero `damagePerPellet`, and a direct/near hit rarely even reaches that max once `rocketDamageAt`'s distance falloff is applied. Bumped `damagePerPellet` 70 → 100 (`weapons.ts`), still felt weak in a second playtest pass — bumped again to 150. Blast radius/falloff floor/ammo cost/cooldown all left untouched both times, so this is purely "hits harder," not "hits farther or cheaper" (note: this does mean point-blank self-splash is now more dangerous too, an intentional side effect of the existing "standing too close to your own blast still hurts you" risk/reward design, not a new bug). Verified with `tsc --noEmit`; no other file hardcoded the old damage numbers [Task 60].

- [x] rockets / ghidra pellets should have a nice "boom" particle effect when they hit (be it a wall, or a target) — the existing rocket-blast VFX (`Explosion` in `effects.ts`) was just a growing/fading flat orange ring, no actual debris. Added `ExplosionParticle`: a burst of 16 small squares per detonation that fly outward and up under gravity, cooling in color from a hot white-yellow core through orange to a smoky ember as each one's own remaining life runs out (`spawnExplosionParticles`/`updateExplosionParticles`/`renderExplosionParticles`), layered on top of the existing ring rather than replacing it. Deliberately not gated by the Gore setting (that's specifically about *blood* volume) — a rocket always kicks up the same debris regardless of gore level. Per a same-thread follow-up ask, also gave rockets their own distinct "boom" — `audio.ts` gained `playRocketExplosion()` (a deep sub-bass sweep plus a short filtered white-noise crack transient, bigger/punchier than the existing mine thud) and `advanceRockets` now calls it instead of the shared `playExplosion()`. A second follow-up asked for the same particle-burst treatment on mines too: `traps.ts`'s `updateMines` used to return a bare aggregate damage number, silently swallowing *where* a mine went off — changed to return a `MineDetonation[]` (mirroring `rockets.ts`'s existing `RocketExplosion` shape) so `RaycasterEngine` can spawn the same ring+particle VFX at each mine's exact position, for both the proximity fuse (`applyTrapDamage`) and the gunfire-disarm path (`destroyMine`, including a "safely disarmed at range" shot — it still visibly detonates, just too far to hurt the player). Mines intentionally kept their own existing (smaller, duller) `playExplosion` boom rather than switching to the rocket's new one, since "similar visual effects" was the ask, not "identical to a rocket in every way," and the two already had a deliberately distinct sound identity. `MINE_BLAST_RADIUS` (`traps.ts`) exported so the VFX sizing agrees with the real hitbox, same principle `ROCKET_BLAST_RADIUS` already followed. Verified with a real headless-Chromium harness against the actual bundled code (esbuild + `/snap/bin/chromium --headless=new`, this project's standard recipe for non-parser-layer changes): firing a real rocket down a synthetic corridor into a wall, and walking a synthetic player onto a synthetic mine's fuse radius, both produced non-empty `explosions`/`explosionParticles` arrays that correctly aged back out to empty, and the mine correctly went from `alive: true` to `false` — 9/9 assertions passed [Task 61].

- [x] automap not really useful, should work like in diablo instead. rendering is also weird / wasting space — root cause of the "wasted space" look: `GameMap` is always square (mapGenerator's `mapSize()`, 64-160 tiles), but the canvas is a fixed 640×400 landscape surface; the old "shrink the whole grid to fit a box" sizing always let the height budget win, letterboxing the map as a ≤320×320px square with dead scrim on both sides, and shrinking to ~2px/tile on large maps. Reworked to a Diablo-style non-blocking overlay: opening it (Tab) in `engine.ts` no longer early-returns/freezes the sim — movement, combat, enemy AI, hazards, and projectiles all keep running while it's up, with only the purely-visual weapon viewmodel/crosshair/corner-minimap/compass suppressed (the bottom HUD bar now correctly stays visible too, which the old freeze-based path silently dropped). `automap.ts` replaced the shrink-to-fit math with a fixed 10px/tile viewport that uses nearly the full canvas (reserving the bottom HUD strip and a small margin) and pans/clamps to keep the player centered, exactly like Diablo's map — falling back to centering the whole map only when it's smaller than the viewport. Also restyled to a muted greyscale palette (walls/floor/doors/teleporters/secret walls/lore terminals) per explicit follow-up request ("prefer greyscale like in diablo... shouldn't be in the way visually"), keeping small colored accents only on hazards/active spike traps/mines/the exit so danger/goal tiles still read at a glance; the corner minimap's existing accent hues were reused for consistency. `hud.ts`'s `HUD_HEIGHT` constant exported so the automap can reserve the exact same strip. Verified against the real dev server via Playwright: loaded `mcdope/codeenstein3d` (a real 160×160 grid), opened the automap, walked (W) and turned (D) with it open and confirmed via screenshots that the player kept moving/rotating and the camera panned to reveal newly-explored grey tiles while staying centered on the marker, the HUD bar remained visible throughout, and the map now spans the full canvas width instead of a letterboxed square.

- [x] reduce minimap size by atleast 25%, maybe even 50% — the always-visible top-left corner minimap (`renderMinimap` in `raycaster.ts`, distinct from the Tab-toggled full-screen automap overlay reworked in Task 61) has its entire on-screen footprint driven by a single `maxPixels` default (140), which the per-tile `cell` size, panel `w`/`h`, and outer panel rect all derive from — no other constant needed to change. Halved it to 70, a 50% reduction, per the "maybe even 50%" upper bound in the ask. The compass badge (`COMPASS_BADGE_RADIUS = 13`) is a fixed size independent of the panel and was left untouched. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` [Task 62]

- [x] copyright & license footer, pointing to github repo — the sidebar (`index.html`'s `#sidebar`, a flex column with `#sidebar-header` fixed at top and `#file-tree` flex-growing to fill the rest) had no footer element anywhere in the codebase. Added a new `<footer id="sidebar-footer">` as a sibling right after `#file-tree`, so it pins to the bottom of the sidebar for free without needing its own `flex` rule. Contains a "© 2026 Tobias Bäumer — licensed under AGPL-3.0-or-later" line (linking to the canonical `gnu.org` license text, matching `README.md`'s existing License section and the SPDX header comments already present in `index.html`/`style.css`/etc.) plus a plain link to `https://github.com/mcdope/codeenstein3d`, the repo URL already used consistently elsewhere (Player Guide link, privacy doc link). Styled muted/small/centered via a new `.sidebar-footer` CSS rule, matching the existing `.muted` convention rather than reusing `.settings-btn` since it isn't a call-to-action button. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` and via Playwright against the real dev server: screenshotted the sidebar and confirmed the footer renders pinned to the bottom with both links carrying the correct `href`s [Task 63].

- [x] hash in highscores should be about the workspace, not the current file - this prevents proper comparing — root cause: `HighscoreEntry.hash` (`recordRunHighscore` in `main.ts`) was `hashRun(JSON.stringify(parsed), campaignName())` where `parsed` is only the single file the run happened to end on (died on, or last cleared) — so two runs over the exact same, unedited workspace that merely ended on different levels got unrelated hashes and looked like "different code" in the Highscore UI, defeating the hash's whole purpose. Fixed by folding the hash into the existing whole-codebase background aggregation (`computeCodebaseStats`, already walking every parsable file via `flattenParsableFiles` to sum lines/complexity): each file's path-prefixed parsed-AST JSON is collected in the same stable depth-first order and NUL-joined, then hashed once via `hashRun` alongside the campaign name — `CodebaseStats` gained a `hash` field, and `recordRunHighscore` now prefers `codebaseStats.hash`, only falling back to the old single-file hash if the background aggregation (or `kickOffCodebaseStats`'s own catch path, which now also produces a hash) genuinely didn't finish within `recordRunHighscore`'s bounded wait. The per-level `astHash` used for replay-integrity verification (`replay.ts`'s `ReplayLevelSegment`) is a deliberately separate, still file-scoped mechanism and was left untouched. Updated `doc/user/privacy.md` and `doc/user/hud-and-ui.md` to describe the hash as workspace-wide rather than per-level. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` [Task 64].

- [x] broaden secret rooms causes for looting — dead code (unreachable statements after a `return`) was the only AST pattern that ever triggered a secret room, too rare a find in clean, well-tested codebases. `ParsedFile.deadCodeRegions` generalized into `ParsedFile.secretTriggers: SecretTrigger[]` (a `kind` tag plus start/end line), fed by four new detectors in `astUtils.ts` alongside the existing dead-code one: `findEmptyCatchBlocks` (a catch/except/rescue whose body is empty or contains only comments — a swallowed exception), `findDeprecationMarkers` (`@deprecated`, `[Obsolete]`, or a bare "deprecated" found in a comment, decorator/annotation/attribute node, or string literal — the last one deliberately included so Python-style triple-quoted docstrings are caught too, at the minor cost of a stray runtime string mentioning "deprecated" also counting), `findCommentedOutCodeBlocks` (contiguous comment-node runs, merged across adjacent lines since stacked `//` lines are separate sibling nodes, that span more than 6 lines or contain `{`/`}`/`;`), and `findMagicNumberBlobs` (string literals over 100 chars with no whitespace — Base64/hash-shaped — or a well-known hex magic number: `0xDEADBEEF`, `0xCAFEBABE`, `0x1337`, `0xC0FFEE`, `0xBAADF00D`, `0xDEADC0DE`, `0x8BADF00D`, `0xFEEDFACE`). Node-type vocabulary for catch/annotation/string/number literals added to the shared generic-adapter table (`vocabulary.ts`, covering all 13 generic languages) and as small local constants in the PHP/C bespoke adapters, matching each's existing per-adapter node-type-list pattern; C skips empty-catch detection entirely (the grammar has no exceptions) and scans only comments for deprecation markers (no decorator/attribute concept in scope). `placeSecretRooms` (`mapGenerator.ts`) now shuffles the combined trigger list before applying its existing 5-per-level cap, so the five pattern kinds sample fairly instead of whichever kind happens to be concatenated first (dead code) silently hogging every slot in a file that has several. Also addressed a same-thread follow-up ask: the level-start briefing now shows a "Secrets" stat (`GameMap.secretRoomCount`, threaded through `LevelStartInfo`) alongside Rooms/Enemies, so the player knows up front whether it's worth watching the walls. `doc/user/mechanics.md`, `doc/dev/game-design.md`, and the `index.html` intro screen's "How it works" mapping updated to describe the broadened trigger set; `consoleSidebar.ts` gained a matching flavor hint. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` [Task 65]. Follow-up: playtesting surfaced three real bugs from the broadened trigger set, all fixed. (1) A secret wall next to a lore terminal always triggered the terminal instead — the interact handler (`engine.ts`) checked lore-terminal proximity first (any terminal within `LORE_INTERACT_RADIUS = 1.8` tiles, any direction) and unconditionally returned before ever reaching `tryOpenSecretWall()`'s much tighter facing-based `SECRET_WALL_REACH = 0.9` check; flipped the order so the more deliberate facing-based secret-wall check runs first, falling back to the lore terminal only when it doesn't hit one — `tryOpenSecretWall()` now returns whether it actually opened something. (2) A single file produced 5 secret rooms all carved off the same anchor room — `placeSecretRooms` had no memory of which anchor rooms it had already used, so nothing stopped several different trigger kinds resolving to the same room (or even the same trigger's anchor) from each independently succeeding on a different side of that one room; added a `usedAnchors` set capping it at one secret room per room, walking the full shuffled trigger list (not just the first 5) until the level-wide cap is filled or triggers run out. Fixing that surfaced a deeper, pre-existing root cause shared with lore terminals and goto teleporters: `roomForLine` returned the *first* room whose entity line-range contained a trigger's line, but a method's range always sits inside its enclosing class's range too — since classes always sort before their own methods by `startLine`, every trigger physically inside *any* method of a class was silently anchoring to the whole class's room instead of that method's own room. Fixed `roomForLine` to pick the containing room with the smallest line span (the innermost/most specific entity) instead of the first match — benefits lore terminal and teleporter placement the same way, not just secret rooms. (3) Every secret room seen so far only ever contained health, never rockets — confirmed with the user this was simply because the rocket launcher (ghidra) wasn't unlocked yet on that run, which makes it always health by design (`hasRocketLauncher && rng() < 0.5 ? "rockets" : "health"`, the same ownership gate every other rocket-ammo source in the game already uses) — not a bug, no code change. All three verified against the real running `RaycasterEngine`/`MapGenerator`/parser code in headless Chromium (a stubbed `InputSource` driving real `advance(dt)` calls to confirm interact-priority; synthetic multi-pattern-in-one-function and multi-method-in-one-class PHP sources through the real parser+generator to confirm one-room-max and correct per-method anchoring) and against the real app via Playwright (GitHub-loaded `mcdope/codeenstein3d`, Secrets count rose from 3 to 5 once methods stopped collapsing into their class's room). Second follow-up: (3) turned out to actually need a fix after all — the underlying two-way `hasRocketLauncher ? coinflip(rockets, health) : health` table meant any player who hadn't unlocked ghidra yet really would see *only* health, run after run, with no other outcome ever possible; per explicit ask, secret-room loot needed a real chance at a weapon unlock and an armor drop too, since "if they only spawn health all the time there is no point in searching them." Reworked `placeSecretRooms`'s loot roll into a uniform pick among whatever's actually available that run: health and armor (`"swap"`, `SECRET_LOOT_SWAP_AMOUNT = 40`) are always candidates, rockets joins once the launcher's owned, and a still-unowned gdb/ghidra joins as a `"weapon"` candidate once at least one exists — so armor alone already breaks the "always health" complaint regardless of weapon/rocket state, and finding a secret room can now hand you a weapon outright instead of just ammo/health. `AmmoPickup` (`map/types.ts`) gained a `"weapon"` kind and an optional `weaponIndex` field (mirroring `LootDrop`'s existing shape); `RaycasterEngine.collectLoot()`'s static-pickup path gained a `"weapon"` case, and the already-owned-weapon-tops-up-ammo-instead-of-wasting-the-drop logic (previously only in `applyLoot`, for enemy-kill drops) was factored out into a new shared `grantOrTopUpWeapon()` used by both paths, so a secret-room weapon drop that turns out to already be owned (e.g. a second secret room rolling the same still-missing weapon, or the player unlocking it mid-level via an Elite kill first) gracefully falls back to an elite-sized ammo top-up instead of doing nothing. `UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS` moved from a private `engine.ts` constant to `weapons.ts` (exported) so `main.ts` can compute a `missingWeaponIndices` list from `ownedWeapons` and hand it down through `MapGenerator.generate()` to `placeSecretRooms` as an opaque `number[]` — the map layer never imports engine-layer weapon concepts (a hard architecture rule, see `doc/dev/architecture.md`), same reasoning `hasRocketLauncher` already followed. Both `main.ts` call sites (live level launch and replay playback) updated identically, threading it from the correct source (`carryover`/`segment.carryover`) for each so replay determinism isn't disturbed — same care `hasRocketLauncher` already required. Loot-billboard rendering needed no changes at all: `collectLootBillboards` was already structurally typed against `LootDrop["kind"]` rather than `LootDrop` itself, so a secret room's `"weapon"` pickup automatically gets the same pulsing violet ring as an Elite's weapon drop. `doc/user/mechanics.md`/`doc/dev/game-design.md` updated to describe the new loot table. Verified against the real code in headless Chromium (60+ seeds confirmed health/swap/rockets/weapon all actually occur — not just theoretically possible — plus every `"weapon"` entry carries a valid index and the gated-off case correctly never produces rockets/weapon; a real `RaycasterEngine.advance()` pass confirmed walking onto a `"weapon"` pickup grants and auto-equips it, and a second pass confirmed an already-owned one tops up gdb's smg ammo pool instead) and via Playwright against the real app (no console errors). Third follow-up, per explicit request: both the always-on corner minimap (`renderMinimap` in `raycaster.ts`) and the Tab automap (`drawAutomap` in `automap.ts`) rendered an unopened secret wall in its own subtly-distinct color from a plain wall — a real, if small, giveaway of exactly where every secret room was, defeating the whole "find it yourself" point; the corner minimap's version was worse, since (unlike the automap) it has no fog-of-war gate at all and drew every secret wall's tinted position from the moment a level loaded, before the player had gone anywhere. Both now render `SECRET_WALL_TILE` with the exact same fill as a plain wall — merged into the same branch/color pass rather than a separate one — so an unopened secret wall is genuinely indistinguishable from a real one on either map; once opened it's just plain floor (tile `0`) and needed no special handling at all. The automap's now-unused `SECRET_WALL_COLOR` constant was removed. The one remaining, intentional discovery hint is the much subtler in-view tint (`secretWallTint` in `raycaster.ts`'s 3D wall-cast, a separate code path, untouched) — "very slightly tinted if you look closely," per `doc/user/mechanics.md`. `doc/user/hud-and-ui.md`'s automap description (which had explicitly documented the leak as a feature) corrected to match. Verified by directly calling the real `renderMinimap`/`drawAutomap` against a synthetic map with both a plain and an unopened secret wall and sampling the actual rendered canvas pixels (`ctx.getImageData`): the two tiles' pixels are now byte-identical, the old distinct secret-wall colors (`#424a5f`/`#377fb8` minimap, `#c2c2c8` automap) no longer appear anywhere in either render at all, and an opened secret wall (tile reset to `0`) renders identically to ordinary explored floor. Fourth follow-up: the previous fix only hid the *passage* — the room's interior itself, carved to plain floor (`0`) at generation time in `trySecretRoomOffAnchor`, was still a giveaway: since the corner minimap has no fog-of-war at all and only ever draws wall-colored pixels (floor is simply never painted, left as the translucent panel background), a 3×3 patch of floor sitting inside a sea of solid rock showed up as an obvious room-shaped *hole*, from the moment the level loaded, regardless of the door tile's own color now matching. The automap leaked the same shape too, via a different path: `markVisited`'s reveal radius (`VISITED_REVEAL_RADIUS = 5`, no wall/line-of-sight awareness at all) easily reaches 1-2 tiles past an unopened door from the anchor room's own floor, marking the interior `visited` and letting it fall through to the automap's plain-floor color well before the door is ever opened. Root-caused and fixed properly rather than patched around: `trySecretRoomOffAnchor` now carves the *entire* room footprint — interior and the one connecting tile alike — as `SECRET_WALL_TILE`, not floor; since every render path already treats `SECRET_WALL_TILE` as an ordinary wall (from the prior two follow-ups), a room made entirely of it is genuinely indistinguishable from solid rock on any map, unconditionally, with no fog-of-war or visited-radius edge case left to leak through. `tryOpenSecretWall()` (`engine.ts`) now flood-fills the whole connected 4-directional `SECRET_WALL_TILE` component from the tile opened, converting it all to floor at once, instead of only the single tile the player interacted with — opening the door reveals the whole room in one beat, as it visually always appeared to. Also hardened the room-placement "clear" check with a 1-tile margin around the footprint (not just the footprint itself) before carving, so two different secret rooms can never end up 4-connected/touching — without it, the new flood-fill-on-open could otherwise leak from one room into a second, unrelated one if their footprints ever happened to abut. Confirmed harmless to existing pathfinding/collision/scoring: `SECRET_WALL_TILE` was already treated as solid/impassable everywhere (player collision, enemy AI, `shortestPath`'s BFS) identically to plain wall, and the sole *walkable-tile* consequence is that the "100% Clear" score bonus's denominator now actually excludes secret-room interiors as `isWalkableTile`'s own doc comment already (inaccurately, until now) claimed — previously only the single door tile was excluded, with the interior silently counted despite being unreachable before opening. Verified against the real parser+`MapGenerator`+`RaycasterEngine` in headless Chromium: flood-filled every `SECRET_WALL_TILE` component on a real generated map and confirmed the component count matches `secretRoomCount` with no undersized (1-tile) or merged components; rendered the real minimap and confirmed every tile of every secret room's footprint (not just the door) pixel-matches plain wall exactly; and drove a real engine interact through a real secret door, confirming the *entire* connected 10-tile room flips to floor in one action, not just the tile faced.

- [x] discovering/entering a secret room should yield a score bonus — added `SECRET_ROOM_BONUS_PER_ROOM = 200` to `scoring.ts` (double `LORE_BONUS_PER_TERMINAL`, since opening one takes actively interacting with a specific stretch of wall rather than just walking near a terminal), following the exact same pattern already used for lore terminals: `RaycasterEngine` gained a `secretRoomsOpened` `Set<string>` (keyed by the door tile's `"x,y"`, mirroring `loreRead`), populated inside `tryOpenSecretWall()` the moment a wall is actually opened (before the flood-fill), and its `.size` feeds `computeScore`'s new `uniqueSecretRoomsOpened` input alongside the existing `uniqueLoreTerminalsRead`. Uncapped/count-based like the lore bonus, not a completion-style threshold bonus — no reason to treat it differently since `secretRoomCount` (the level's total) is only ever used for the level-start briefing today, not as a scoring denominator. `doc/user/mechanics.md`/`doc/dev/game-design.md`'s scoring sections updated to mention it. Verified with `tsc --noEmit`; no other call site of `computeScore`/`ScoreInput` exists anywhere else in the codebase to update [Task 66].

- [x] unlockable flamethrower, cone aoe, called "Friday Hotfix". Same drop/unlock mechanics as the existing ones. forced unlock at level 12. Uses dedicated ammo "Gas", to be dropped like other ammo — added as a sixth `WEAPONS` entry (`weapons.ts`): fully automatic, 6 pellets in a 100px-wide cone (nearly 1.5x the shotgun's 70px, the widest in the arsenal), so it melts anything at point-blank range but the spread scatters wide of a target well before `FOG_FAR`, giving it a flamethrower's short effective range without a dedicated max-range mechanic. Drew its own `"gas"` `AmmoType`/`LootKind` pool rather than repeating gdb's original shared-bullets mistake (see the `smg` split in Task 57) — its own HUD readout ("GAS", fiery orange), starting reserve (`STARTING_GAS_AMMO = 40`), drop size (`GAS_DROP_AMOUNT = 30`, magazine-sized like `SMG_DROP_AMOUNT`), and elite-already-owned top-up (`ELITE_GAS_DROP_AMOUNT = 80`). `FRIDAY_HOTFIX_WEAPON_INDEX` added alongside `GDB_WEAPON_INDEX`/`GHIDRA_WEAPON_INDEX` and folded into `UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS`, so the existing Elite-kill guaranteed-drop and secret-room weapon-unlock loot slot (`placeSecretRooms`, already generic over an opaque `missingWeaponIndices` list) picked it up with no map-layer changes at all. `main.ts`'s `FORCED_UNLOCK_LEVELS` gained a `{ level: 12, weaponIndex: FRIDAY_HOTFIX_WEAPON_INDEX }` entry. Threaded `gas` through the full ammo lifecycle the same way `bullets`/`rockets`/`smg` already were: `EngineStats`/`EngineCarryover`, `fire()`'s ammo check/deduction (factored the growing bullets/rockets/smg/gas ternary chain into a small `ammoPool()` helper rather than nesting a fourth level), the IDKFA cheat, `CampaignSave`/`loadCampaignSave` (defaults missing `gas` to 0 for older saves), `rollLoot`'s new `hasFridayHotfix` gate (excludes `"gas"` from the loot table entirely until owned, mirroring `hasGdb`/`hasRocketLauncher`), and `computeScore`'s ammo-remaining bonus (now a four-way average across bullets/rockets/smg/gas instead of three). Gave it a distinct viewmodel silhouette (`viewmodel.ts`): a stubby flared nozzle over a squat strapped-on fuel tank instead of a long barrel, with a lit pilot-light dot and a layered teardrop-blob flame burst on fire instead of the usual star-shaped muzzle flash. `doc/user/mechanics.md`, `doc/dev/game-design.md`, and `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s Ammo/Loot Gating theme updated to describe the fourth weapon/ammo pool. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` and against the real dev server via Playwright: typed `IDKFA` to own every weapon, pressed `5` to switch to Friday Hotfix and confirmed the HUD shows a distinct "GAS" readout, the flamethrower viewmodel renders, and holding fire drains only the gas pool while bullets/rockets/smg stayed at their cheat-maxed values [Task 67].

- [x] flamethrower playtest: range too high, a flamethrower is a closecombat weapon - guess that mean like 1 to 3 tiles, needs testing. firing animation looks like bullet shots, should look more like an actual firing flamethrower instead. if possible: add a burning particle fx to hit enemies as well (no DoT though, it hits hard enough already) — Task 67's cone-spread-only "reads as short range" bet didn't hold up in practice: the Cone of Fire's random deviation still let pellets connect well past a real flamethrower's reach. Added a new `Weapon.maxRange` field (`weapons.ts`, ranged-only counterpart to the existing melee-only `meleeRange`) and set it to 2.5 tiles (later bumped to 3.5, see follow-up below) for Friday Hotfix — `fire()` now gates a hitscan hit (both enemies and mines) behind `weapon.meleeRange ?? weapon.maxRange`, a genuine hard cutoff layered on top of the spread instead of relying on it alone. Replaced the per-pellet thin bullet-tracer line Friday Hotfix shared with every other hitscan gun with a dedicated `FlameStream` effect (`effects.ts`): one fanning, flickering two-layer wedge per shot (spanning the widest/narrowest column any of that shot's pellets actually landed on) instead of 6 separate straight lines, re-jittered every frame so a held trigger reads as a roaring flame rather than a static shape or a spray of bullets. Added `spawnBurnParticles` (`effects.ts`): a handful of small, short-lived embers reusing the existing rocket-detonation `ExplosionParticle` type/update/render pipeline (same cooling white→orange→ember palette, just far fewer/slower/shorter than a full detonation burst, since Friday Hotfix can land several hits a second), spawned from `damageEnemy()` only when the hit came from the `"gas"` ammo pool — purely cosmetic, no damage-over-time added. Verified with a headless-Chromium harness (this project's standard non-parser-layer recipe) driving the real bundled `RaycasterEngine.fire()` against a synthetic map: a point-blank (1 tile) shot damages the target, a 6-tile shot (past the new 2.5-tile `maxRange`) does not, a gas shot pushes exactly one `FlameStream` and zero per-pellet bullet traces, a connecting gas hit spawns burn particles, and a plain pistol shot still pushes its ordinary bullet trace (regression check) — 7/7 assertions passed [Task 68]. Follow-up: two more playtest notes on the same feature. (1) The cone was still too wide and flared immediately off the nozzle instead of only widening near the end — narrowed `spreadPx` 100 → 45 (now tighter than the shotgun's 70px; the hard `maxRange` does the actual range-limiting work now, not a wide cone) and reworked `FlameStream`'s rendering (`effects.ts`) from a straight-sided wedge to a `flameJet` shape whose edges follow a `t^power` curve instead of a straight line — needle-thin for most of the distance, only actually fanning out in the last stretch before the tip, with the brighter inner core layer using a higher power so it stays narrower still. (2) The burn-hit particle effect was barely noticeable — root cause was reusing `ExplosionParticle`'s "burn out mid-air, never settle" physics, sized for a rocket's instant flash rather than a lingering ember; added a dedicated `BurnParticle` type/pipeline (`spawnBurnParticles`/`updateBurnParticles`/`renderBurnParticles`) that falls under gravity like debris but then settles and glows for `BURN_SETTLED_LIFE` (1.6s) once landed, the same "fall, then linger" shape as `BloodParticle`'s floor stain. Hit a real bug while tuning this: the airborne `life` value (originally 0.3-0.5s) could expire before the particle physically reached the floor (worst case ~0.8s given its gravity/velocity ranges), deleting the ember mid-fall before it ever got to settle and linger — fixed by not aging airborne particles out at all; `life` only starts counting down once `settled` flips true. Verified with an extended headless-Chromium harness: `spreadPx` confirmed narrower than the shotgun's; a pixel-sampled canvas check of the real `drawFlameStreams` output confirms the jet stays unfilled off-center halfway up its length but is filled both dead-center there and out at the edge near the tip (the narrow-then-flare shape, not a straight wedge); a real gas hit's ember is confirmed still alive a full second later (proof it settled and is lingering, not blinking out) and confirmed gone by 3 seconds (proof it isn't permanent either) — all assertions passed on the first run after the airborne-life fix. Second follow-up: `maxRange` bumped one tile further, 2.5 → 3.5 (`weapons.ts`) — the short-range identity itself wasn't in question, just felt a notch too tight in practice.

- [x] ensure engine is paused when canvas is not focussed — the existing pause-on-blur only fired on the whole *window* losing focus (alt-tab). It never checked whether the canvas itself was the focused element, so `InputController`'s keydown/keyup listeners (bound to `window`) still fired while, say, the GitHub-repo sidebar text field had focus: typing there sent WASD/Space/Tab/etc. straight into the game (moving the player, firing, toggling the automap) and even blocked the letters themselves from being typed, since `preventDefault()` ran unconditionally for movement keys/Space/Tab. Moved the gameplay keydown/keyup listeners from `window` to the canvas element itself (`input.ts`), so they now only ever fire while the canvas is the actually-focused DOM element; added a `blur` listener on the canvas (alongside the existing `window` one) that forces the same pause-and-clear-keys path as an alt-tab, so losing focus to any other on-page control now pauses the game exactly like it already did for losing focus to another app. `onCanvasClick` now also explicitly calls `canvas.focus()` for browsers (Safari) that don't auto-focus a clicked non-text element. Verified against the real dev server via Playwright: with the canvas focused, W visibly advances the minimap marker; clicking into the GitHub repo text field pauses the game (the existing "PAUSED / Click to resume, or press Esc again" overlay appears) and typing "wasd test" (including the space) lands correctly in the field instead of moving/firing/toggling the automap; merely refocusing the canvas (without a real click) correctly leaves it paused, matching the pre-existing click/Escape-to-resume design rather than silently un-pausing on focus alone. Follow-up: the canvas-blur-forces-pause half of this fix introduced a real regression — pressing Escape while playing (pointer-locked) stopped pausing the game at all. Root cause: real browsers (headless Chromium doesn't reproduce this, which is why the earlier verification missed it) also release pointer lock as a mandatory side effect of Escape while locked, and that release blurs the canvas a moment later, either just before or just after the Escape keydown itself is processed depending on timing; `RaycasterEngine.advance()` drains blur and Escape in the same frame (`if (consumeBlur()) isPaused = true; if (consumeEscape()) isPaused = !isPaused;`), so the blur-forced pause and Escape's own toggle collided and canceled each other out regardless of which order they landed in. A first attempt at fixing this with a timing-window guard in `input.ts` (suppress the canvas-blur pause if it followed an Escape press within ~100ms) only covered the escape-then-blur ordering and still broke on the reverse — a real browser can blur the canvas *before* the Escape keydown is even dispatched to the page at all, meaning a canvas-scoped keydown listener could miss the event entirely. Replaced both attempts with two changes: (1) Escape's own keydown handling moved out of the canvas-scoped listener into a dedicated `window`-level one (`onWindowEscape`, `input.ts`) — the same reasoning `GameHud`'s own overlay-dismiss listener already uses window-scoping for — so it's guaranteed to fire regardless of where DOM focus actually lands; (2) `RaycasterEngine.advance()` now resolves Escape as authoritative over a same-frame blur instead of two independent writes (`if (escaped) isPaused = !isPaused; else if (blurred) isPaused = true;`), so Escape always wins regardless of which order the two fired in within that frame, and the input-layer timing-guess machinery could be deleted entirely. Verified in headless Chromium by manually dispatching both race orderings (Escape-then-blur and blur-then-Escape) directly on `window`/the canvas — both now resolve to a correct pause from a genuinely-running state — and reconfirmed plain Escape toggling, sidebar-click-forces-pause, and typing into the sidebar text field all still work exactly as before. Second follow-up: still didn't actually fix it — the user confirmed live that Escape while playing only released the mouse lock and never paused. The real root cause was more fundamental than a race: real browsers deliberately do NOT dispatch a `keydown` event at all for the specific Escape press that exits pointer lock — a documented Pointer Lock API security measure (so a page can never detect or interfere with the user's own escape hatch) — meaning `onWindowEscape` never sees that keypress no matter where it's bound. Headless Chromium never surfaced this because it doesn't implement real pointer-lock release on Escape either (confirmed: `document.pointerLockElement` stayed the canvas after a synthesized Escape press throughout every earlier "verification," which is why all of that testing was blind to the actual bug). Fixed by detecting the *loss of pointer lock itself* instead of trying to catch the keypress that causes it: added a `pointerlockchange` listener (`onPointerLockChange`, `input.ts`) that queues a new edge-triggered `pointerUnlockQueued` signal whenever `document.pointerLockElement` stops being the canvas — this is the only reliable channel left, and it doesn't care what caused the unlock (Escape being the common case, but it's correct for any cause). Threaded a matching `consumePointerUnlock()` through `InputSource`/`InputController`/`ReplayPlaybackInput` and a `pointerUnlock` field through `InputSnapshot` (`input.ts`, `replay.ts`) so replay recording/playback stays byte-faithful. `RaycasterEngine.advance()` now forces a pause on `blurred || pointerUnlocked`, with Escape's own toggle still taking priority if a real keydown *also* lands in the same frame (kept from the previous fix, since some browsers/situations may not suppress it). Verified in headless Chromium by forcing the exact real-browser signal directly — overriding `document.pointerLockElement` to `null` and dispatching a bare `pointerlockchange` event with *no* accompanying keydown at all — and confirmed the pause now fires correctly from that alone; also reconfirmed plain Escape (pointer not locked), sidebar-click-forces-pause + text field typing, resuming via click, and a keydown+pointerlockchange landing together (still resolves to Escape-wins) all still behave correctly [Task 69]

- [x] keys part of a ongoing cheat input sequence shouldnt be interpreted as input. if input started with "I", ignore following keys for controls if they could be part of a cheat. else interupt the cheat detection sequence and treat it as normal input — `InputController.onKeyDown` (`src/engine/input.ts`) now checks the cheat-prefix match *before* any control branch: a letter that keeps `cheatBuffer` a valid prefix of `IDDQD`/`IDKFA`/`IDCLIP` is swallowed entirely (never added to the held-keys set, never triggers R/F/etc.) and returns early, so typing e.g. "IDDQD" can no longer also strafe (D) or turn (Q) the player via those overlapping letters. The instant a typed letter stops extending every prefix, the buffer resets to empty and that letter (and anything typed after it) falls through to normal control handling again, exactly as before. Replaced the old design (a non-blocking rolling suffix-match buffer that let every cheat letter double as a live control) — `CHEAT_BUFFER_MAX`/slicing removed since the buffer is now naturally capped by the longest code's length. Verified in headless Chromium (bundled `input.ts` standalone via esbuild, dispatched real `KeyboardEvent`s at a canvas): typing "IDDQD" now leaves `KeyD`/`KeyQ` unheld while still firing the cheat; an interrupted sequence ("I" then "W", not a valid continuation) correctly drops the buffer and lets "W" register as normal held movement; a fresh valid sequence typed right after an interruption still fires correctly; and plain WASD with no relation to any cheat is completely unaffected [Task 70]

- [x] still annoyingly long corridors at times. if we cant solve it with geometry, add random rooms and enemies (called "edge case", visually different from other enemies and smaller) there to break the "endless walk feeling". best would be: shorter coridors AND that breakup mechanism — did both halves, per the note. Geometry half: `mapGenerator.ts` gained `MAX_CORRIDOR_STRAIGHT_LENGTH = 9`; a new post-carve pass (`breakUpLongCorridors`, run right after `connectRooms`) scans the fully-carved grid for straight runs past that length — necessary because run length is a property of the whole grid, not any single carved leg, since two unrelated corridor legs can land collinear and combine into one long run neither leg alone exceeds. Each run is interrupted by injecting a small 3x3/4x4 walkable "breakup room" into the path (`tryInjectBreakupRoom`), falling back to forcing a 90° jog (sever a short choke-point stretch back to wall, reroute 2-3 tiles around it) when a room would collide with existing geometry (`tryForceJog`) — a primary pass splits each run into evenly-spaced segments in one shot (cheap, well-distributed), with a small bounded set of wide-search safety-net rescans mopping up anything left over (two legs landing collinear, or a couple of adjacent evenly-spaced targets both landing in the same locally-blocked spot). Breakup rooms are tracked as a new `GameMap.breakupRooms: Rect[]`, deliberately kept separate from the AST-derived `Room[]` array (no synthetic `CodeEntity` needed) so every existing kind-gated system (enemy spawning, hazards, doors, secret rooms, pillars, ...) is structurally blind to them with zero changes — only `placeKeys`/`placeTraps`, which scan the whole grid rather than iterating `rooms`, needed an explicit exclusion. Gameplay half: every breakup room gets 1-3 new "Edge Case" enemies (`spawnEdgeCaseEnemies`, modeled on the existing TODO/FIXME "Bug" encounter's synthetic-`CodeEntity` pattern) — small (0.55x sprite scale), jarring cyan-tinted (`#00FFFF`, `sprites.ts`), 10-15 HP, ~2.2x normal move speed, erratic darting roam (random early retargeting + a per-step heading wobble instead of a smooth glide, `enemyAi.ts`), and ~0.4x normal melee/ranged damage — a literal "bug in the system." Still runs through the exact same aggro/line-of-sight/chase state machine as every other enemy (`Enemy.edgeCase`, mirroring the existing `elite` flag), just with different speed/damage constants. Verified against the real generator/AI/sprite code: a Node+esbuild harness confirmed deterministic regeneration from the same seed, structural exclusivity in both directions (no normal enemy ever spawns in a breakup room, no Edge Case enemy ever spawns outside one), and correct HP (10-15)/per-room count (1-3) ranges; a real headless-Chromium canvas pixel-sample confirmed the correct cyan tint and ~0.55x projected size vs. a normal enemy at the same distance. Caveat found while stress-testing with unusually large/dense synthetic files (30-45 entities on a maxed-out 160-tile map): `connectRooms` chains rooms by parse order, not proximity, so some individual corridor legs are already extremely long by construction (one synthetic case had a 93-tile unbroken run) — the breakup pass reliably brings ordinary/moderate-sized files fully under the limit and dramatically shortens even the worst adversarial cases (that 93-tile run reduced to 13), but doesn't mathematically guarantee zero over-limit runs in every extreme case, matching this generator's existing "best-effort, never a hard failure" placement convention used throughout the file (see `placeTraps`'s doc comment) [Task 72]. Follow-up: playtest feedback that breakup rooms always looked like the same square and the corridor ran straight through them uninterrupted, both correctly diagnosed as real gaps in the first pass. `tryInjectBreakupRoom` now rolls its along-run and across-run dimensions independently (3-5 tiles each, not always a forced 3x3/4x4 square) and offsets the run's own line off-center across the room's width instead of always dead-centering it, so consecutive rooms actually look different from each other. More importantly, a new `breakUpRoomSightline` walls off one interior column/row of the room with a single guaranteed 1-tile gap positioned away from the entry/exit line (the same "solid wall, one gap" technique `carveLabyrinth`'s `divide` already uses for guaranteed connectivity) — without it, the room was just a wider stretch of the same straight corridor, since flooding a rect on top of an already-carved straight run does nothing to interrupt the sightline still running directly through its middle. Hit a real, systematic bug while verifying this: `enemyPositions`' first-enemy-at-room-center pick frequently landed exactly on the new baffle tile for these small odd-dimensioned rooms, and `clearCriticalTiles` (which force-clears the tile under every enemy's position to guarantee it's floor) then silently punched the baffle back open — in a first verification pass this defeated the sightline fix on the majority of rooms, not a rare edge case. Fixed with a new `nearestFloorInRect` that snaps any Edge Case enemy spawn position landing on a wall tile to the nearest actual floor tile within its room (BFS-bounded to the room's own footprint) before `clearCriticalTiles` ever sees it. Verified against the real generator: a Node+esbuild harness confirmed real shape variety (9+ distinct width×height combinations) and, across repeated seeds on small/typical/medium-sized synthetic files, the intended entry-exit sightline is broken in every room checked (0 failures) — a residual ~15% "still straight" rate persists only in an artificially dense worst-case stress scenario (30+ entities packed onto a maxed-out map), traced to coincidental adjacency from a *different*, unrelated corridor touching the room's other wall (a pre-existing property of this generator's parse-order-chained corridor connectivity, not a failure of the baffle itself, which the room's own entry/exit line was confirmed broken in every case checked)

- [x] remove compass feature flag, not needed anymore — the exit compass had been stable and enabled since Task 39's redesign, so the `COMPASS_ENABLED` gate (`hud.ts`) was dead weight; removed the constant and its doc comment and inlined `drawCompass`'s call in `engine.ts` unconditionally (dropped the `if` and the now-unused import). No behavior change — the flag was already `true`. Verified with `tsc --noEmit` [Task 73]

- [x] fps counter shows 60fps with <17ms frametime when paused, is the engine really paused when the game is paused? — investigated, not a bug: `advance()` (`engine.ts`) genuinely early-returns while `isPaused` is true, right after drawing the paused overlay, so no movement/AI/physics/combat runs. What's still ticking is the render loop itself — `frame()` calls `updateFpsCounter(rawDt)` unconditionally every `requestAnimationFrame` tick, *before* `advance()`'s pause check, deliberately using the raw unclamped delta (see its own comment) so the overlay reflects true frame cadence rather than being floored by `MAX_DT` clamping; `renderPausedOverlay()` also does real per-frame work (redraws the frozen scene + pause overlay), so the browser keeps firing rAF at its normal refresh rate throughout. The counter was always measuring render-loop cadence, not simulation activity, so ~60fps/<17ms while paused is expected. No code change made.

- [x] normal enemies should have a tiny chance of dropping a unlockable weapon, on top of their usual drops — added a new pure `rollBonusWeaponDrop` (`loot.ts`), gated the same way as `dropEliteLoot`'s guaranteed-drop roll (only rolls when a weapon is actually still missing, and only draws the second "which weapon" rng() call once the odds roll hits, so a run with every weapon owned never spends an extra rng draw on a kill) at a new `NORMAL_KILL_WEAPON_DROP_CHANCE = 0.01` (1%). Wired into `damageEnemy`'s non-elite branch (`engine.ts`) as an additional `this.drops.push(...)` alongside the existing `rollLoot` drop, not a replacement — a regular kill can now leave two items on the ground. `doc/user/mechanics.md`'s weapon-unlock rows and Loot & Difficulty section, and `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s Ammo/Loot Gating section, updated. Verified with a Node+esbuild harness bundling the real `loot.ts`: zero rng() draws when nothing's missing, exactly one draw on a miss, exactly two (and a correctly-indexed pick) on a hit, and a 200k-trial statistical check landing within 0.3% of the intended 1% hit rate — 8/8 assertions passed. Also `tsc --noEmit` clean [Task 74]

- [x] elite enemies should have not only their usual loot, but also drop a still locked weapon additionally (two drops) with a chance of 60% — reworked `dropEliteLoot` (`engine.ts`): the health-or-ammo drop used to be an either/or fallback only rolled when the weapon-drop branch *didn't* fire; it's now always rolled first, unconditionally. A separate, independent roll then decides whether to *additionally* push a second "weapon" drop at the same position, via `rollBonusWeaponDrop` (`loot.ts`, generalized from Task 74's normal-kill version to take an explicit `chance` parameter, defaulting to the normal-kill rate) at a new `ELITE_BONUS_WEAPON_DROP_CHANCE = 0.6`. Net effect: an Elite now leaves two items on the ground most of the time a weapon's still missing, not a choice between them. Updated every doc/comment describing the old "guaranteed weapon drop" framing (`weapons.ts`'s `STARTING_WEAPONS`/`UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS`/arsenal doc comments, `engine.ts`'s `ownedWeapons` field comment, `doc/user/mechanics.md`'s Elite row and Loot & Difficulty bullets, `doc/dev/game-design.md`'s Elite description, `doc/dev/decisions.md`'s Ammo/Loot Gating section) to "high-odds bonus drop" instead. Follow-up: user flagged the "still-unlocked weapon" wording in the docs/comments as backwards (a weapon you don't have yet is *locked*, not *unlocked*) — corrected to "still-locked" everywhere it appeared (`loot.ts`, `doc/user/mechanics.md`, `doc/dev/decisions.md`); `engine.ts`'s existing "still-unowned" phrasing was already correct and left alone. Verified with a Node+esbuild harness bundling the real `loot.ts` against the actual `mulberry32` seeded PRNG this game uses for every sim-relevant roll (not a toy RNG): confirmed `ELITE_BONUS_WEAPON_DROP_CHANCE` is 0.6, the default (no explicit chance arg) still resolves to the 1% normal-kill rate, an explicit elite-chance roll that would miss at 1% correctly hits at 60%, and a 500k-trial statistical check landed within 0.08% of the intended 60% hit rate — 5/5 assertions passed. Also `tsc --noEmit` clean [Task 75]

- [x] generate code resulting in demo levels (also to be used for testing) — should have all map features; should have all enemy types/combinations; should result in a campaign so we can also use them for verifing saving, highscores and replays; best case they get harder with each level, ending in a heavy battle with multiple elite enemies and packs of normal enemies; level chain/campaign length should satisfy all forced weapons unlocks; extend as useful for future tests; best would be to use multiple languages, to properly demo the engine — added `demo-campaign/` at the repo root: 18 hand-authored source files (`main.c` + 17 `stageNN_*`), one per each of the 15 registered parser adapters (C and PHP each reused once more as thematic bookends — C for the bonus `.h` header, PHP for the finale), ordered purely by filename (`main.c` sorts before any `stageNN_` prefix under the same case-insensitive comparator `compareNodes` uses, so campaign order needed no engine changes at all — confirmed empirically that digits/underscores collate before letters). Each file's complexity/nesting/param-count/comments/visibility were deliberately tuned (verified by actually running the real `MapGenerator` against each, not hand-computed, since RNG-seeded placement like which TODO becomes a trap/mine/"Bug" enemy can only be observed) to hit every map feature and enemy type at least once across the campaign: all 4 enemy pack-size bands, 3 Elites, hundreds of Edge Case swarm enemies, all 3 TODO-encounter outcomes, locked doors+keys, hazard rooms, both `goto`-capable languages' teleporter pairs (C, Go), all 5 secret-room trigger kinds, and the bonus level. The finale (`stage17_the_monolith.php`, ~330 LOC/27 entities) pushes the map to its 160-tile cap, reliably triggering `breakUpLongCorridors`' corridor-breakup rooms for a genuinely chaotic multi-Elite final battle. Deliberately did *not* literally place these under `demo_and_testing_levels` as originally suggested — `demo-campaign/` matches the "Demos" UI naming added alongside it (see the following entry) and reads better as a repo-root directory. Shipped two committed, rerunnable verification scripts rather than another throwaway harness: `scripts/verify-demo-campaign.mjs` (`npm run verify:campaign`) bundles the real parser registry + `MapGenerator` for plain Node via a small custom esbuild plugin (rewrites every Vite `?url` grammar import to a plain absolute path — no DOM/browser needed at all for this one), parses all 18 files in campaign order, and gates on a 21-item coverage checklist (every pack-size band, Elite/Edge-Case presence, all 3 TODO outcomes, every secret-trigger kind, both teleporter levels, the bonus flag exactly once, all 15 languages, zero parse failures) — currently 21/21 passing, non-zero exit on any regression. `scripts/verify-campaign-playthrough.mjs` (`npm run verify:campaign:playthrough`) is a headless-Chromium (Playwright) script that drives the *real* app end-to-end — workspace load via an `Origin Private File System`-backed `showDirectoryPicker` stub (a real `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`, no native dialog), a synchronous "virtual clock" replacing `requestAnimationFrame`/`performance.now`/`Date.now` so seconds of simulated gameplay resolve in milliseconds of real test time, and genuinely real (non-cheat) WASD navigation driven by live position feedback from a small test-only hook added to `src/engine/engine.ts` (`window.__codeensteinTestHooks`, gated behind a `?testHooks=1` query param, inert otherwise) — verifying save-after-level-transition survives a full reload+"Continue Run", a non-cheated death after clearing ≥1 level records a highscore entry whose hash this script independently recomputes (not just trusting the app's own arithmetic) and matches, the save clears on death, and the attached replay payload is structurally sound. Deliberately drives two tiny purpose-built fixtures (`scripts/fixtures/`) rather than the literal `demo-campaign/` files for this specific script: `main.c`'s own first room reliably kills a non-fighting, non-aiming walker (confirmed empirically — bit-identical outcome across repeated runs despite a fresh random gameplay seed each time, since the encounter is close enough that roam-RNG timing never matters) — real combat/aiming is exactly what a real player is expected to bring and this script deliberately doesn't, so it tests the save/highscore/replay *mechanism* against safe content instead; `demo-campaign/`'s own structural correctness is already covered exhaustively by `verify:campaign`. `doc/dev/architecture.md`'s "no test framework" claim narrowed to reflect these two now-committed exceptions. Verified: `npm run verify:campaign` 21/21, `npm run verify:campaign:playthrough` passing reliably across repeated runs, `tsc --noEmit` clean, and a Playwright screenshot check confirming `demo-campaign/` loads and renders correctly (briefing stats matching `verify:campaign`'s own numbers exactly) [Task 76]

- [x] add a UI tab next to GitHub, called "Demos" (originally "Demo level", renamed per follow-up), with a selector to launch the `demo-campaign/` campaign — added `src/fs/demoCampaign.ts`: inlines every `demo-campaign/` file's raw text into the app's own bundle at build time via `import.meta.glob(..., { query: "?raw", eager: true })` and builds the same synthetic `TreeNode` shape `readDirectoryTree`/`fetchGithubTree` already produce (a `RemoteFileHandle` wrapping the already-in-memory string instead of a real handle or a network fetch), so every downstream consumer (file tree UI, entrypoint detection, level launching, replay) needed zero changes. `index.html` gained a fourth `#tab-demo`/`#tab-panel-demo` launch tab (styled/wired identically to the existing three — the tab-activation loop in `main.ts` was already generic over `Object.keys(launchTabs)`, so extending the `LaunchTab` union was the only logic change needed there) with a single "Launch Demo Campaign" button, following the same load-sequence `loadGithubRepoFromInput` uses (`workspaceTree`/`workspaceRootName`/`campaignLevelIndex` reset, `renderFileTree`, `autoLaunchInitialLevel`) but synchronous since there's no fetch to await. Added a new `workspaceIsDemo` flag alongside the existing `workspaceIsRemote` (a bundled tree can't be re-picked locally either, so autosave/"Continue Run" stay disabled the same way GitHub already does) specifically so a demo run's highscore entry gets its own `source: "demo"` instead of being misattributed as `"github"` — widened `HighscoreEntry.source` (`highscores.ts`) to `"github" | "demo"` and added a matching `startReplay` branch that rebuilds the bundled tree instead of prompting a picker or re-fetching. Updated `README.md` (Loading Options, Project Structure tree), `doc/dev/architecture.md` (`fs` layer description), `doc/user/getting-started.md` (four tabs now, not three; corrected a pre-existing inaccuracy along the way — GitHub runs never actually autosaved either, `workspaceIsRemote` blocks `persistProgress` entirely, so the old doc's "GitHub has no re-pick prompt" phrasing implied a save/continue flow that never existed), and `doc/user/privacy.md` (a new bullet stating the Demos tab makes no network request and reads no local files at all). Verified with `tsc --noEmit` and against the real dev server via Playwright: clicking the new tab shows the right panel, clicking "Launch Demo Campaign" loads `demo-campaign` and auto-starts `main.c` with briefing stats matching `verify:campaign`'s own output exactly [Task 77]

- [x] unlockable chainsaw, called "Toolchain" - melee weapon, replaces the knife after pickup/unlock. stronger lifesteal effect than knife, dmg = knife times 2. endless ammo. no forced unlock. unlock spawned in secret rooms starting from the 4th level and dropped by elite mobs. should fire as long as melee hotkey is hold — added as a second melee `WEAPONS` entry (`weapons.ts`), 2x the knife's damage (80 vs 40), a bigger lifesteal (3 vs 1), infinite ammo like the knife, and `auto: true`/`fireIntervalSec: 0.35` so it fires repeatedly while Left-Ctrl is held instead of once per press — the first melee weapon in the game to do so, requiring a new `isMeleeHeld()` (`input.ts`, mirroring `isFireHeld()`) and a `meleeHeld` field threaded through `InputSnapshot`/replay recording so held-melee replays deterministically. "Replaces the knife" is handled by a new `currentMeleeWeapon(ownedWeapons)` helper (`weapons.ts`) that `engine.ts`'s quick-melee firing and viewmodel-overlay code now call instead of the old hardcoded `MELEE_WEAPON` constant, resolving to Toolchain the instant it's owned rather than needing a second equip slot. Deliberately *not* added to `UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS` (excludes it from the ordinary 1%-per-kill roll and the `FORCED_UNLOCK_LEVELS` safety net, matching "no forced unlock") — instead a new `TOOLCHAIN_MIN_LEVEL = 4` gates its own separate entry into the secret-room candidate list (`main.ts`'s new `computeMissingWeaponIndices`, replacing two duplicated `UNLOCKABLE_WEAPONS.filter(...)` call sites) and the Elite bonus-drop candidate list (`dropEliteLoot` in `engine.ts`), both reusing the existing 60%-odds Elite drop mechanism unchanged. The level gate reads a new `EngineCarryover.campaignLevelIndex` field, threaded through so a recorded replay's own `carryover` reproduces the exact same gated candidate list (and thus the same seeded RNG draw) it saw live. New chainsaw viewmodel silhouette in `viewmodel.ts` (motor housing + serrated guide bar), reusing the knife's existing recoil-thrust animation so repeated hold-to-fire swings read as a buzzing chug for free. Updated `doc/user/mechanics.md` (weapon table + loot bullets), `doc/dev/game-design.md` (weapon/economy intent paragraph), and `doc/dev/decisions.md` (Ammo/Loot Gating section) [Task 78]

- [x] limit engine to 60fps, no point in running faster — tried and abandoned; net no code change. Added a `TARGET_FPS`/`MIN_FRAME_INTERVAL_MS` throttle to the internal rAF loop (`RaycasterEngine`'s private `frame()` in `engine.ts`): every rAF tick still fired (staying vsync-aligned), but the sim/render step was skipped until a full target frame interval had elapsed since the last processed frame, with a modulo correction on `lastTime` so the cadence locked to the target instead of drifting. Verified in a headless-Chromium harness that this correctly throttled a simulated 240Hz input down toward the target while leaving a slower 30Hz input untouched. In real use on the user's own 60Hz display, though, a plain `TARGET_FPS = 60` (16.67ms threshold) didn't reliably hit a full 60fps (ordinary timer jitter dropped some legitimate ticks); raising it to 75 (13.3ms threshold) to give that jitter headroom made real performance measurably *worse* instead of better — lower fps, higher frame time. Since a looser threshold can only ever pass through the *same or more* real ticks, that result means the throttle itself was the problem, not a tuning target: on this hardware/engine the raw per-frame sim+render cost is already at the edge of what the pipeline can sustain, and merely checking/gating every rAF tick against a threshold (plus the resulting request/skip/re-request churn) added enough overhead to push it into contention (missed/queued paints), capping the user at ~50fps when the engine ran a clean, comfortable 60fps completely uncapped before this was ever added. Fully reverted (`frame()` back to unconditionally running `advance()` off the raw rAF delta, `TARGET_FPS`/`MIN_FRAME_INTERVAL_MS` removed) — confirmed via `git diff` that `engine.ts` matches its pre-Task-79 state exactly. Conclusion: this engine's real bottleneck is per-frame cost, not "running faster than useful"; a throttle here trades away headroom the renderer needs rather than saving unnecessary work. Revisit only alongside real render/sim optimization (see the open "code optimization/reuse, complexity reduction" item), not as a standalone cap [Task 79].

- [x] ship default highscores for demo campaign, record them via automated playthrough. do 10 playthroughs, keep the best 3 as default highscores with replay for players not having localStorage content yet — added a headless-Chromium bot (`scripts/generate-default-highscore.mjs`, `npm run generate:default-highscore`) that plays the real bundled `demo-campaign/` with genuine (non-cheated) input and bakes its best runs into `src/engine/defaultHighscore.ts`, a `DEFAULT_HIGHSCORE_ENTRIES: HighscoreEntry[]` shown only when a player's own board is empty. Navigation (`scripts/lib/routePlanner.mjs`) is a worklist over `pathfind.mjs`'s existing BFS: try a route to the exit, detour to a reachable key then a reachable locked door if blocked (mirroring `openDoorAhead()`'s "any key opens any door" rule and `mapGenerator.ts`'s solvability guarantee), and prefer routes avoiding hazard/spike tiles but fall back to crossing them (they're timed/tick damage, not instant death, and some levels — confirmed empirically, e.g. `main.c` — only have a route across one). Combat is a small heuristic: extended `window.__codeensteinTestHooks` with a new read-only `getEnemies()` (`engine.ts`, same inert-unless-`?testHooks=1` pattern as the existing two), and the bot turns toward the nearest alive+aggroed+nearby enemy and fires once roughly aimed, otherwise turns/walks toward its planned waypoint. Firing turned out to only need `Space` — both a single-shot weapon's edge-triggered `fireQueued` and an auto weapon's held `isFireHeld()` key off it (`this.keys.has("Space")`), so the bot never touches the mouse or Pointer Lock at all. That absence of mouse input wasn't an obvious first design: an earlier version drove firing through real `page.mouse.down()`/`up()` (reasoning "firing requires Pointer Lock"), which empirically turned out to synthesize real `mousemove` events even with no explicit move call — once Pointer Lock engaged, those landed as huge, uncapped mouse-look rotations (unlike keyboard turning, hard-capped at `ROT_SPEED*dt`/frame) and made the bot's facing spin uncontrollably; switching to `Space`-only input fixed it completely and is simpler besides. The generator runs 10 fresh playthroughs (isolated `browser.newContext()` per run, so `localStorage` starts genuinely empty and `main.ts`'s own `randomSeed()` naturally varies each run's `gameplaySeed`), keeps the 3 highest-scoring non-cheated results, and re-verifies each kept entry's replay `astHash` values against a fresh on-disk hash before writing the output file (catches the campaign changing mid-generation). Wired into the read path via a new `loadHighscoresForDisplay()` (`highscores.ts`) that only substitutes the shipped entries when the real `loadHighscores()` result is empty — `recordHighscore` still calls the real, untouched `loadHighscores()` directly, so a live run's read-modify-write cycle can never merge the fake entries into a player's actual board; only `main.ts`'s Highscores-dialog click handler was switched to the new function. `defaultHighscore.ts` is dynamically `import()`ed inside `loadHighscoresForDisplay` rather than statically, since the 3 embedded replays (full per-frame input data, uncompressed by design — no `localStorage` quota to work around, and gzipping a source constant would make it an unreviewable diff) come to ~3.4MB; a dynamic import code-splits that into its own chunk fetched only the first time someone actually opens an empty Highscores dialog, not on every page load. Verified end-to-end against the real dev server via a headless-Chromium check: a genuinely empty `localStorage` shows all 3 shipped rows (not the "No runs recorded yet" empty state) each with a working "Watch" button; clicking one successfully relocates the bundled demo tree, re-parses, re-hashes, and starts real playback with no `astHash`-mismatch refusal (confirmed via live `getPlayerState()` position changing over 2 real seconds of playback); and driving one more genuine (non-cheat) run to death correctly shows exactly the 1 real entry afterward, not mixed with or reverting to the 3 shipped ones.
