Every one of the 10 generation runs that produced a valid entry cleared exactly `main.c` (level 1) and died on `stage02_bootstrap.sh` (level 2) — consistent rather than varied, but within the accepted scope: `recordHighscore` accepts any non-cheated run with `levelsCleared >= 1`, the bot was never intended to reliably clear all 17 levels (especially the multi-Elite `stage17_the_monolith.php` finale), and "run several times, keep the best" was the explicit ask rather than a guaranteed full clear. `defaultHighscore.ts`'s own doc comment (and this entry) flag that it must be regenerated if `demo-campaign/`'s source files ever change, since a stale `astHash` makes `startReplay` refuse playback — a doc-comment warning, not an automated staleness checker, since this is a manually-triggered, infrequent regeneration step [Task 80].

- [x] add a GitHub Action to run the verify-campaign script — added `.github/workflows/verify-campaign.yml`: checks out, sets up Node 20 (with npm cache), runs `npm ci`, then `npm run verify:campaign` (`scripts/verify-demo-campaign.mjs`, the pure-Node/no-browser structural coverage check over `demo-campaign/`). Triggers on push to `master`, on every pull request, and via manual `workflow_dispatch`. Set `PLAYWRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_DOWNLOAD=1` for the job — `npm ci` would otherwise trigger the `playwright` package's postinstall Chromium/Firefox/WebKit download, which this specific script never needs (it only bundles the parser + `MapGenerator` via esbuild, no browser at all) — keeps the run fast. Deliberately scoped to just this one script per the explicit ask, not the broader still-open "CI for the tests" backlog item (`verify:campaign:playthrough` needs real Playwright/Chromium and a `tsc`/build gate would be a separate job) [Task 81].

- [x] code optimization/reuse, complexity reduction etc — four rounds of engine/parser perf and dedup work, in commit order. **Task 82** (`sprites.ts`/`engine.ts`/`main.ts`/`astUtils.ts` + adapters) — a shotgun/MP shot was re-projecting every living enemy and every visible mine from scratch per pellet; new `projectLivingEnemies`/`projectVisibleMines` snapshot the projection once per shot, and `findTargetInProjections`/`findMineInProjections` hit-test against that snapshot (still re-checking `.alive` per pellet, since an earlier pellet in the same shot can kill/destroy the target mid-loop) — `findTargetAtColumn`/`findMineAtColumn` stay as thin single-hit wrappers for the aim reticle. Also switched `main.ts`'s background `computeCodebaseStats` scan from a fixed every-20-files yield to a `CODEBASE_STATS_TIME_BUDGET_MS` (6ms) wall-clock budget (bounds worst-case stall regardless of how large/slow any one file is, instead of letting a burst of unusually large files run unbounded between yields), and had the C/PHP/generic adapters share one `descendantsOfType` comment-node walk between `extractLargeComments`/`findCommentedOutCodeBlocks` instead of each re-walking the tree (`astUtils.ts`'s new `...FromNodes` variants). **Task 83** (`ammo.ts`/`lootApply.ts`/`enemyAi.ts`/`sprites.ts`, pure dedup/no behavior change) — four parallel ammo fields plus ~7 repeated 4-way branches collapsed into one `Record<AmmoType, number>` + per-pool metadata table; `applyLoot`/`grantOrTopUpWeapon`/`dropEliteLoot` moved out of the 300+-line `engine.ts` into their own `lootApply.ts` behind a narrow `LootContext`; the elite/edge-case damage and speed ladders and the two near-identical per-axis wall-slide implementations in `enemyAi.ts` collapsed into shared helpers; `projectiles.ts`/`rockets.ts`'s near-duplicate orb-billboard collectors (including a duplicated private clamp) unified into one palette-parameterized `collectOrbBillboards` in `sprites.ts`. **Task 84** (`enemyAi.ts`/`rockets.ts`, new `spatialGrid.ts`) — an already-aggroed enemy no longer re-ray-marches line of sight every frame just to redo an idempotent write, and the aggro-check/ranged-fire-check now share one lazily-memoized per-enemy-per-frame LOS result instead of computing it twice; rockets got a new tile-bucketed `EnemySpatialGrid` (rebuilt only on frames with a rocket in flight) replacing a full-enemy-array scan per rocket for proximity and per blast for AoE — kill order (and the seeded loot rolls it drives) is preserved via ascending array-index order. **Task 85** (new `pathField.ts`) — the biggest remaining scaling cost on enemy-dense maps: every chasing enemy previously flood-filled its own fresh windowed BFS every frame; replaced with one shared full-map BFS distance field rooted at the player's tile (flat `Int32Array`, reflooded only when the player crosses a tile boundary or the grid mutates, tracked via a new engine `gridVersion` counter), which every enemy now just descends with the same neighbor order/strict-decrease rule as before (the old ±(aggro+2)-tile window check is kept so distant sticky-aggroed enemies still steer straight rather than gaining cross-map pathing). SIM-AFFECTING: the old per-enemy flood was window-clipped, so a chasing enemy can now occasionally find a route the old code couldn't see — shipped default highscores/replays regenerated (`npm run generate:default-highscore`) and re-verified to match. All four rounds kept `tsc --noEmit`/`verify:campaign`/`verify:campaign:playthrough` green; Tasks 82-84 confirmed behavior-identical via a same-session replay trajectory digest, Task 85 confirmed replay-*compatible* (not identical — see SIM-AFFECTING above) after regeneration. Directly targets the magento `check_copyright_pr.php` shooting-performance report (below): the described symptom — a large function count (many enemies) plus repeated per-frame/per-shot O(enemies) work while firing — is exactly what Tasks 82/84/85 remove, though not independently re-confirmed by the reporting users post-fix.

- [x] When loading magento from github, some users reports tanking performance when shooting in check_copyright_pr.php (and other files) even though the game runs at 120fps for them usually. This does not happen in the demo campaign for them. — likely explained by Tasks 82/84/85 above: `check_copyright_pr.php` (and similar large real-world files) parse to a high function/complexity count, which spawns far more enemies than any hand-authored `demo-campaign/` level, and the removed hot paths (per-pellet enemy/mine reprojection, redundant per-frame LOS ray-marches, full-enemy-array rocket scans, per-enemy windowed BFS flood fills) all scale with enemy count and were specifically worst while actively firing — matching "tanking performance when shooting" exactly, and why the demo campaign (few enemies per level) never showed it. Closing on that strength of match rather than a confirmed repro — no direct before/after profile from an affected user's machine exists, so reopen if it resurfaces.

- [x] Textures from WADs? (to be discovered/done later) — added real texture-mapped walls, doors, and floors to the raycaster, sourced by default from procedurally-generated patterns (a brick wall, a steel bonus-wall panel, a two-leaf door, a flagstone floor — `src/engine/textures.ts`, keeping the engine's existing "no external asset files" approach) or, if the player loads a DOOM WAD file via a new sidebar "Load WAD Texture Pack" button, real composited wall/door textures and floor flats parsed out of it by a from-scratch WAD/PLAYPAL/TEXTUREx/patch/flat binary parser (`src/wad/`, hand-rolled — see Dependency Minimalism in decisions.md). Texture selection is fully automatic: a hardcoded name allowlist per slot (regular wall, bonus-level wall, door, regular floor, bonus-level floor), tried in order, first match wins, silent per-slot fallback to the procedural default otherwise — no texture-picker UI. `raycaster.ts` gained real texture-coordinate math (the classic DDA `wallX` formula) and per-column `drawImage` sampling for walls/doors (plus a shading overlay reproducing the old fog/side-shade multiply), and the floor-cast (`renderBackground`) now always runs (its old flat-fill fast path for hazard-free maps was retired) and samples a real floor texture per pixel. Secret walls deliberately reuse the plain wall texture plus the existing subtle tint, now applied as a translucent overlay instead of a flat-color RGB nudge — must stay near-visually-identical to a plain wall, unchanged design constraint. Doors join the textured group (a real WAD door texture, or a distinctly two-panel procedural default, replaces the old flat `DOOR_RGB` fill entirely) since DOOM door linedefs use ordinary composite wall textures. Lore terminals, the ceiling, and hazard/teleporter/spike-trap floor tiles deliberately stay flat-colored/pulsing — each already has its own load-bearing visual language a generic texture would dilute. Checked the npm registry first and found nothing usable (Node-only native-binding packages, unmaintained 0.0.x experiments scoped as full map editors, or geometry libraries for full BSP triangulation) — hand-rolling ~400 lines against the actual (small, fully-specified) WAD format was the better trade. Verified in stages: `npm run verify:wad-parser` (new, Node/no-DOM, hand-built synthetic WAD fixtures — no real IWAD bundled, copyright) covers header/directory/palette/PNAMES/TEXTURE1 round-trip including a two-patch overlap-with-a-hole compositing case, flat decoding, and every fallback path (missing PLAYPAL, missing flat block, allowlist miss, malformed/truncated input) never throwing; `npm run verify:wad-textures` (new, Playwright against the real dev server) covers default-texture pixel variance, a synthetic-WAD swap actually reaching the live renderer (status text + changed pixels), a broken file failing gracefully without breaking the game, and the ceiling staying flat regardless of pack. The allowlist itself was verified against real IWADs — Freedoom Phase 1 & 2 (free/open-source, not the copyrighted `doom.wad`/`doom2.wad`) — every slot's first candidate name (STARTAN3/COMPBLUE/BIGDOOR2/FLOOR4_8/CEIL5_1) matched on both, and a Playwright screenshot confirmed the real textures render correctly in live gameplay. Updated `doc/dev/architecture.md` (Rendering section, `src/wad/`'s place in the layering) and `doc/dev/decisions.md` (Dependency Minimalism) [Task 86].

- [x] github ci actions for the tests (to be executed on github, no custom runner) — extended [Task 81]'s `.github/workflows/verify-campaign.yml` (renamed to `verify.yml`, now covering more than just the demo campaign) with a `verify:wad-parser` step in the existing no-browser job, and a new `verify-browser` job that installs Playwright's Chromium (`npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, cached via `actions/cache` keyed on `package-lock.json`'s hash), boots the real Vite dev server in the background on port 5173 behind a curl poll-loop readiness gate, and runs `verify:wad-textures` plus `verify:campaign:playthrough` against it (the latter via a `CODEENSTEIN_DEV_URL` override, since its own default port is 5183, to reuse the one dev server instead of starting a second). All four `verify:*` npm scripts are now CI-wired on push to master, every PR, and manual `workflow_dispatch`. A broader `tsc`/build/lint gate remains out of scope — tracked separately below [Task 87].

- [x] textures for lore terminals and traps. maybe also ceiling? — extended the WAD/procedural texture system from [Task 86]'s wall/door/floor slots to 5 more: lore-terminal walls (a real composite wall texture, e.g. Doom's `COMPUTE2` computer-bank patch, plus a much more visible pulsing tint overlay on top — unlike secret walls' barely-there overlay, lore terminals want to be *noticed*, and that pulse used to be their entire "this is interactive" signal before texturing), hazard/acid floors (`NUKAGE1-3` toxic-sludge flats), teleporter-pad floors (`GATE1-4` swirling flats), and spike traps (two different flats — a plain/metal one and a blood/lava one — swapped based on `activeSpikeTileKeys`'s existing safe/active phase check, rather than tinting one texture, so the real "don't stand here right now" signal survives as which flat is showing instead of a color). Hazard/teleporter tiles get no tint at all, matching how Doom itself textures these; the old JS-computed sine-wave brightness pulse on those is gone (real Doom animates them via multi-frame flat-cycling, which this engine doesn't implement). This explicitly overturned the prior "each already has its own load-bearing visual language a generic texture would dilute" design call from Task 86 — a deliberate, discussed reversal, not an oversight. The ceiling is the one thing left out of scope, still flat-colored, since it isn't a `Tile` kind with map-grid identity the way the others are. All 10 slots' allowlist candidates were verified end-to-end (not just name presence) by driving this repo's own parser against a real Doom IWAD the user had locally (`/home/mcdope/Schreibtisch/doom.wad`) — every one decoded to real, fully-opaque pixels. Extended `scripts/fixtures/buildTestWad.mjs` and both `verify:wad-parser`/`verify:wad-textures` with checks for the 5 new slots; both scripts stay green. Updated `doc/dev/architecture.md`'s Rendering section and the `CHANGELOG.md` Unreleased entry [Task 88].

- [x] chainsaw holdfire crashing my local chromium, wtf? repro on other machines — turned out not to be a memory leak (an initial fix to a real but unrelated Web Audio node-caching leak in `distortionNode()`/`audio.ts` — a single shared `WaveShaperNode` was reused forever and accumulated a permanent `connect()` edge per shot — was legitimate but didn't stop the crash). Root cause: Toolchain's melee-fire was bound to Left-Ctrl, and holding it while also pressing W (forward) spells out `Ctrl+W` — Chrome's (and every major browser's) hardcoded "close this tab" shortcut, which page JavaScript cannot `preventDefault()` under any circumstances, by design. The browser was closing itself outright, not crashing — confirmed by the reported symptom of focus falling back to VS Code with Ctrl still held, immediately re-triggering VS Code's own `Ctrl+W` "close terminal" binding. Confirmed unreproducible via any synthetic/programmatic `KeyboardEvent` dispatch (Playwright `dispatchEvent`) since those never reach a browser's native accelerator table — only a real physical keychord does, which is why extensive headless/headed Chromium repro attempts with real unmuted Web Audio and live heap/audio-node instrumentation never reproduced it despite closely matching conditions. Fix: quick-melee (knife/Toolchain) rebound from Left-Ctrl to Space (`input.ts`'s `isMeleeHeld`/`consumeMelee`/`onKeyDown`), and ranged weapon fire's old Space binding was dropped entirely rather than moved — fire is now mouse/gamepad-only for real players, since keyboard-only play was never a supported control scheme. Updated every doc referencing the old binding (`README.md`, `doc/user/controls.md`, `doc/user/mechanics.md`, `doc/user/tips.md`, `doc/dev/game-design.md`) plus internal code comments in `weapons.ts`/`engine.ts`. `scripts/generate-default-highscore.mjs`'s headless bot (Task 80) had relied on the old Space-fires-ranged-weapons behavior specifically to dodge a Pointer-Lock/`page.mouse` mouse-synthesis bug (real mouse events under Pointer Lock synthesize spurious `mousemove` deltas that spin the camera uncontrollably) — rather than leave that broken, added a second, deliberately undocumented keyboard fire key (`Backquote`) purely as an automation escape hatch, picked specifically because it's a lone non-modifier key nowhere near WASD/Q/E so it can't collide into a reserved browser shortcut the way Ctrl+W did. Bot script and its doc comments updated to use it; verified end-to-end against the real dev server that a `Backquote` tap correctly fires the equipped ranged weapon.

- [x] ensure running github loaders are terminated on start of a new workspace — a GitHub tree fetch (`fetchGithubTree`) is the one workspace-loading step slow enough to still be in flight when the user fires off a second load before it resolves (a different repo, a local folder pick, the demo campaign, Continue Run, or Watch Replay's own re-fetch) — previously that stale fetch would eventually resolve or reject and clobber whatever the newer load had already committed (confirmed via a Playwright repro: starting a GitHub load then switching to the demo campaign showed the demo load correctly, then ~3s later the stale GitHub fetch's failure overwrote `#workspace-name` with "Failed to fetch" anyway). Fixed with a module-scoped generation counter (`workspaceLoadGeneration`/`beginWorkspaceLoad()` in `main.ts`) bumped at the start of every workspace-loading entry point (`selectButton`, `loadGithubRepoFromInput`, `loadDemoCampaign`, `continueButton`, `startReplay`) — each checks its own captured generation against the current one after every `await` (success path and `catch`) before touching shared state or the DOM, discarding stale results silently. `fetchGithubTree` (`src/fs/github.ts`) also gained an optional `AbortSignal` parameter threaded through both its underlying `fetch()` calls, and `beginWorkspaceLoad()` aborts the previous load's in-flight controller outright — a real network-level "terminated", not just an ignored result, so a superseded fetch stops consuming GitHub's rate limit/bandwidth for a tree nobody wants anymore. Verified via the same Playwright repro with the fix applied: the stale fetch's rejection is silently swallowed and `#workspace-name` stays "demo-campaign" [Task 89].

- [x] ensure github loaders dont escalate with loading, when the next few levels are preloaded that is enough. no need to parse any file ahead in a repo with some thousand files. — three changes in `main.ts`, all scoped to reducing unnecessary eager file reads for a remote (GitHub) workspace: (1) `findEntrypoint`'s filename-convention stage (looking for `main.c`/`index.js`/etc.) now runs against a plain synchronous tree walk (`flattenAllFiles`, zero I/O) instead of first calling `flattenParsableFiles`, which used to sniff every extensionless file's content over the network across the *whole* tree just to build a "parsable files" list before even checking for a name match — safe since every `ENTRYPOINT_FILENAMES` entry already has a registered extension, so parsability there was never actually in question. For the common case (a repo has a conventional entrypoint filename) this removes the full-tree network scan entirely. (2) `findEntrypointByScanning` (the exhaustive main()-scoring fallback, only reached when no filename matches) now takes an `AbortSignal` — previously `autoLaunchInitialLevel`'s `withTimeout` only stopped *waiting* on this scan once `ENTRYPOINT_DETECTION_TIMEOUT_MS` (4s) elapsed, but the scan itself kept fetching and parsing the rest of the repo's files in true background forever regardless, for a result nothing would ever read; it's now wired to `AbortSignal.timeout(ENTRYPOINT_DETECTION_TIMEOUT_MS)` and checks it every iteration, so it actually stops making further requests once the game has already given up and moved on. (3) `kickOffCodebaseStats` (the whole-codebase-hash background aggregation used for highscore fairness — hashes every parsable file so two runs over an identical, unedited codebase compare equal regardless of which level they ended on) now skips itself entirely for a remote workspace over `REMOTE_CODEBASE_STATS_FILE_CAP` (300) files, rather than unconditionally fetching and parsing every file in a repo of "some thousand files" just for stats most runs will never even wait on — safe to skip outright since `recordRunHighscore` already had a graceful fallback (hash just the single ended-on file) for exactly the "aggregation isn't available" case, the same path it already took when the background pass simply didn't finish within its existing 8s wait. Local (disk-backed) workspaces are untouched by all three changes — same full-tree behavior as before, since disk reads have no per-file network/rate-limit cost the way a GitHub fetch does. Verified via `npm run typecheck` (clean) and two Playwright checks against the real dev server: the demo campaign still correctly auto-detects and launches `main.c` via the new fast name-match path (no regression), and `npm run verify:campaign`'s full structural coverage check still passes end-to-end [Task 90].

- [x] follow-up to Task 90 — user reported still seeing "hundreds of http requests, way over the limit" after that fix shipped. Asked about downloading the repo as a single zip and unpacking it in memory instead (using the zip's own hash in place of the AST-based one). That's not achievable: verified directly via `curl -I` that every path to a GitHub zip archive (`codeload.github.com/.../zip/...`, `github.com/.../archive/....zip`, `api.github.com/.../zipball/...` — the latter two both redirect to the same codeload URL) serves from `codeload.github.com`, which hardcodes `access-control-allow-origin: https://render.githubusercontent.com` — not a wildcard, not origin-reflection — so a browser `fetch()` from this app's real origin is CORS-blocked regardless of entry point, unlike `raw.githubusercontent.com`/`api.github.com` (both already used here, both send `access-control-allow-origin: *`). A CORS proxy was considered and rejected (would route arbitrary repo content through third-party infrastructure, against this project's own "no data leaves the app" goal); GitHub's GraphQL API can batch file reads but requires an authenticated token even for public repos, breaking this app's fully-anonymous operating model — also rejected. User chose to keep the existing per-file architecture and tighten it further instead. Planned fix (in-session): `advanceToNextLevel`/`findNextParsableFile` re-walks the *entire* tree via `flattenParsableFiles` on every single level clear, not just once at load — memoized it per workspace root (`parsableFilesCache`, a `WeakMap<TreeNode, Promise<TreeNode[]>>`, skipped only for the one caller that needs live per-file progress callbacks) so that walk now happens at most once per workspace load. That fix is real (removes redundant async/CPU overhead across a multi-level campaign either way) but empirical audit (Playwright, counting real `raw.githubusercontent.com`/`api.github.com` requests against `id-Software/DOOM`, ~97 parsable files — chosen because it has no `ENTRYPOINT_FILENAMES` match, its real entrypoint being `d_main.c`/`i_main.c`) showed it wasn't the dominant cost: **99 requests fired during initial load alone**, all before any gameplay or even a second level. Root cause turned out to be `findEntrypointByScanning` (the exhaustive main()-scoring fallback) — its `AbortSignal.timeout` (Task 90) only bounds wall-clock time, not request count, and fetching is fast enough that it can make it through most or all of a ~100-file repo well within its 4s budget; skipping `kickOffCodebaseStats` (also Task 90) didn't move the number since that background pass never got that far in this case, confirmed via a re-measurement in between (still ~104 requests with only that piece fixed). Fixed by skipping the scoring-fallback stage entirely for a remote (GitHub) workspace (`findEntrypoint`, `main.ts`) — falls straight through to `autoLaunchInitialLevel`'s existing "first parsable file in tree order" fallback instead, the same one already used when scanning times out or finds nothing, so this isn't a new code path, just reached proactively instead of after paying for a scan. `workspaceIsDemo` is excluded from this skip even though it also sets `workspaceIsRemote` — the bundled demo's files are already in memory, so scoring costs nothing there, though moot in practice since the demo's own `main.c` always resolves by name first anyway. Also widened `kickOffCodebaseStats`'s existing remote skip from "only repos over 300 files" to "any remote workspace" — DOOM's ~97 files were comfortably under that threshold yet still generated a full round of per-file fetches on their own in the first measurement, disproving the "smaller repos are cheap enough to fetch in full" assumption behind the original cap. Re-measured against the same DOOM repo after all fixes: **99 → 13 requests** (2 metadata + 11 legitimate one-time touches — extensionless-file parsability sniffs plus the one file actually launched — for the now-unavoidable "first parsable file" fallback scan, which only ever runs once thanks to the new `parsableFilesCache` memoization). Verified via `npm run typecheck` (clean), `npm run verify:campaign` (full structural coverage, still passing), and `npm run verify:campaign:playthrough` (real multi-level progression via `advanceToNextLevel`/`findNextParsableFile` — the exact code path the memoization targets — plus Continue Run resume and highscore/replay hash integrity, all passing against a local fixture) [Task 91].

- [x] sometimes rooms without exit are generated, example: src/DockerManager.php of mcdope/wrpdistributor — even the spawn room. Root-caused two independent bugs, both in `src/map/`: (1) `MapGenerator.placeRooms()` (`mapGenerator.ts`) could end a level with exactly 1 room (an empty file, or one entity that's the only one that fits) — `connectRooms()` (`generation/corridors.ts`) only carves a corridor once a *second* room exists, so that sole room got zero corridors, silently. Fixed by topping `placeRooms()` up to at least 2 rooms whenever needed (`placeFillerRoom()`, a new private `MapGenerator` method); the synthetic filler entity uses `kind: "class"` (not `"function"`) so it's never enemy-eligible — avoids leaking a placeholder name like `<filler>` onto an enemy's on-screen nameplate (`sprites.ts` draws `room.entity.name` there) — and the pre-existing 0-entity `centeredRoom()` fallback (`generation/geometry.ts`) got the same `kind: "class"` treatment for the same reason. (2) The actual cause of the original DockerManager.php report: `carveLabyrinth()`'s recursive-division maze (`generation/labyrinth.ts`, triggered for any entity with `nestingDepth >= 2`) kept a single connecting gap per split, but a child split's *own* later wall could land exactly on the cell a parent split's gap depended on to reach it — sealing that whole side of the room off despite every individual split "keeping a gap". Stress-tested in isolation (12,800 room-shape/nesting/seed combinations): ~9% of seeds at the shapes/depths actually seen in real code produced a disconnected maze pre-fix, 0% after. Fixed with a post-carve repair pass (`repairConnectivity()`/`floorComponents()`/`bridgeComponents()` in `labyrinth.ts`): flood-fills the room's floor tiles into connected components and, while more than one exists, bridges the closest pair via a 0-1 BFS shortest-wall-crossing path (deterministic, no extra RNG draws — doesn't perturb replay determinism elsewhere in generation). Also added a cheap dev-time safety net, `assertAllRoomsReachable()` (`generation/pathing.ts`, called at the end of `MapGenerator.generate()`): BFS from spawn (treating every door as opened, same reasoning as `shortestPath`'s doc comment) and `console.error`s a spoiler-free count if any room's center isn't reachable — should never fire again, but makes a future regression loud instead of silent (it's what actually caught bug (2) during this fix, since bug (1) alone didn't explain the original report — DockerManager.php has 20 real entities and was never affected by (1)). Verified: full sweep of every file in a local wrp-distributor checkout (`rooms >= 2` and 100% BFS-reachable, 20/20 files); `npm run typecheck`; `npm run verify:campaign`; `npm run verify:campaign:playthrough` (needed a fixture update — `scripts/fixtures/main.c`'s trivial one-function content now legitimately generates a 2-room level instead of the old buggy 1-room one, so its previously-tiny spawn-to-exit path grew past what the script's non-fighting scripted walker could survive; re-tuned by trying alternate trivial function names until the regenerated (seed-hashed) map's path was short/far-enough again — see the added doc-comment note in that script for why editing the fixture requires re-verifying it).

- [x] replay playback for github worlds not working properly. loading is slow as hell, and replay is buggy — closed by a string of separate fixes across several tasks, each addressing one half of the original report: (1) "Watch Replay" outright failing for a GitHub-sourced entry — it unconditionally called `pickWorkspace()`'s local folder picker, which could never match a recorded run's `owner/repo`-prefixed paths; fixed by a `HighscoreEntry.source?: "github"` flag that routes `startReplay` back through `fetchGithubTree` instead [Task 38 follow-up]. (2) a multi-level replay payload silently vanishing from the Highscores dialog — it could blow the `localStorage` quota, and the whole new entry (not just the oversized replay) was getting dropped; `recordHighscore` now retries without the replay(s) so the score always survives [Task 38 second follow-up]. (3) a stale in-flight GitHub tree fetch (including `startReplay`'s own re-fetch) clobbering a newer load's result; fixed with a load-generation counter plus a real `AbortSignal`-based cancel [Task 89]. (4) the actual "loading is slow as hell" cost — `findEntrypointByScanning`'s unbounded-by-count fallback scan and `kickOffCodebaseStats`'s full-repo aggregation firing on every remote load, plus `advanceToNextLevel` re-walking the whole tree every level clear — cut a real repo (`id-Software/DOOM`, ~97 files) from 99 requests down to 13 [Tasks 90/91]; `startReplay` shares this same `fetchGithubTree`/tree-walk path, so the speedup applies to replay loading too. Not independently re-confirmed against the original reporter's repo/session, but every distinct failure mode the report could plausibly have meant is now individually fixed and verified — reopen with specifics if a GitHub-sourced replay still misbehaves.

- [x] tests (unit [coverage target 100%!]) — the unit half of the broader "tests" backlog item below (e2e/functional/visual coverage and a "no data leaves the app" audit remain open). Added a full Vitest suite (`vitest.config.ts`, a `?url`-as-path Vite plugin so `web-tree-sitter`'s wasm loading works under plain Node, `test/mocks/` for hand-rolled canvas/audio/File-System-Access/rAF doubles) covering every file under `src/` except `defaultHighscore.ts` (a 115k-line generated data literal) and `types.ts` (fully type-only, zero runtime code) — bottom-up through the `fs → parser → map → engine` pipeline, then the browser-API layer, then `engine.ts` and finally `main.ts` (2239 lines of module-top-level DOM orchestration with no exported class, driven via a full DOM/mocking harness and simulated user interaction rather than any new production-code testability seam). 1468 tests / 76 files; `npm run coverage` gates on 100% lines/statements/functions/branches, wired as a blocking CI job (`.github/workflows/verify.yml`) alongside the existing `verify`/`verify-browser` jobs. A handful of lines carry an honest `/* v8 ignore */` with a doc comment explaining why (TypeScript's conservative optional-field typing vs. what production code actually guarantees, e.g. a `?? []` fallback for a field every real call site always populates) — none claim something is unreachable without that reasoning spelled out inline. Along the way, found and fixed a real test-infrastructure bug: `window.__codeensteinTestHooks` (a debug hook `RaycasterEngine`'s constructor sets directly on `window`, gated behind `?testHooks=1`) was never cleared between tests, so a `!!testHooks()` "has this test's own engine loaded yet" check could be satisfied by a completely unrelated earlier engine still sitting on `window` — masking, for a while, whether two replay tests were actually observing the replay under test at all. See `doc/dev/testing.md` for the full setup/conventions writeup [Task 92].

- [x] update README.md to point out how code relates to gameplay — the "Vision" section only listed 4 of the many code→gameplay mappings (folder→level, file→room, function→enemy), while the full set (globals→acid pools, private methods→locked rooms, gotos→teleporters, comments→lore terminals, dead code/etc→secret rooms, headers→bonus levels) was buried further down under "Level Generation" with no cross-link. Added a scannable "What in your code becomes what in the game" table right under Vision covering all of them, linking down to the existing "How It Works" section for the full detail. `doc/dev/architecture.md` already covers the fs→parser→map→engine layering for developers, so left that untouched.

- [x] document the welcome sideeffect that you can describe levels to coding agents to design a level, like we did for the demo campaign or the test fixtures. Combine it with tips for how to manually write levels — added `doc/user/level-design.md`: explains that since a level is generated straight from a file's parsed structure, you can deliberately write (or ask a coding agent to write) a file to produce a specific level, exactly how `demo-campaign/` and `scripts/fixtures/` were authored. Covers both the "describe it to a coding agent" path (with an example prompt, pointing the agent at `demo-campaign/` files as style references, and an explicit warning that RNG-seeded placement means nobody — human or agent — can hand-compute a file's exact output, only load-and-observe) and a manual "which knob do I turn" cheat sheet. Verified every concrete number/claim in the cheat sheet against the actual current source before publishing rather than trusting `notes`/memory, and corrected one along the way: goto/teleporter support isn't just "C and Go" as an earlier `notes` entry (Task 76) said — confirmed via `src/parser/c/`, `src/parser/php/`, and the generic adapter's `vocabulary.ts` that it's actually **C, C++, PHP, and Go**. Also confirmed exact constants (HP = complexity×25, Elite threshold ≥40, >5 params/>3 nesting smell bonuses, the 5 exhaustive secret-trigger kinds, always-reachable key placement, TODO/FIXME's length-gate bypass plus its 3-outcome nearby encounter and 6-terminal-per-file cap, and the 160-tile map cap) against `src/map/`/`src/parser/` directly. Linked from `doc/user/README.md`'s contents list and added a cross-reference bullet in `doc/user/tips.md`; left `doc/dev/game-design.md` (the "why" doc) and `doc/user/mechanics.md` (the full reference table) untouched since this new page is a "how-to" that points at them rather than duplicating them.

- [x] Add credits for utilized packages to README, incl. repo link and license info - kinda a software BOM — added a "Credits & Third-Party Licenses" section to `README.md`, split into "shipped to players" (the one real runtime dependency, `web-tree-sitter`, plus the 14 Tree-sitter language grammar packages compiled to WASM and bundled at build time — all MIT) and "build & test tooling, never shipped" (TypeScript, Vite, esbuild, Vitest/`@vitest/coverage-v8`, Playwright, jsdom, `@types/node`). License/repository values pulled directly from each package's own installed `node_modules/*/package.json` (not guessed/recalled) so the table reflects what's actually pinned in `package.json` right now. Links back to the existing [Dependency Minimalism](decisions.md#dependency-minimalism) doc for why the dependency list is this short in the first place.

- [x] completion status in the readme looks incomplete — the `README.md` "Completion Status" table stopped at Task 86, missing everything from Task 87 through 92 (full CI pipeline, WAD texture coverage extended to lore/hazard/teleporter/trap tiles, GitHub workspace-loading stale-load cancellation + request-count fixes, the 100%-coverage Vitest suite) plus the newer untasked `tsc --noEmit && vite build` CI gate fix. Added rows for all of it, matching how existing multi-task rows group related work.

- [x] github repo loads should ignore folders like "test", "Test", "tests" and so on — `IGNORED_DIRECTORIES` (`src/fs/workspace.ts`) now also skips `test`/`tests`/`__tests__`, and matching is case-insensitive across the board (via a new `isIgnoredDirectoryName` helper) instead of an exact-string `Set.has`, so `Test`/`Tests`/`__Tests__` etc. are caught too. Shared by both the local File System Access walker and the GitHub tree builder, same as the rest of the skip-list. Follow-up: a dedicated test *directory* doesn't cover ecosystems (including this repo's own) that colocate tests right next to the source they cover — added `isIgnoredFileName`, a regex-based filter for `foo.test.ts`/`foo.spec.js`, `test_foo.py`/`foo_test.go`, `foo_spec.rb`, and `FooTest.java`/`FooTests.cs`-style suffixes, applied to individual files in both walkers. The Java/C#-style suffix match is deliberately case-sensitive on the capital "Test" so real words ending in "test" (`Contest.java`, `Latest.php`) aren't caught.

- [x] github workspace loading when loading a replay doesnt show loading indicator — `startReplay` (`src/main.ts`) re-fetches a GitHub-sourced replay's workspace via the exact same `fetchGithubTree` used by the "Load GitHub repo" button, but never showed the loading screen or wired up its byte-progress/file-scan-progress callbacks, so a replay's re-fetch of a large repo just looked like a frozen page. It now calls `showLoadingScreen`/`setLoadingStatus` the same way the other three loaders (local pick, GitHub button, demo campaign) already do — including the "(X received)" byte readout for the GitHub fetch and the "Scanning file tree… (checked/total)" readout for the parsable-file scan — and falls back to `showFileTreePlaceholder()` on a failed re-fetch instead of leaving the loading screen stuck on-screen forever (a gap that only existed because there was no UI shown to reset in the first place).

- [x] already read lore terminals shouldnt glow anymore — a per-level `loreRead` Set (`src/engine/engine.ts`) already tracked which terminals had been interacted with at least once (only for the scoring bonus), but nothing rendering-side ever consulted it: the in-world pulsing tint overlay (`renderScene`) and the corner minimap's pulsing marker (`renderMinimap`, both `src/engine/raycaster.ts`) glowed for every `LORE_TILE`/`loreTerminals` entry unconditionally, forever. Both now take a `readTerminals: ReadonlySet<string>` param (keyed the same `"x,y"` way as `loreRead`) and skip their glow/pulse for an already-read terminal's coordinates; `engine.ts` passes `this.loreRead` at all four `renderScene`/`renderMinimap` call sites. The 3D view keeps sampling the distinct `loreWall` texture even once read (so it's still findable/re-readable) — only the animated overlay stops. Left the Tab automap's flat lore-tile color untouched: it isn't animated/pulsing to begin with, it's a static wayfinding legend color like doors/hazards/traps get, not a "glow".

- [x] keys often spawned near each other, should be split over all rooms reachable without keys — root-caused 2026-07-16, fixed same day: `placeKeys` (`src/map/generation/doorsKeys.ts`) picked each key from the *entire cumulative* reachable-tile set, which only grows across the loop, so the (usually largest) initial public area near spawn stayed a valid candidate for every later key and dominated the random pick. Now each key is confined to the tiles newly reached since the previous door opened (`newlyReachable`, a diff against the prior iteration's reachable set), falling back to the full reachable set only if that diff has no usable tile left (e.g. a door looping back into already-explored floor). Verified with two new tests: one confirming later keys never land back in the pre-any-door region, one confirming the fallback still places a key instead of dropping it; `doorsKeys.ts` stays at 100% coverage. Playtested and confirmed working by the user.

- [x] add _WEAPON_INDEX consts for all non-melee weapons — `weapons.ts` had named consts for gdb/ghidra/Friday Hotfix (`*_WEAPON_INDEX`) plus Toolchain, but the two starting ranged weapons — pistol (index 0) and shotgun (index 1) — were still raw literals (`WEAPONS[0]` in `ammo.ts`'s `startingBullets()`, plus `0`/`1` mixed into `weaponPriority` arrays in `scripts/run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`). Added `PISTOL_WEAPON_INDEX = 0` and `SHOTGUN_WEAPON_INDEX = 1`; also added `KNIFE_WEAPON_INDEX = 2` (melee, technically outside the item's literal wording, but the mjs script already had its own local copy of that one, and it was the sole remaining un-named index once 0/1 were filled in). `STARTING_WEAPONS` in both `weapons.ts` and the mjs mirror now build from the named consts instead of `[0, 1, 2]`. These are pure `WEAPONS` array-index identities (same as `ownedWeapons`/`weaponIndex`/saves/replays use) — unrelated to number-key bindings, which stay on the separate derived `NUMBER_KEY_WEAPONS` mapping. Verified with `tsc --noEmit`, the full Vitest suite (1515 tests, all passing), and `node --check` on the mjs script.

- [x] default highscore replay runs faster than normal gameplay — root-caused 2026-07-16, fixed same day. `scripts/generate-default-highscore.mjs` recorded every `ReplayFrame` as a 50ms `VIRTUAL_STEP_MS`-sized virtual-time chunk, vs. a real live session's ~16.7ms-per-frame (60fps); `src/main.ts`'s replay `step()` consumes exactly one recorded frame per real render tick regardless of that frame's own `dt`, so a recording with ~3x-fewer-but-3x-longer frames played back in ~1/3 the real time a real playthrough of equivalent simulated length would take. Fixed by decoupling the bot's own decision cadence (`VIRTUAL_STEP_MS`, kept at 50ms — unchanged real Playwright round-trip count, unchanged `MAX_TICKS_PER_WAYPOINT`/`FINAL_APPROACH_TICKS`/`DOOR_OPEN_TICKS` virtual-time budgets) from the recorded frame's own granularity (new `RECORD_STEP_MS = 1000/60`, matching a real 60fps frame): `applyAction`'s `window.__pumpVirtualTime(stepMs, recordStepMs)` now pumps the same 50ms of bot-decision time in 3 real-frame-sized sub-steps, recording 3 `ReplayFrame`s per bot decision instead of 1 coarse one — same generation-time cost, correctly-paced output. `dismissOverlay`'s two hardcoded `pumpVirtualTime(_, 50)` calls got the same `recordStepMs` treatment for consistency (those frames get recorded too). Also added run-level concurrency (`RUN_CONCURRENCY`, default 4, `CODEENSTEIN_HIGHSCORE_CONCURRENCY` env override) to `main()`'s `RUN_COUNT` loop — extracted the per-run body into `runOnePlaythrough()` and batch it via `Promise.all`, the same pattern `run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`'s `ATTEMPT_CONCURRENCY` already uses; safe since every run already got its own fresh, isolated browser context and only reads the shared, never-mutated `levelPlans`. Regenerated `src/engine/defaultHighscore.ts` (`npm run generate:default-highscore`) and verified numerically — summed recorded `dt` per level segment now exactly matches `frameCount × (1000/60)` (ratio 1.000 for every segment, vs. the old file's exact 3.000) — and visually via a live Playwright "Watch Replay" spot-check against the real dev server (implied movement speed ~2 tiles/sec, well within `MOVE_SPEED = 3.2`'s normal range, nowhere near the ~3x-too-fast speed the bug would have produced).

- [x] end of replay playback should drop back in the highscore dialog — `startReplay` (`src/main.ts`) only ever launches from the Highscores dialog's "Watch" button, but every termination path (natural win, natural death, Escape-to-stop, or any load/verify error) called the same `teardown()`, which unconditionally resets to the plain file-tree placeholder — never back to the dialog. Extracted the dialog's open logic (previously inlined in `viewHighscoresButton`'s click handler) into a standalone `openHighscoreDialog()`, and added `returnToHighscores = () => { teardown(); void openHighscoreDialog(); }` inside `startReplay`, wired into the three "genuinely done watching" overlays: `hud.showKernelPanic`, `hud.showBuildSuccessful`, and `hud.showReplayEnded` (covering Escape and every load/verify error, which all funnel through `endReplay`). Deliberately left `stopActiveReplay = teardown` (the supersession guard used when the user picks a real file or a *different* replay mid-viewing) untouched — those are the user explicitly navigating away, not "done watching," so popping the dialog back open there would fight whatever they just launched. `openHighscoreDialog()` genuinely re-reads the board (`loadHighscoresForDisplay()`) rather than reusing stale data, since nothing caches `HighscoreEntry[]` at module scope. Verified via the existing Vitest replay-playback suite (all win/death/error-path tests passing unchanged) plus a live Playwright spot-check against the real dev server: started a replay, pressed Escape, dismissed the "REPLAY ENDED" overlay, and confirmed `#highscore-dialog` reopened with the same three entries rendered.

- [x] export replays as video or gif — shipped video only (webm), GIF dropped per the investigation's own recommendation (no native browser encoder, would need a new dependency for a strictly worse result on this content). `canvas.captureStream()` → `MediaRecorder`, no new dependency: a Record button (⏺/⏹) added to the replay transport bar (`buildReplayControls`, `src/main.ts`) and an "Export" button added next to each Highscores-dialog entry's "Watch" (`HighscoreTableOptions.onExportReplay`, `src/ui/highscorePanel.ts`). New shared `downloadBlob(blob, filename)` helper (`src/ui/download.ts`) — the `createObjectURL`/`<a download>`/`revokeObjectURL` pattern both this item and the sibling top-down-PNG-export item flagged as a shared gap; that item can now reuse it. Design decisions made with the user during planning: **video only**, no GIF; **force 1x playback while recording** (speed/seek/pause all lock for the duration — a `MediaRecorder` captures in real wall-clock time, so any other speed would desync the video from what it's supposed to show); **an explanatory notice (`GameHud.showRecordingNotice`, reusing the existing canvas-overlay mechanism) only gates the Highscores dialog's "Export" button**, not the transport bar's own Record button — Export jumps straight from the dialog into a recording with no replay UI seen yet, the one path where a user could plausibly wonder what's happening, whereas a manual Record click happens with the user already looking at the replay. The notice isn't just shown-then-recording-starts-anyway: `startReplay`'s own `advanceLevel()` call (the sole trigger for the replay's playback loop starting at all) is gated *behind* the notice's acknowledgement for the `autoRecord` path, so it's structurally impossible for the replay to end before the user has seen the notice and recording has actually started — nothing is playing yet for it to end. Recording auto-flushes/downloads on `teardown()` (any of the three "genuinely done watching" paths), so stopping early or letting the replay end never loses a capture. Feature-detected (`typeof MediaRecorder !== "undefined" && typeof canvas.captureStream === "function"`) — the Record button hides itself and Export silently falls back to plain Watch behavior on an unsupported browser, rather than erroring. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; full Vitest suite (1519 tests) at 100% line/statement/function/branch coverage, including 5 new tests covering the manual-Record flow, the notice-gated auto-record flow (asserting nothing is loaded while the notice is up), the unsupported-browser fallback, and the mime-type-fallback branch; live Playwright spot-checks against the real dev server for both entry points — manual Record produced a real, non-empty, correctly-named `.webm` download, and Export's notice-then-record flow was confirmed screenshot-by-screenshot (blank canvas + notice with nothing loaded, then live gameplay + a red "recording" Record button once dismissed). Follow-up (user-reported): "Watch"/"Export" shared one `<td>` in the Highscores table with no gap between them, reading as one squashed control — fixed with `.highscore-table td .replay-btn + .replay-btn { margin-left: 6px; }`, matching `.replay-controls`' own 6px button gap.

- [x] explain the game for "non-coders" on the startpage — the startpage (`#intro-screen`, `index.html`, static markup shown once and permanently removed on any launch) already avoided the deepest jargon (no spelled-out "AST"/"cyclomatic complexity"/"tree-sitter" — those stay in `doc/user/*.md`), but still leaned on unexplained programmer vocabulary right alongside the core pitch: entrypoint, private/protected method, goto/label, parsable file. Added a new first-position, full-width "New to coding?" card (reusing the existing `.intro-card.intro-card--full` classes, no new CSS) ahead of "How it works," giving a genuine concrete next step — "click **Demos** in the sidebar for a ready-made example with no setup" — plus one jargon-free analogy ("every function becomes a monster to fight, and reaching the end of the file clears the level"), framing the technical mapping table below as optional detail rather than required reading. Left the mapping table itself untouched — its arrow format is already a fun, self-explanatory hook for the audience that does code, and rewriting it further would blunt that pitch rather than help non-coders, who now get an explicit off-ramp before reaching it. Also swapped two jargon terms in "What you'll do" (action instructions, not flavor, so these should read cleanly regardless of audience): "entrypoint" → "start", "parsable file" → "file it can read." Verified: full Vitest suite (1519 tests) unaffected (no test asserts intro-screen content; `test/mocks/mainDom.ts`'s DOM fixture reproduces `#intro-screen` as an empty stand-in); live Playwright check against the real dev server confirmed card order (`New to coding?` → `How it works` → `What you'll do` → `Privacy`), that the new card is full-width and appears first, and that its "Demos" mention matches the real sidebar tab label exactly.

- [x] export generated level as top-down-view png — shipped as a **visually accurate top-down render**, not the flat-color diagram an earlier draft of this plan proposed (user correction): new `src/map/exportView.ts`'s `renderExportMap(map, textureSet)` stamps the *actual* wall/door/floor/hazard/teleporter/spike/lore-terminal `TextureBitmap`s (`src/engine/textures.ts`'s `textures.getActiveSet()` — procedural defaults, or a loaded WAD's real textures, whichever the session is actually using) via `ctx.drawImage(bitmap.canvas, ...)` per tile, mirroring `raycaster.ts`'s own tile→texture selection exactly so the export matches what the player actually saw in first-person. Small spawn (red) and exit (green) dot markers on top, no kill markers, no title banner baked into the image (the filename carries level/campaign identity instead) — deliberately dropped from an earlier richer draft per the "visually accurate ... just top-down" correction. `debugView.ts` (the map-generator's own flat black/white diagnostic tool) is untouched, a separate module for a separate audience. Button: an "Export Map as PNG" `<button class="settings-btn">` appended into `#viewport` inside `launchLevel`'s `onWin` (`main.ts`) — resolves both open questions the investigation left: **no new "has this level been won" state needed anywhere** (`launchLevel` already clears every non-canvas `#viewport` child at the start of every level load, the same way `resetToFileTree`/`startReplay` already do, so the button is destroyed automatically the instant the player advances, dies later, or leaves — strictly simpler than either the sidebar-button or `GameHud`-overlay-hit-testing options the note's own investigation had been weighing), and **the map/textures are already in scope with zero plumbing** (`onWin`'s closure captures the same `map` object the just-finished engine mutated in place). Reuses `downloadBlob` (`src/ui/download.ts`, built for the video-export item above) and the existing `sanitizeFilenamePart` helper for the filename. `onGameOver` gets no such button — death never exports. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; full Vitest suite (1528 tests) at 100% coverage, including a new `exportView.test.ts` (tile→texture mapping, bonus-level variants, still-locked doors, unopened-vs-opened secret walls, markers) and new `main.test.ts` cases (button appears only after a live win, not on game-over; a null-`toBlob`-result no-ops cleanly; the button disappears once `resetToFileTree` clears the viewport) — needed a new `stubCanvasToBlob()` test mock (`test/mocks/canvas.ts`), since jsdom's `canvas.toBlob` is unimplemented and never invokes its callback at all. Live Playwright spot-check: god-mode-walked the bundled Demo Campaign's first level to a real win, clicked the button, and confirmed a real ~250KB PNG downloaded showing a recognizable textured top-down floor plan (dark floor texture, tan/brick walls, purple teleporter pads, red spawn/green exit dots) — genuinely looks like an accurate overhead view of the level just played, not a debug grid.

Follow-up (2026-07-16, user-reported, same day): two problems with that first ship. **(a) Wasted wall space** — `MapGenerator` allocates a full `size × size` grid of solid wall (`mapGenerator.ts:109-110`) and carves rooms/corridors out of it; confirmed this is real engine data, not a rendering artifact, but the exported PNG was painting *all* of it, producing a huge tan rectangle with the actual maze occupying a small corner. Fixed entirely within `exportView.ts` (no engine/`mapGenerator.ts` changes): a wall tile only draws if at least one of its 8 neighbors is real content (floor/hazard/door/teleporter/spike/lore/opened-secret) — giving exactly a 1-tile wall border ring around actual rooms — everything else is skipped and stays transparent (the canvas's own default). The output canvas is also cropped to the tight bounding box of everything actually drawn (content + its border ring, no extra padding layered on top — that would have just doubled the margin), instead of sizing to the full generated grid. **(b) Button discoverability** — user reported it took ~2 minutes to find. Root-caused, not guessed: `#viewport` (`style.css`) is a vertically-centered flex column with `overflow: hidden` — clips, doesn't scroll — and the button was `appendChild`'d *last*, after `hint` and the fairly tall keybinding-legend grid, so it risked being exactly what got clipped, and even when visible used `.settings-btn` (width:100%, deliberately muted, built for the sidebar's vertical stack) so it barely stood out. Fixed by `viewport.insertBefore(button, hint)` (now the first thing below the canvas, right under the win overlay) plus a new dedicated `.export-map-btn` class styled like this app's other primary CTAs (`#select-workspace` et al.: accent-colored, bold, sized to content) with an 🖼️ icon prefix. **(c) User asked whether the wasted-wall-space problem also costs real engine performance, not just export cosmetics** — investigated and confirmed a real, plausible (not yet profiled) mechanism: `PathField.ensure()` (`pathField.ts:36-50`, the shared player-rooted BFS distance field every chasing enemy steers by) allocates `Int32Array(map.width*map.height)` once per level but calls `this.dist.fill(-1)` on *every reflood* (routine during normal movement, not just once per level) — an O(width×height) cost that scales with the entire grid including all the never-visited filler (the BFS traversal itself stays cheap regardless, since wall-gated expansion never enters solid space). `automap.ts` is unaffected (already windowed to a viewport-sized region). Logged as its own separate `notes` item below rather than fixed now — more invasive (touches map generation + `PathField` + any other `map.width`/`height` consumer), needs its own profiling pass first. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; full Vitest suite (1530 tests) at 100% coverage, including 2 new `exportView.test.ts` cases (crops to content+border, skips deep filler even *within* the cropped box using a two-separate-rooms-with-a-gap fixture) and updated `main.test.ts` assertions for the new button text; live Playwright spot-check confirmed the button sits comfortably within the viewport (not clipped) immediately below the canvas, and the re-exported PNG is now a tightly-cropped image with a thin wall border and a transparent background instead of a mostly-solid-wall rectangle.

- [x] investigate whether PathField's full-grid reset actually costs real performance (follow-up to (c) above, 2026-07-16, same day) — **measured, ruled out, no fix needed.** `MapGenerator`'s grid is clamped to `[minSize, maxSize] = [64, 160]` (`mapGenerator.ts:60-61`), so the worst possible grid is 160×160 = 25,600 tiles. Benchmarked the two costs that actually scale with grid size (Node `process.hrtime.bigint()`, V8 — the same engine Chrome uses): the recurring per-reflood `dist.fill(-1)` cost averaged **~0.0015ms per call** at the worst-case 160×160 size (1000 calls totaled 1.484ms) — reflooding only happens on real tile-crossings (roughly a few times/sec during normal movement, not every frame), so even 100 refloods/sec would total ~0.15ms/sec against a 16.67ms *per-frame* budget at 60fps; the one-time per-level array allocation averaged **~0.06ms** at the same worst-case size (100 allocations totaled 6.03ms), paid once per level load, not per-frame. Also confirmed no other cost scales with total grid size: `PathField.distAt()` (the per-enemy lookup) is an O(1) typed-array read, the BFS flood itself only ever visits reachable tiles (wall-gated expansion never enters solid filler, so it was never proportional to filler size to begin with — only the `fill(-1)` reset was), and `automap.ts` is already windowed to a viewport-sized region, not the full grid. The theoretical O(width×height) scaling flagged in the note above was correct, but the constant factor (native `TypedArray.fill`/allocation, both essentially memory-bandwidth-bound memset operations) is small enough that even this game's largest possible grid never turns it into a real bottleneck — cropping `MapGenerator`'s grid to content bounds (the fix speculated about above) would not produce a measurable improvement, and isn't worth the real invasiveness it'd add (touches map generation, `PathField`, and every other `map.width`/`height` consumer's coordinate space) for a cost that isn't actually there. No source changes made — this is purely a measured verdict. Doesn't affect the level-export PNG's own crop/transparency fix above, which was a real visual problem regardless of runtime cost.

- [x] generalize telemetry bot into a reusable bot class, use it also for highscore defaults creation (and later multiplayer bots maybe). highscore creation should run all skill levels until each one has 3 runs reaching level 4 for casual, level 5 for gamer and level 6 for pro, keep the best one for each — shipped 2026-07-17, per plan `~/.claude/plans/generalize-telemetry-bot-into-kind-popcorn.md`. `scripts/run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`'s ~1450 lines of navigation/combat/loot decision-making extracted into a `Bot` class (`scripts/lib/bot.mjs`, config now explicit constructor opts instead of module-level `process.env` consts read once at import); `scripts/lib/qualifyLoop.mjs` extracted the generic retry-until-N-qualifying-runs pattern shared by both the telemetry harness and the highscore generator; `scripts/lib/virtualClock.mjs` deduped a previously byte-for-byte-duplicated `installVirtualClock`. `scripts/generate-default-highscore.mjs` rewritten on top of these exactly as specified: plays each of the 3 skill profiles (Casual/Gamer/Pro) until 3 runs qualify (clears level 4/5/6 respectively — `QUALIFY_LEVEL_INDEX_BY_PROFILE`), keeps the single best-scoring qualifying run per profile (3 entries total), replacing the old profile-blind "10 generic runs, keep top 3" approach. No `HighscoreEntry` schema change — the 3 entries aren't tagged with which profile produced them.
  - **Verification surfaced a real, unrelated bug**: the smarter/deeper-surviving bot made the regenerated `defaultHighscore.ts` balloon to **84MB** (3 entries × 14+ levels of recorded replay frames each) — this is what actually crashed a prior session mid-verification (timed out `highscores.test.ts`'s 30s limit and OOM'd the full Vitest suite; parsing an 84MB array-of-objects literal into an AST is the expensive part, not decoding the data itself). Fixed by shipping the entries gzip+base64-encoded (`gz1:` prefix, reusing `storageCompression.ts`'s existing localStorage-compression scheme) instead of a plain array literal — 84MB → 618KB (136x), verified byte-identical after decompression and working end-to-end (Highscores dialog renders, Watch Replay plays back cleanly) in a real browser. Found and fixed a second latent bug while there: `storageCompression.ts`'s `gzip`/`gunzip` used `new Blob([bytes]).stream()`, which jsdom's `Blob` shim doesn't implement (every real browser does) — meant the real compression path had never actually been exercised by any test (`compressForStorage`'s try/catch silently fell back to plain JSON). Replaced with a small `ReadableStream`-based helper, which works identically under jsdom and in real browsers.
  - **Verified**: full suite (1543/1543), `tsc --noEmit`, and coverage (100% on every touched file) all clean. `npm run balancing:scan` post-refactor: 353 anomalies, 0 nav-warns, max stall 63 ticks — within this project's established normal range, no navigation/combat regression from the extraction. `run-balancing-campaign.mjs` and `watch-bot-sessions.mjs` (both untouched consumers of the refactored exports) confirmed still working.

- [x] highscore bot presses fire key so fast, the pistol becomes a smg lmao. it should be human like, depending on skill level — fixed 2026-07-17, same day as the `Bot` class extraction above. Root cause, confirmed by reading the engine source: semi-auto ranged weapons (pistol/shotgun/ghidra) have no `fireIntervalSec` cooldown at all (`updateFiring` in `engine.ts` — "the pistol/shotgun have none, so they're... fire exactly as fast as the player can press/click") — `Bot#applyAction` dispatches a fresh `Backquote` keydown every single decision tick the bot decides to fire, and since `consumeFire()` is edge-triggered per keydown with no `e.repeat` involved (synthetic events never set it), a target locked on for many consecutive ticks fired once per tick: ~20/sec at the headless 50ms tick rate, ~7.7/sec even in headed real-time mode — both far beyond any human trigger-pull rate. Auto weapons (gdb/Friday Hotfix) were never affected — their own engine-side `fireIntervalSec` cooldown already paces them realistically while the key is held.
  - **Fix**: new `profile.fireCooldownMs` (Casual 220ms/~4.5 shots-per-sec, Gamer 160ms/~6.25, Pro 120ms/~8.3 — all plausible real semi-auto trigger-pull rates, tightest→loosest matching the existing `fireAngleEps`/`rotSpeedMultiplier` skill ladder) in `run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`'s `PROFILES`. `Bot` (`bot.mjs`) tracks cumulative simulated time (`this.simTimeMs`, incremented once per `applyAction` call by that call's real `stepMs`) and the sim-time of its last semi-auto shot; the ranged-fire decision branch now gates `fire` on `simTimeMs - lastFireSimTimeMs >= profile.fireCooldownMs` for any weapon *not* in the new `AUTO_RANGED_WEAPON_INDICES` set (gdb/Friday Hotfix stay unthrottled, correctly).
  - **Verified via direct trace, not assumed**: a `CODEENSTEIN_TELEMETRY_DEBUG_NAV=1` trace (Gamer/normal) measured the tick-gap between consecutive genuine semi-auto ranged fires (filtering out melee swings, which go through a completely separate `Space`-key/`meleeCooldown` path already correctly rate-limited) — dominant clustering at 4-5 ticks (200-250ms), matching Gamer's 160ms cooldown rounded up to tick granularity; the one apparent 2-tick outlier turned out to be a benign weapon-switch-mid-fight tracing artifact (an unthrottled auto-weapon shot correctly exempted from the cooldown), not a real violation.
  - **A real regression found and fixed in the same pass, not shipped blind**: the first `balancing:scan` after this change came back with anomaly count 473 (vs. the established 331-390 range) and max stall 179 ticks (vs. 42-91) — `detectAnomalies`'s `mostlyFiring` exclusion (a stationary run is only flagged as a stall if *not* mostly-firing, since holding ground and shooting is correct combat behavior) relied on `fire: true` covering most ticks of a real firefight, which was true when the bot fired every tick but is no longer true now that most ticks are spent waiting out a human-paced cooldown instead. Fixed by adding a new `fireOnCooldown` trace field (true when the bot was aimed/ready/willing to fire but held back purely by the cooldown, not by e.g. rocket-safety) and broadening the exclusion to `fire || fireOnCooldown` — deliberately narrow (only this specific new waiting state is exempted, not "any threat present," which would have risked masking the class of genuine stuck-in-combat bugs found earlier this project's history). Re-scan after the detector fix: anomaly count 366, max stall 62 ticks, both back within the established normal range; 3/3 qualifying in 5 attempts for all 3 profiles, unchanged from before this whole fix.
  - No `src/` changes — bot-script-only (`scripts/lib/bot.mjs`, `scripts/run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`), Vitest suite unaffected.

- [x] support other browsers as good as possible (ignore the non available FS api) — investigated (2026-07-16, no code changed yet). **Scope note (user, 2026-07-16): desktop only, mobile is explicitly not a target** — this game asks for a proper screen and proper input devices (mouse/keyboard/gamepad), so this is about desktop Firefox/Safari/other-Chromium-derivatives, not phones/tablets. Drops any mobile-specific platform quirks (e.g. iOS Safari's fullscreen limitations) out of scope entirely — not worth tracking here.
  - **File System Access API: already fully handled, nothing to do here** — `src/fs/workspace.ts`'s `isFileSystemAccessSupported()` feature-detects `window.showDirectoryPicker`, and `main.ts` already disables the "Select Workspace"/"Continue Run" buttons with a clear "Use Chrome, Edge, or Brave" message when it's missing, rather than letting a click throw. Matches the note's own "ignore the non available FS api" instruction exactly — confirming this part is already done, not a gap.
  - **Gamepad and Web Audio already have real fallback handling**: `gameHud.ts`'s gamepad poll checks `typeof navigator.getGamepads === "function"` before calling it; `audio.ts`'s `AudioContext ?? webkitAudioContext ?? null` already covers older Safari's vendor-prefixed name. Neither needs further work.
  - **Pointer Lock and Fullscreen: unprefixed, no fallback, likely fine on desktop.** `input.ts`'s `canvas.requestPointerLock()` and `document.exitFullscreen()`/`canvas.requestFullscreen()` are called with no vendor-prefixed fallback (no `webkitRequestPointerLock`/`webkitRequestFullscreen`). Both APIs' unprefixed forms have been supported in desktop Safari for years (Pointer Lock since 12.1/2019, Fullscreen even earlier) — on a desktop-only scope, this reads as a low-risk non-issue rather than something needing active work, unlike a mobile scope where Fullscreen specifically would have been a real structural gap.
  - **The real structural gap: zero automated cross-browser verification exists anywhere in this project.** Every Playwright-driven script — `scripts/verify-campaign-playthrough.mjs`, `scripts/run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`, `scripts/watch-bot-sessions.mjs`, `scripts/generate-default-highscore.mjs`, `scripts/verify-wad-textures.mjs` — hardcodes `chromium.launch()`. Firefox/WebKit compatibility is currently completely unverified, and any regression there would be entirely invisible to this project's existing testing discipline (same class of blind spot as the "CI green doesn't mean tsc ran" lesson from earlier this session — a passing check isn't evidence for something it never actually exercised). Playwright already bundles Firefox and WebKit engines (no new dependency needed) — swapping `chromium.launch()` for `firefox.launch()`/`webkit.launch()` in even one existing verification script would be a cheap first real signal of what, if anything, currently breaks, before investing in fixing anything speculatively. This is the one real, actionable next step here.
  - **Firefox verification shipped 2026-07-17** (picked up as low-hanging-fruit item #2, after the bot fire-rate fix): new `scripts/lib/browserEngine.mjs` reads `CODEENSTEIN_VERIFY_BROWSER` (chromium/firefox/webkit, default chromium) instead of every script hardcoding `chromium.launch()`. `verify-campaign-playthrough.mjs` (OPFS-stubbed workspace, never touches the real picker) and `verify-wad-textures.mjs` (bundled demo campaign via `fetch` + WAD via a plain `<input type="file">`, no FS API at all) were the two candidates identified above — both wired up and re-run against real Firefox: every check passes, including `verify-wad-textures.mjs`'s strict "zero console/page errors across the whole run" assertion, and visual screenshots confirm correct rendering (procedural textures, a loaded synthetic WAD's textures, HUD/minimap all correct). Wired into CI as a blocking matrix (`verify-browser` job, `.github/workflows/verify.yml`, `browser: [chromium, firefox]`) — user's explicit call, weighing recurring CI cost against real coverage. One real Firefox-only quirk found and documented (not a bug): `page.reload()` fires `addInitScript` twice, once for a transient intermediate document (`location.href === ""`, `navigator.storage` genuinely undefined) that gets discarded before the script ever interacts with it — confirmed directly by instrumenting the init script, logged-not-asserted in the script with a doc comment explaining it.
  - **WebKit unblocked and shipped, same day**: user ran `sudo npx playwright install-deps` (`libwoff1` was the missing piece) — `webkit.launch()` now works. `verify-wad-textures.mjs` runs clean against it (all checks pass, zero errors — it never touches the File System Access API at all). `verify-campaign-playthrough.mjs` genuinely can't, though: confirmed directly (`typeof navigator.storage === "undefined"` on a plain page load) that Playwright's own WebKit build has no `navigator.storage`/OPFS support at all — a gap in that specific build, not a real Safari limitation (real desktop Safari has supported OPFS since 15.2). CI matrix is now `[chromium, firefox, webkit]`, with `verify:campaign:playthrough`'s step conditioned on `matrix.browser != 'webkit'` so it only runs where it can actually work. Confirmed WebKit is still a real, live, worth-testing target despite most other historically-WebKit browsers (old Opera, old Chrome) having forked to Blink over a decade ago — desktop Safari is exactly the platform this scope note's own "Firefox/Safari/other-Chromium-derivatives" framing already called out.
  - **A real user bug report arrived mid-investigation (2026-07-17, same day), root-caused and resolved (not a code bug)**: "Firefox shows empty canvas till focussed" — happens after both a page reload *and* a same-session restart-after-death (no reload involved), fixed by clicking into the canvas. Extensive automated repro attempts (headed Firefox + Chromium via Playwright, blur/focus instrumentation, pixel-content canvas checks, OPFS/reload-quirk investigation) found nothing — every automated case rendered correctly in well under a second. User provided two screen recordings; ffmpeg contact-sheet analysis (this project's established video-analysis technique) of the first showed the level-start briefing overlay appearing quickly, then the canvas going genuinely blank (sidebar/console still visible, no spinner, no "PAUSED" text) for ~4.5s before gameplay suddenly appeared — a real, reproducible-on-their-end symptom automation couldn't catch.
    - **Root-caused with hard data**: added temporary `[timing-probe]` console.log instrumentation (`performance.now()` timestamps at `GameHud.show()`, `dismiss()`, the level-start `onAck` callback, `engine.start()`, and the first 20 `frame()` callbacks incl. `document.visibilityState`/`hasFocus()`) — this project's console sidebar mirrors `console.log` verbatim, so it showed up directly in the user's next recording without needing DevTools. A third recording's console output was conclusive: `drawOverlay returned t=6660.0` → `dismiss() invoked t=12406.0` — the entire ~5.75s gap sits *before* `dismiss()` is even invoked; every step after it (`onAck` → `engine.start()` → first `requestAnimationFrame` → first rendered frame) completes in under 5ms, every time. The whole engine/render pipeline this session's earlier investigation focused on was never the problem.
    - **Real mechanism, confirmed via the user's own testing + their GNOME settings**: the user confirmed their *first* click/keypress does nothing but "activate the canvas" (i.e., refocus the browser window at the OS level) — only their second interaction actually reaches the page. `gsettings get org.gnome.desktop.wm.preferences focus-mode` = `'click'`, GNOME's own standard default, not a misconfiguration. This is a genuine desktop/browser window-focus interaction — `window.addEventListener("keydown", ...)` (what `GameHud.show()`'s dismiss listener uses) categorically cannot receive events while the OS window itself lacks real keyboard focus, and no web page can force real OS focus or detect "I don't have it yet" before the fact. Not fixable at the app-code level; not Codeenstein-specific either, would affect any page expecting immediate keyboard/click input right after a window-focus-changing action (alt-tab, reload via keyboard shortcut, etc.) under this exact desktop-environment behavior.
    - No code changes — the temporary `[timing-probe]` instrumentation (`src/engine/engine.ts`, `src/main.ts`, `src/ui/gameHud.ts`) was fully removed once root-caused, verified via `git restore` + a clean `tsc --noEmit`.
    - Documented for players too, per user request: `doc/user/getting-started.md`'s "The first level" section now explains the "briefing seems unresponsive" symptom inline (try clicking/pressing Start again) right where a player would actually hit it.

- [x] ensure all keys are placed so they can be found before needed, i have the feeling that it is not always the case — investigated 2026-07-17, real gap found and closed. `placeKeys` (`doorsKeys.ts`) already guarantees each key it *does* place sits reachable before its own door, but has one accepted fallback (its own doc comment: "skips placing a key... when every reachable tile is already used") that can leave a door with **no matching key placed anywhere** — a room-solvability gap, not just a findability one. The function meant to catch exactly this, `assertAllRoomsReachable` (`pathing.ts`, called at the very end of `MapGenerator.generate` after every other system has placed its tiles), turned out to not actually check it: its own doc comment assumed "generation guarantees every door is eventually openable" and treated every door as pre-opened regardless of key state, by design — an unverified assumption standing in for a real check.
  - **Empirically, real levels are fine**: swept 613 real, structurally-diverse third-party files across 11 supported languages (this repo's own `src/`, a broad `node_modules` sample, and a large multi-language sample pulled from `~/sources` — C, PHP, Python, Ruby, Go, Objective-C, Scala, TS/TSX, JS, Java, C header files), 105 of which generated at least one locked door. Zero had `keys.length < doors.length`; zero were unsolvable under a real key-consumption simulation (collect any reachable key, spend one to open a reachable locked door, repeat — the same model `RaycasterEngine.openDoorAhead`/`keysHeld` actually uses, shared-pool, any key opens any door). The theoretical gap is real but evidently rare enough to never have manifested in this data.
  - **Fixed anyway, since "evidently rare" isn't "structurally impossible"**: rewrote `assertAllRoomsReachable` to run the same real key-gated reachability simulation used in the sweep above (new `keys` parameter), instead of unconditionally treating every door as passable. Converts a previously-silent assumption into an actively-enforced, permanent dev-time safety net — exactly the job this function already existed for, just implemented against the wrong model. Zero generation-behavior change (purely a stricter verification pass); if a future change to `placeKeys`/`placeSecretRooms`/`placeTraps` ever does strand a room behind a keyless door on a real file, this now logs it loudly (spoiler-free, no coordinates, matching the function's existing convention) instead of silently shipping it.
  - **Verified**: existing `pathing.test.ts` coverage updated (the old "counts an opened door as passable" test unconditionally trusted a locked door — rewritten to require a real, correctly-reachable key, matching what `placeKeys` actually guarantees) plus 2 new tests: one reproducing `placeKeys`' exact accepted-fallback scenario and confirming it's now correctly flagged as an error, one confirming a legitimate multi-door key-in-sequence chain (open door A using a key reachable at spawn, discover key B only after door A opens, use it on door B) is still correctly accepted. Full suite (1545/1545, +2 net new tests), `tsc --noEmit`, and coverage (100% on every touched file, including `pathing.ts`) all clean. The existing `mapGenerator.test.ts` regression test ("never logs an unreachable-room warning for any bundled demo-campaign file") now exercises the *real* key-gated check on all 17 bundled levels, not the old unconditional one, and still passes clean.

- [x] selectable wads to be loaded via internet — shipped 2026-07-17 as a curated "Or pick an online texture pack" list in the sidebar, next to the existing local-file WAD picker. Architecture ended up different from the user's original ask in one key way (a correction mid-build): a direct browser `fetch()` of any of the drafted sources is impossible (checked directly — GitHub Release assets, `codeload.github.com`, and every small Doom-community host send no usable CORS headers), and the user's own follow-up call was to fetch+extract at **build time** instead (`scripts/fetch-online-wads.mjs`, run via `predev`/`prebuild` npm hooks, idempotent) into `public/wads/`, served same-origin at runtime — not committed to the repo (a first pass committed the ~170MB of extracted WAD bytes as static assets; corrected to gitignored + re-fetched per machine/CI run instead). Needed a hand-rolled ZIP reader (`scripts/lib/zipReader.mjs`, same dependency-minimalism reasoning as the WAD parser itself — see decisions.md) since Node has no built-in ZIP support.
  - **Every candidate from the user's original draft JSON was independently re-verified, not trusted** — and most didn't survive: Blasphemer's drafted repo (`viti95/Blasphemer`) is a stale fork, the real one is `Blasphemer/blasphemer`, but even fixed it's a **Heretic-engine** WAD with completely different lump names — round-tripped through this project's own `loadWadTextures`, it matched **0/10** texture slots. OTEX's drafted "CC BY-SA 4.0" license was wrong (its real license is non-commercial-use-only, confirmed by downloading and reading its own bundled `.txt`); it also ships with no `PLAYPAL` lump of its own (a texture-only resource pack meant to ride alongside a real IWAD in an actual source port) and even after a donor-palette splice hack only matched 4/10 slots. Zauberer/RETRES/32in24-15/NB_RECOL all had dead or unverifiable URLs.
