Fixed a real, previously-dormant bug as a side effect: `PlayerState.priorPlayerStats` was building from the one shared telemetry instance, so a multiplayer run with `PLAYER_STATS_ENABLED` on would have mixed every player's stats into one bucket. **2b**: new `src/multiplayer/connectionStats.ts` (first `RTCPeerConnection.getStats()` usage anywhere in `src/`) — `getConnectionStats(id)` (real RTT), `getMissedTickStats()` (cumulative `heldInputFallback` tally), `getReconciliationCorrections()` (guest-only — the host is authoritative and never applies a snapshot to itself; corrections only count once past a small noise floor so ordinary cross-peer float drift doesn't register as a false positive). The merge commit itself closed the one seam neither fork owned by design (to stay disjoint on `main.ts`): wiring `getMultiplayerTelemetrySnapshot` through `MultiplayerSessionHandle` on both host/guest.
  - **Phase 3** (`8ac4e1e`, scripts-only): curated mixed-skill bot combos (`curateMixedProfiles` — 2p adjacent-tier pairs only, 3-4p one weakest+strongest+filler combo, deliberately not a blind cartesian product), a `tickSkewGrowthByPair` detector (first-third-vs-last-third mean comparison, flagging a real desync-*widening* trend rather than just mean/max), and a standalone, scored `disconnectIsolation` report scenario (a repeatable version of `verify-multiplayer-disconnect.mjs`'s ad hoc scenario 1, runs once per invocation not per combo).
  - **Post-Phase-3 fix** (`73a835b`): found while writing Phase 4 docs — neither fork had actually wired Phase 2a's new engine hook into the *report*; the script's own doc comment still described the pre-Phase-2a "coarse" shape. Exported `run-balancing-telemetry.mjs`'s `aggregateLevelRuntime()` (its 7-category report builder, unchanged) and reused it directly for a new `perPlayerTelemetry` report section — the multiplayer snapshot shape matches single-player's field-for-field, since both are built from the same `buildTelemetrySnapshotFor`.
  - **Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean throughout; full Vitest suite green (299 files, 6094 tests) after the Phase 2a/2b merge; real live multiplayer smoke tests at every phase against real, isolated signaling+dev server pairs (never a developer's own manual session); single-player's own `balancing:telemetry`/`getTelemetrySnapshot()` re-confirmed unaffected (additive-only, same "N=1 is a case of the general shape" discipline this project applies everywhere else).
  - **Follow-up: both real findings root-caused and fixed, on request** (including "does this affect single-player too, if so fix it everywhere" — it did, for one of the two, and both fixes now live in shared `bot.mjs` where single-player automatically benefits too). (1) **The severe ~596-tick stall**: `checkExit()` (`engine.ts`) starts the level-transition countdown once *any single* alive player touches the exit (a real, intended co-op mechanic — "exit touch is a shared simulation event" — not a bug), and once it elapses the whole roster (including a still-mid-route teammate) gets carried to the next level's spawn. That teammate's `Bot` instance had no way to notice — it kept walking its pre-planned waypoint list against a live position that had moved to a different level entirely, using its own stale map for every navigation decision (exactly why the stall position read as solid wall against the original level's grid — it was never really there). Fixed in `bot.mjs`'s shared `driveLegs`/`driveTowardWithReplan`/`maybeDetourForLoot`: a mid-route `"teleported"` result (already detected, previously silently ignored and continued past) now stops the walk immediately. `run-balancing-telemetry-multiplayer.mjs`'s `driveOneBot` maps this into a new `"levelAdvanced"` outcome, counted the same as `"reachedExit"` in `teamOutcome`. Multiplayer-only in practice — single-player has no "teammate finishes first" scenario at all. (2) **The mine-corridor stall**: `findDangerousMine`'s "retreat now" trigger only fired once already inside a mine's blast radius, but a mine's real fuse (0.9s) ticks regardless of bot decision cadence — `MultiplayerBot`'s own real ~400ms decision window is long enough that a mine armed by the *other* nearby mine in a cluster (or by the bot's own earlier approach) could finish its fuse and detonate entirely within one held decision, with no chance to react. Fixed by giving `findDangerousMine` a `reactionBufferTiles` parameter scaled to a real decision's own max travel distance (`ENGINE_MOVE_SPEED × ENGINE_SPRINT_MULTIPLIER × stepMs`), mirroring the already-existing `MELEE_CLOSE_MIN_DISTANCE` fix's exact pattern — genuinely shared: single-player's much shorter decision windows (130ms headed, 50ms virtual) make this a near-no-op there, multiplayer's much longer window gets a buffer that actually matters. Verified live for both: the exact combo that had never once qualified across every earlier test run in this whole investigation (`Casual/normal/2p`) qualified on its very first attempt after fix (1), then 2/2 after fix (2) — with no more `stall`+`healthDrainFrozen` compound pattern near the mine cluster (occasional mine damage there is still expected — mines are hazards by design — what's fixed is getting physically stuck taking damage with no chance to react).

- [x] **Should be resumable — shipped 2026-07-22.** `npm run balancing:campaign-multiplayer` (`scripts/run-balancing-campaign-multiplayer.mjs`) now covers this: one OS process per combo, each writing its own file under `balancing_runs_multiplayer/`, resumable purely by scanning what's already on disk — see the SSH-host-parallelism `## Done` entry above for the full write-up (the same underlying change also added SSH-lane support). `run-balancing-telemetry-multiplayer.mjs` itself is unchanged (still one-shot) — same relationship single-player's `balancing:telemetry` (one-shot) has to `balancing:campaign` (resumable).

- [x] SSH-host parallelism for compute-bound verify/balancing scripts, plus a resumable orchestrator for multiplayer telemetry — shipped 2026-07-22. `scripts/lib/laneOrchestrator.mjs` extracts `run-balancing-campaign.mjs`'s own proven queue/resumability/watchdog design (a `Runner` interface, `LocalRunner` preserving today's exact `child_process` behavior); `scripts/lib/sshRunner.mjs`'s `SshRunner`/`buildSshRunners()` add SSH-host lanes on top, reading a very simple gitignored `ssh-hosts.env` (one `user@host` per line, `ssh-hosts.env.dist` ships the template) — auth stays entirely external (a pre-unlocked `ssh-agent` key), and every configured host is auto-bootstrapped upfront (clone-or-fetch into `/tmp/codeenstein3d-ssh-lane`, force-checkout the exact local commit, `npm ci`, Playwright install) rather than assuming any manual pre-provisioning; a host that fails bootstrap is excluded with a warning, never fatal to the whole run. New `scripts/run-balancing-campaign-multiplayer.mjs` gives `run-balancing-telemetry-multiplayer.mjs` the same resumable per-combo-file design `balancing:campaign` already has for single-player — closing that script's own "no incremental persistence" gap (see the multiplayer-telemetry-enhancements item below) as the same underlying change as adding parallelism, not two separate efforts. Needed a new `CODEENSTEIN_MP_TELEMETRY_COMBO_PROFILES` env var on the underlying script to pin one *exact* combo (including a specific mixed-skill one) per spawned invocation — a bare profile filter could only express a uniform combo. Local lane count for the multiplayer campaign defaults to 1, not 2: concurrent local invocations would collide on `multiplayerTestServers.mjs`'s fixed ports — real parallelism there is meant to come from SSH lanes instead. Known, documented gap: a local watchdog-triggered kill only best-effort propagates to a remote host's own process (`ssh -tt`), not a guarantee. See `doc/dev/balancing-telemetry.md`'s new "SSH-host parallelism" section for the full write-up.
  - **Follow-up, same day**: bootstrap now also installs `git`/a modern-enough Node+npm itself via `apt`/NodeSource if either is missing or too old (Debian/Ubuntu-only by design, needs passwordless `sudo` on the remote host) — user confirmed all hosts they'll actually use are Debian/Ubuntu-based, so no manual pre-provisioning is needed beyond SSH access + sudo now. Confirmed ARM hosts work fine too — nothing in `sshRunner.mjs` is architecture-specific, and Playwright's Chromium build has genuine Linux ARM64 support.
  - **Second follow-up, same day (user wanted a scoped sudoers config, not broad root access)**: found a real problem while trying to document exact sudoers entries — NodeSource's own setup script is `curl | sudo bash`, and a sudoers rule can only match the *executable* (`bash`), never what's piped into its stdin, so a NOPASSWD rule permitting that is indistinguishable from unrestricted passwordless root, not a scoped install step. `npx playwright install --with-deps` has the same class of problem one step further: Playwright itself decides and `apt-get install`s an arbitrary, OS/version-state-dependent package list at run time — confirmed directly via `--dry-run`, which reported different missing packages on hosts at different patch levels — so there's no fixed command line a sudoers rule could ever pin for it either.
  - **Third follow-up, same day (user found even the scoped-sudoers version too fiddly)**: replaced the whole sudoers-scoping approach with a cleaner split instead — new `scripts/setup-ssh-lane-host.mjs`, run manually/interactively once per host (`node scripts/setup-ssh-lane-host.mjs [user@host ...]`, real `ssh -t` session, prompts for sudo like running the commands by hand) installs git/a modern Node/Playwright's system deps/clones the repo/`npm ci`. The *automated* `bootstrapHost()` in `sshRunner.mjs` now never touches sudo or apt at all — it only checks git/an adequate Node already exist (failing with a pointer back to the setup script if not) before clone-or-fetch/checkout/`npm ci`/`playwright install chromium` (browser binary only). No sudoers file to write at all now — just run the setup script once per host.
  - **Fourth follow-up, same day (real failure hit on an actual lane host)**: the remote clone was shipping *this* machine's own `git remote get-url origin` (an SSH-style `git@github.com:...` URL — the natural default for a repo owner who pushes) straight to the remote host, which had no matching GitHub SSH key and failed to clone. Added `toHttpsCloneUrl()` (`sshRunner.mjs`, exported, used by both `bootstrapHost()` and `setup-ssh-lane-host.mjs`) — normalizes the two common SSH forms (`git@host:owner/repo.git`, `ssh://git@host/owner/repo.git`) to `https://`, which needs no credentials at all for this public repo; anything already `https://` (or unrecognized) passes through unchanged. Also, per the same follow-up request: `setup-ssh-lane-host.mjs` now appends a newly-set-up host to `ssh-hosts.env` automatically once its setup succeeds (`appendHostIfMissing`, dedupes against what's already listed) — adding a host is now just `node scripts/setup-ssh-lane-host.mjs user@newhost`, no separate manual file edit.

- [x] `verify-multiplayer-transition.mjs` — guest's own session could end unexpectedly before the transition, root-caused and fixed 2026-07-22. Real CI failure: host reached the exit alive (the earlier melee-grind fix, above, held), but the guest's `activeMultiplayerSession` had already gone `null` by the time the script checked its status right before the transition, and every guest-side check after that read stale nulls (`host and guest agree on the new level's exit/grid` both failed). Root cause: `debugSetGodMode("host", true)` (`RaycasterEngine`) is a purely local mutation with no wire message broadcasting it — calling it only on the host page made the *host's own local copy of itself* invulnerable, but left the *guest's own local copy of "host"* exactly as vulnerable as before. Each peer runs its own full lockstep simulation from the same inputs, so the demo campaign's real enemies could kill "host" on the guest's own simulation (while the real host page's own simulation never took any damage), occasionally leaving the guest's own engine concluding the whole team was eliminated and ending its session entirely — while the host's own session, correctly invulnerable from its own point of view, carried on normally. Fixed by calling `debugSetGodMode("host", true)` on *both* pages, keeping both peers' own local simulations in agreement about "host" being invulnerable — exactly like a real, correctly-synced piece of game state would be. Also hardened the "land on a new level" check itself: it read `getMapGrid()` and compared against the pre-transition grid, but once a peer's whole session ends every hook (including `getMapGrid()`) starts returning `null`, and `JSON.stringify(null) !== JSON.stringify(prevGrid)` reads as "the grid changed" even though nothing transitioned — added an explicit `grid !== null &&` guard so a peer's session ending can no longer produce a false-positive pass on this specific check. Verified: 3 clean repeated real runs post-fix (guest status correctly reading "alive" each time, not `null`), `tsc --noEmit` clean, full Vitest suite green (2098 tests, 100% coverage) — script-only fix, no `src/` changes needed.

- [x] Split the two slow multiplayer verify steps into their own parallel CI jobs — shipped 2026-07-22, raised after a "can SSH-lane parallelism speed up CI too" question. It can't directly (GitHub-hosted runners aren't SSH-reachable), but the real question — more CI parallelism on a Free account — had a genuinely free answer: this repo is public, so Actions minutes are free regardless of plan, and the actual constraint is a 20-concurrent-job account-wide cap the workflow was nowhere near (6 jobs in use). `verify-browser`'s chromium leg ran 8 `npm run verify:*` steps sequentially, and two — `verify:multiplayer-transition`/`verify:multiplayer-multiguest` — are the genuinely slow, multi-minute real-session ones (already chromium-only, so no matrix coverage to lose). Pulled both into their own dedicated jobs (`verify-multiplayer-transition`/`verify-multiplayer-multiguest` in `.github/workflows/verify.yml`), each mirroring a single chromium leg's own setup (checkout/cache/Playwright install/WAD cache/signaling+dev server pair) — confirmed independent beforehand (each owns its own connect/teardown boilerplate, no shared state across steps). Job count goes from 6 to 8, still well under the cap. Updated `doc/dev/testing.md`'s two stale references to the old `if: matrix.browser != 'firefox' && matrix.browser != 'webkit'` skip condition (no longer accurate — no matrix to skip a leg of anymore). Verified: YAML re-parses cleanly (`python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"`), full Vitest suite green (2098 tests, 100% coverage, no `src/` changes) — real timing improvement to be confirmed on the next CI run.

- [x] Create docker container to run multiplayer server incl. stun/turn — `docker/`: a compose stack with a `signaling` service (node:20-alpine + the single `multiplayer-server.mjs`, built from the repo on the VPS, published `127.0.0.1:8787` behind the existing host proxy) and an opt-in `coturn` service (`--profile turn`, host networking, hardening in `coturn/turnserver.conf.base`, deployment values appended by `coturn/entrypoint.sh` so the shared secret never reaches argv or disk). Needed two server-side additions: `CODEENSTEIN_MULTIPLAYER_BIND_HOST` (the process hardcoded `127.0.0.1`, unreachable from outside a container) and `CODEENSTEIN_MULTIPLAYER_TRUSTED_PROXY_IPS` — without the latter the proxy arrives as the bridge gateway rather than loopback, `X-Forwarded-For` is ignored and *every* player shares one rate-limit bucket. Two container gotchas found by actually booting it: `cap_drop: [ALL]` makes `execve` of `turnserver` fail with EPERM because the binary carries `cap_net_bind_service` as a file capability, and coturn (uid 65534) can't read a root-only Let's Encrypt privkey without `TURN_CERT_GID`. Verified end-to-end with docker: per-IP rate limiting through the bridge (and its collapse without the trusted-proxy setting), a hand-computed HMAC credential allocating successfully while a wrong-secret one fails, and `denied-peer-ip` refusing 127.0.0.1 as a relay peer (403) on both plain and TLS listeners. Docs: `docker/README.md`, deployment runbook §6 + troubleshooting, server-spec topology note

- [x] multiplayer tab should be hidden if `VITE_MULTIPLAYER_SERVER_URL` is not set — previously the tab was only ever *disabled* (greyed out, with an explanatory tooltip) based on workspace eligibility (GitHub/Demos), regardless of whether a signaling server was even configured for the build; an unconfigured build could still show an enabled-looking Multiplayer tab that fails the moment Host/Join is clicked. `updateMultiplayerTabEnabled` (`src/main.ts`) now checks `import.meta.env.VITE_MULTIPLAYER_SERVER_URL` first and fully hides the tab (`display: none`) when unset, before ever reaching the existing eligibility logic; the button also now starts `display: none` in `index.html` to avoid a flash of a visible-then-hidden tab on load

- [x] ensure debughooks like clearAllEnemies are omitted from dist/prod builds — every `?testHooks=1` automation hook (`window.__codeensteinTestHooks`, `window.__codeensteinMultiplayerTestHooks` including the mutating `debugSetGodMode`/`injectDesync`, and the bot rotation-speed URL-param override) was previously gated only by the URL query string at runtime, with no build-time gating at all — the code always compiled into and shipped inside the real production bundle, reachable by anyone who appended `?testHooks=1` to the deployed site and opened devtools (a real cheat vector in multiplayer, not just clutter). New shared `isTestHooksActive()` (`src/engine/engine.ts`, exported and reused from `main.ts`) adds an `import.meta.env.DEV &&` check ahead of the existing query-string check; since all of this project's verify/balancing/perf scripts drive a real `vite`/`npm run dev` server (confirmed — never a built/`vite preview` bundle), this costs CI nothing, while a real `vite build` (`import.meta.env.DEV` statically `false` in that mode) lets esbuild's minifier dead-code-eliminate the entire gated branch — confirmed by grepping the built `dist/assets/index-*.js`: the string `"testHooks"` and both window-hook install sites are completely absent post-build (only the now-unreachable class methods themselves remain, same as any other dead method). Deliberately left `?perfDebug=1`/`window.__codeensteinPerfStats` (`perfDebug.ts`) ungated — its own doc comment establishes it's a real field-diagnostics feature meant for an actual affected player's browser via a handed-out URL, not headless automation, so it must keep working in production; confirmed still present in the built bundle. Found and fixed one genuinely dead branch while wiring this up: `updateMultiplayerTabEnabled`'s (from the item above) unconfigured-server early return had a copy-pasted "bounce back to Local if this tab was selected" check that can never actually fire (`MULTIPLAYER_SERVER_CONFIGURED` is fixed for the page's whole lifetime, so a hidden/disabled tab was never selectable in the first place) — removed, which is what surfaced as a real 99.98%→100% branch-coverage gap. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full Vitest suite green (104 files, 2230 tests) at 100% coverage across lines/statements/functions/branches, and a real `npm run build` + bundle grep as described above

- [x] Host's game could show a stuck "PAUSED" overlay right around team-elimination, with no canvas/workspace reachable afterward — reported 2026-07-25, initially misdiagnosed as a multiplayer cross-peer team-elimination-consensus desync (ruled out: real DevTools evidence from both peers showed both independently and correctly agreeing the team really was eliminated). Automated bot-driven repro attempts across many variables (headless, headed with real window focus/backgrounding restored, host Chromium + guest real Chrome, a real production `vite build` bundle) all failed to reproduce it — turned out to be structurally impossible for a synthetic-event-driven harness to trigger at all (see root cause). Root-caused only via the user's own real screen recordings + live DevTools console, cross-referenced with temporary `[ENGINE-LIFECYCLE]`/`[INPUT-RAW]`/`[PAUSE-STATE]`/`[SAMPLER-RAW]`/`[BUNDLE-LOAD]` diagnostic logging (all reverted once root-caused). Real root cause: loading a workspace auto-launches a local single-player preview level (`launchLevel()`) and shows its "Start" briefing overlay (`GameHud.showLevelStart`); that overlay's dismiss listeners (`window` keydown for Enter/Space/Escape, canvas mousedown) are armed immediately and only self-remove once they actually fire. Switching straight to Multiplayer/Host instead of clicking "Start" left that preview engine `.stop()`'d but never nulled out (`beginMultiplayerLevel()`), so its stale listeners stayed live. The first real Enter/Space (e.g. the melee key)/Escape/canvas-mousedown during the real multiplayer session then fired that stale callback, calling `activeEngine?.start()` on the old, already-stopped preview `RaycasterEngine` — reviving it for real (its own live `InputController`, its own render/simulate loop) sharing the same canvas as the actual session, invisibly. That zombie engine could independently receive a genuine `blur`/`pointerlockchange` event and set its own `isPaused = true`, rendering a "PAUSED" overlay disconnected from the real session, which nothing in the teardown flow knew to clear. Fixed with a one-line change: `beginMultiplayerLevel()` now sets `activeEngine = null` right after `.stop()`-ing it, the same pattern `resetToFileTree()` already used for single-player teardown — the stale callback becomes a guaranteed no-op. Only ever reachable by whichever peer loaded a workspace locally (hosting), never a joining guest. Verified live by the user against a real production `vite build` recording (level transition + revive on the next level also confirmed working for guest). Doc: `doc/dev/multiplayer-netcode-spec.md`'s "known open issue" section rewritten to describe the resolved root cause.

- [x] new `scripts/verify-multiplayer-campaign.mjs` — real, chained multi-level multiplayer verification, added 2026-07-25 after confirming neither `verify-multiplayer-transition.mjs` (proves one transition, host god-moded) nor `run-balancing-telemetry-multiplayer.mjs` (deliberately one bundled level per run) ever actually drove a session across *several* consecutive levels. Real 2p/`easy`/Casual session, **both** peers bot-driven with real combat (no god mode), loops "drive both bots to the exit, wait for the transition, repeat" until the session ends or a `MAX_LEVEL_ITERATIONS=20` runaway-loop safety net (the real demo campaign is 17 levels) is hit. Pass bar is reaching at least level 4 (`MIN_LEVEL_INDEX`, overridable via `CODEENSTEIN_MP_CAMPAIGN_MIN_LEVEL` for fast local smoke-testing) — deliberately **not** a stopping point; the loop keeps going past it for real, all the way to `"campaign-complete"` if the team can manage it. Session-end reason (`team-eliminated`/`host-disconnected`/`campaign-complete`/`level-transition-failed`) is read off `#multiplayer-status`'s exact text (the comparison table itself is canvas-drawn, not real DOM) — generalizes `verify-multiplayer-disconnect.mjs`'s own single-reason text-match trick to all four. Starts its own isolated dev+signaling server pair (`multiplayerTestServers.mjs`), chromium-only, **not CI-wired** (uncapped, potentially full-campaign-length wall-clock cost, unlike even the two existing dedicated "slow" CI jobs) — run manually via `npm run verify:multiplayer-campaign`. Smoke-tested live at `CODEENSTEIN_MP_CAMPAIGN_MIN_LEVEL=2`: cleared levels 1→5 cleanly in a row (every check green, including a real `routeFailed` outcome on one bot that didn't stop the team since the teammate's own exit-touch carried it along) before being stopped early by hand — confirms the mechanics work; a full real `campaign-complete` run was not carried to completion (~7 real min/level, would take over an hour for all 17).

- [x] disabled buttons in the multiplayer sidebar (Create Session, Join, Start Session) visually still looked fully active — reported 2026-07-25. Root cause: `.settings-btn` (the shared class every one of those buttons uses) had no `:disabled` rule at all, unlike every other button family in `style.css` (`.tab-btn`, `.suggestion-btn`, `#select-workspace` et al., `.replay-btn`), and its own `:hover` rule wasn't guarded with `:not(:disabled)` either, so even hovering a disabled button still showed the interactive highlight. `#multiplayer-max-players` (the max-players select) had the identical gap. Fixed by adding `.settings-btn:disabled`/`#multiplayer-max-players:disabled` (dimmed `var(--border)` text + `cursor: not-allowed`, matching the existing pattern) and guarding both `:hover` rules with `:not(:disabled)`; also needed a dedicated `.settings-btn.settings-btn-primary:disabled` override (same 2-class specificity as the primary variant itself, so source order alone wouldn't let the accent styling win) — real, reachable case: the Start Session button is "primary"-styled once a guest connects, then gets disabled for the real network round-trip while the map transfers to every guest. Verified live via a headless real-browser check: disabled now computes to dimmed text + `not-allowed` cursor, clearly distinct from enabled's bright text + `pointer` cursor (previously identical).

- [x] FPS overlay always reads 0 in multiplayer — reported 2026-07-25. Root cause: `displayFps` (`RaycasterEngine`) is only ever updated inside `frame()`, the engine's own internal `requestAnimationFrame` loop from `start()` — multiplayer never calls that; both `multiplayerSessionHost.ts`/`Guest.ts` drive the shared engine via `startExternallyDriven()` + a tick-clock-Worker-paced `advance(FIXED_DT)`, which renders every tick but never fed `updateFpsCounter()` at all, so `displayFps` stayed permanently at its initial `0`. Fixed with a new `externallyDriven` flag (set by `startExternallyDriven()`) plus `lastExternalAdvanceMs`: `advance()` now measures real wall-clock time between its own calls and feeds the same `updateFpsCounter()` when driven this way — genuinely useful, not a stand-in number, since a real tick backlog/stall now shows up as a real dip instead of a fixed ~30. Deliberately excludes the replay viewer (never calls `startExternallyDriven()`), whose fast-forward `burstTo()` bursts would otherwise report a meaningless huge FPS. New `getDisplayFps()` read-only getter (mirrors `getExitCountdownRemaining()`'s own pattern) for test/introspection access to the private field. 3 new `engine.test.ts` cases, 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] the level exit was reachable while enemies were still alive nearby (single-player and multiplayer both) — requested 2026-07-25. `checkExit()` (the one shared win-condition gate for both modes) had no enemy-alive check at all. Fixed with a new `exitRoomHasAliveEnemy()`: reuses `Enemy.home` (the room-rectangle every enemy already roams within, set at spawn) directly — `map.exit` falling inside a *living* enemy's own `home` rectangle is exactly "this enemy belongs to the exit's room," no separate room-lookup/id needed. Gates only the *start* of a win/countdown (an already-running multiplayer countdown is untouched, matching its existing "unconditional once started" design) — touching the exit while blocked does nothing at all, same silent "not yet" as a locked door with no key (`openDoorAhead()`'s existing precedent), not a special-cased hint. 6 new `engine.test.ts` cases (single-player win/no-win across all 4 AABB-edge directions + dead-enemy-doesn't-block + one multiplayer-countdown case), 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] the "YOU DIED — spectating X" banner and the "Build finishing in Ns…" exit-countdown toast draw at the exact same canvas position (`w/2, 40`) — reported 2026-07-25 as visually overlapping once a dead/spectating player's team starts the level-transition countdown. Fixed by suppressing the spectate banner once `exitCountdownRemaining !== null` — the countdown wins (more urgent, time-bounded; a dead player already knows they're dead). 1 new `engine.test.ts` case (both players share a hazard-exit spawn tile so the countdown starts immediately, one dies from lingering hazard damage; asserts the countdown text draws and "YOU DIED" doesn't), 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] canvas loses keyboard focus on a multiplayer level transition (host reported, but the fix is symmetric) — requested 2026-07-25, "have to click it again" every time. Root cause: `buildSessionEngine()` (`sessionEngine.ts`) is the one shared engine-construction helper both the initial session start *and* every later `startLevel()` re-invocation (level transitions) call — it rebinds a brand-new `InputController`/`LocalInputSampler` each time, and the just-torn-down old one's own `detach()` already releases pointer lock, but nothing ever re-called `canvas.focus()` the way `main.ts`'s own single-player `launchLevel()` already does per level (that exact call site's comment describes this identical symptom — "silently swallowed until the player clicks the canvas themselves"). The initial session start happened to work anyway (`main.ts`'s `beginMultiplayerLevel()` separately focuses the canvas before the session starts), masking the gap until a live transition exposed it. Fixed with one `canvas.focus()` call in `buildSessionEngine()` itself, right after `localSampler.attach()` — covers both call sites uniformly, harmless/idempotent on the already-focused initial-start path. (Re-acquiring *pointer lock* specifically still needs a real click afterward — that's an unavoidable browser security restriction on `requestPointerLock()`, identical in single-player, not part of this bug.) New `sessionEngine.test.ts` case asserting `canvas.focus` is actually called; 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] multiplayer end-of-run comparison table: kills always 0, score always sub-1k — reported 2026-07-25. Two compounding bugs in `RaycasterEngine.rosterSnapshot()`: (1) `kills` is `p.kills`, a raw per-level counter that resets to 0 at every level transition — unlike `priorScore`, there was no `EngineCarryover` field to carry it forward at all, so the table only ever showed whichever level happened to end the run. (2) `breakdown.total` ("pts") was *supposed* to be a real cumulative run total (`rosterSnapshot()`'s own doc comment already claimed this), but its carryover half (`priorScoreBreakdown`) was only populated `if (p.telemetry)` in `captureCarryoverFor()` — i.e. gated behind `PLAYER_STATS_ENABLED`/`?testHooks=1`, off by default in real play — so in practice it also reset every transition, capping the shown total at one level's own score while the live in-game HUD (`stats.score`, via the *never*-gated `priorScore`) correctly climbed into the thousands the whole time — a visible, jarring live-vs-endscreen mismatch. Fixed with a new `priorKills` carryover field (`EngineCarryover`/`PlayerState`, mirrors `priorScore`'s exact "defaults to 0, unconditional" pattern) and by un-gating `priorScoreBreakdown`'s own accumulation in `captureCarryoverFor()` — `computeLevelScoreBreakdown()` was already safe to call unconditionally (its accuracy-bonus sub-component alone degrades to 0 without telemetry, same as the live per-frame `stats.score` derivation already relies on), it just got needlessly bundled behind the same gate as the genuinely-expensive curated `PlayerFacingStats` accumulation (`priorPlayerStats`, which correctly stays telemetry-gated — unchanged). `rosterSnapshot().kills` is now `p.priorKills + p.kills`, matching the "prior + current" split `priorScore` already used. 3 new/updated `engine.test.ts` cases, 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] CI regression from the exit-alive-enemy gate above, caught by PR #37's own `verify (multiplayer-transition)` job (2026-07-25) — root-caused via a real worktree bisect (isolated dev+signaling servers, pre-session commit `bb6cfa8` vs. the branch tip, then a single-hunk revert to confirm the exact cause) rather than assumed from the log alone: `verify-multiplayer-transition.mjs`'s host is invulnerable *and* never fights (`ignoreThreats: true`, a deliberate choice — see `debugSetGodMode`'s own doc comment on why removing combat variance mattered), so the exit's own room never actually loses its enemies, and the new gate silently blocked the transition forever — the reported "host got stuck on the final approach" wasn't a navigation bug, `checkExit()` just never activated. **Not fixed by loosening the gameplay rule** — fixed by giving the script the same kind of narrow, test-only, both-peers-mutating debug hook `debugSetGodMode` already established: new `RaycasterEngine.debugClearExitRoomEnemies()`, scoped to only the exit's own room (reuses the same `Enemy.home`-vs-`map.exit` containment check as `exitRoomHasAliveEnemy()`) — deliberately *not* a "clear every enemy" hook, since that would have silently killed the same script's other real coverage (the vulnerable guest dying to real roaming enemies *elsewhere*, for the "revived after a pre-transition death" check). Threaded through `MultiplayerSessionHandle`/host+guest drivers/`main.ts`'s test hooks the same way `debugSetGodMode` already is; the verify script now calls it on both pages right alongside the existing god-mode calls. New engine/session-driver/main.ts test coverage; re-verified for real against the actual script (not just the reverted-hunk bisect) before pushing. 100% coverage maintained.

- [x] multikill could be triggered by firing the shotgun at a single enemy, with a fast-enough next kill then registering as an ultrakill — reported 2026-07-26. Root cause: the shotgun (7 pellets, 70px spread) resolves every pellet's target against one pre-shot enemy snapshot taken in `resolveShot()` before any damage is applied, so several pellets routinely land on the *same* close-range enemy in one blast; `damageEnemy()` (`src/engine/engine.ts`) had no guard against being re-entered for an enemy that an earlier pellet in that same volley had already killed, so each "excess" pellet re-ran the whole kill branch — an extra `shooter.kills += 1`, a fresh assist-share payout, and another `registerKillForStreak()` call, stacking multiple timestamps into the rolling multi/ultra-kill window from one single death. Fixed with a one-line `if (!enemy.alive) return;` guard at the top of `damageEnemy()` — pellets targeting a corpse from within the same shot are now a no-op, while pellets on *different* enemies and genuine non-lethal multi-pellet damage are unaffected. Strengthened the existing "fires the shotgun's multiple pellets in one trigger pull" test (`engine.test.ts`) to assert exactly 1 kill from the single overkilled enemy — confirmed it fails (`7` kills) without the guard and passes with it. Full suite (2268 tests) and `tsc --noEmit` green; `npm run balancing:scan` shows only its usual unrelated stall/healthDrainFrozen navigation noise, no new anomalies.

- [x] "Out of ammo!" toast when shooting while out of ammo, fading out quickly with each dry-fire resetting the fadeout timer — requested 2026-07-26. `fire()`'s existing out-of-ammo branch (`src/engine/engine.ts`) previously only did a `console.log`, with zero player-facing feedback. Cloned the codebase's existing `cheatToastText`/`cheatToastFrames`/`showCheatToast`/`drawCheatToast` toast pipeline: a new `outOfAmmoToastFrames` counter on `PlayerState` (no text field needed — the message never varies), a short `OUT_OF_AMMO_TOAST_FRAMES = 45` (~0.75s, deliberately much shorter than the existing 120-frame/~2s cheat/kill-streak toasts), and a new `drawOutOfAmmoToast` (`hud.ts`) styled as `drawCheatToast`'s small top-center pill but with `drawKillStreakToast`'s urgent red palette instead of the green confirmation color. `showOutOfAmmoToast()` unconditionally reassigns the frame counter every dry-fire attempt — same idiom as `showCheatToast`/`damage()`'s `flashFrames` — which gives "reset to full on retrigger" for free, no stacking/queueing logic needed. Per-player, local-only render, matching every other toast's multiplayer behavior (a teammate's dry-fire never surfaces on other clients' screens). No new sound — out of scope, not requested. 2 new/updated `engine.test.ts` cases (toast activates on dry-fire; re-firing mid-fade snaps the counter back to full rather than stacking). Full suite (2269 tests) and `tsc --noEmit` green; visually confirmed in a real browser (demo campaign, drained the pistol's ammo via the test-only Backquote fire key) — the red pill renders and fades as intended.

- [x] Release-prep documentation audit for beta-5 (2026-07-26) — asked to check all docs/changelog for staleness before tagging. Found and fixed: `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased` section was missing the TURN-relay strict-NAT fallback, the "Out of ammo!" toast, and the shotgun-overkill fix, and got renamed to `## beta-5`. `README.md` still said "Node.js 18+" and "1528 tests" — real requirements moved to Node 20.19+/22.12+ (Vite 8's `engines` field) once `vite@8`/`vitest@4.1.10`/`jsdom@29.1.1` landed on master, and the suite is now 2288 tests at a 99.9%/99.9%/99.5%/99.5% coverage gate (not the old flat 100%, per `vitest.config.ts`'s own threshold comment on the v8/istanbul remapping bugs). Same two stale facts (`Vite 6`, "100%... gate") were also wrong in `doc/dev/architecture.md`, and `doc/dev/testing.md`'s "Setup" section still described the pre-bump pinned versions and Node floor as current. Added a `package.json` `engines` field matching Vite's requirement so a too-old Node fails with a clear message instead of a cryptic `styleText` syntax error. Also found `.github/dependabot.yml`'s `docker` ecosystem entry pointed at `/docker` (no Dockerfile there — it's at `docker/signaling/Dockerfile`), which had been failing every weekly run since it was added; split into a `docker` entry for `/docker/signaling` and a separate `docker-compose` entry (a distinct ecosystem, confirmed against the JSON schema) for `/docker`, so the compose-pinned `coturn` image gets tracked too. Verified `npm run typecheck`/`npm test`/`npm run coverage`/`npm run build` all still pass clean on Node 24 before making any of these claims. The rest of `doc/dev/`, `doc/user/`, and the multiplayer specs were re-read in full and found already current — no other stale claims found.

- [x] **`routeEfficiency` ~0.34 investigated (2026-07-30) — mostly not a bot defect, and the metric itself was the problem.** The bot does walk 2.9x the theoretical minimum, but decomposing it against the planned route plus a new per-decision activity attribution (`summarizeActivityDistance`, which charges every tile to the errand the bot was on) gives three factors multiplying to 2.97x: **1.68x** the planned route vs the bare spawn→exit BFS — *entirely* the two key/locked-door levels (demo 3 and 7 at 3.7x/3.5x; the other six plan within 5% of optimal), i.e. level design; **1.32x** loot detours at 24% of all distance, of which 63% of tiles are ammo and 19% mandatory keys and only 3.3% (0.8% of total distance) provably wasted — a health pack grabbed at `hp=1.00` against a `MAX_HEALTH` cap; **1.30x** actually following the plan, the only part the bot owns, with 4.2% of distance covered while engaged and ~1.2% of sim time the known atan2 oscillation. Replan retries and the exit-gate fallback are **~0%** on levels 1-8. So `routeEfficiencyScore` moves mostly with level layout and loot policy: it is now marked `"flat"` (was `"up"`) in `abReport.mjs`, and new `navigationMapFlow.routeFollowingOverhead` (distance ÷ `staticAnalysis.plannedRouteTiles`, measured **1.71**) is the metric to judge navigation changes on. A hypothesis that full-HP health detours were a meaningful waste was measured and **rejected** at 0.8% of distance. Permanent telemetry added: the trace `activity` label + `summarizeActivityDistance` (printed under `NAV_DIAG=1`), `plannedRouteTiles`, `routeFollowingOverhead`, and `hp`/`urgent` on the loot-detour debug line

- [x] **`generate:default-highscore` level-10 wedge — RESOLVED 2026-07-30.** Root cause was never navigation: `checkExit()` keeps the exit inert while any enemy homed to the exit's room is alive, and `pickThreat` only ever considers *aggroed* enemies, so an exit-room enemy the route never provoked was invisible to the bot forever — it stood on a working exit until its caller gave up. Fixed by `Bot#driveToExit`, which walks at the blocker until proximity aggros it and the normal combat branches take over (a navigation fix, deliberately not a new combat policy). The generator has completed and been regenerated three times since (`7770010`, `f31fc3d`, `223067c`). Original diagnosis kept below, including the part that was wrong. — Casual never qualifies, because a stuck run produces no replay entry, and `ATTEMPT_CAP = Infinity` means the script does not fail, it runs forever (the same trap that burned 2h40m before). **Not a bot regression** — verified directly against a pre-bot-work worktree at `7ca3d0a`, which wedges at level 10 in 3 of 4 attempts vs the current bot's 14 of 20, i.e. the same rate. So this arrived with the map-gen features, not the bot rework. **Measured, and it is not the Switchboards.** Levels 1/2/4/8/9/14 all carry 3-4 switchboard spurs and route in a single leg. The driver is the locked-door/key chain: stage10 plans **22 legs / 538 waypoints over 6 doors and 6 keys** — the longest of any regular level, and worse than the already-flagged stage03 (13 legs / 529 waypoints / 8 doors / 4 keys). `planRoute` succeeds, so this is the bot failing to *execute* a 22-leg fetch-key-then-open-door chain, not a planning failure — the same shape as the stage03 item below, one level more severe. `placeDoors` locks every corridor mouth of a private/protected method room, and a Rust kernel module is mostly private functions, so door density is the lever to look at rather than the spur caps. (stage17 is far worse still at 125 legs / 4610 waypoints, but no run gets that far.) Note `verify:campaign` passes: it checks *structural* reachability, not that a bot can route the level. Consequence worth stating plainly — the shipped `defaultHighscore.ts` is both stale (generated before these levels existed) and currently unreproducible.

- [x] **Replay playback rebuilt a different map than was recorded — FIXED 2026-07-30.** `launchLevel` derived the `generate()` options from the raw `carryover`, while the segment it recorded stored `effectiveCarryover` (which force-adds gdb/ghidra/Friday Hotfix at campaign levels 4/8/12) and `buildEngineFor` regenerated from *that*. Measured: those weapon-ownership options leave the enemy roster byte-identical but do change `ammoPickups` and the grid, since Vendor Depots stock ammo for what you already own — so a replay crossing one of those boundaries walked a map with different pickups than the run it was replaying. Fixed by computing `effectiveCarryover` *before* generation and collapsing both derivations into one exported `mapGenerationOptionsFor(carryover, campaignLevelIndex, bonusLevel)`, called with the same carryover at both ends, so the drift is unrepresentable rather than merely fixed. Also removes a live-play inconsistency in its own right: the engine was already constructed with the forced weapons, so a depot stocking no ammo for them contradicted the weapon the player had just been handed. All 17 demo levels re-verified routable under the new options.

- [x] **`defaultHighscore.ts` regenerated 2026-07-30 — the #42 stale-replay blocker is CLOSED.** The shipped entries were recorded before the Elite HP nerf and would have replayed silently diverged (a segment's `astHash` covers "parsed AST JSON + campaign name", and enemy HP is generated *from* that AST rather than being part of it, so the hash still validated). Regenerated only after both underlying fixes landed, so it took one run rather than two: the balance fingerprint (`balanceHash.ts`) and the record/playback map-generation mismatch (`mapGenerationOptionsFor`). Run took **33 minutes**, all three profiles qualified 3/3 within 4 attempts, zero FAIL lines. Independently verified afterwards rather than trusting the generator's own self-check: all 39 segments carry a 64-hex `balanceHash`, 15 distinct across 39 (exactly one per distinct level, confirming the hash is profile-independent and deterministic), and every one was recomputed from a fresh map generated through the same options playback derives — **0 mismatches**, so playback accepts all shipped replays. The Elite nerf also shows up in the runs themselves: they now end on stage12/13/15 instead of all three dying at stage12.

- [x] **Bot weapon priority — DONE 2026-07-30.** gamer skill level of the bot shouldnt favor the RPG as much. we need to define a proper weapon priority for bot - then skilllevels will only modify their weights — shipped as `scoreRangedWeapon` (`combatPolicy.mjs`): weapon choice is now *scored* per shot from expected damage against the target's actual HP, the weapon's real reach (grounded in the engine's Cone of Fire, after a captured fight of 74 shots / 16 hits / 0 kills from firing beyond range), ammo economy via the profile's `ammoThrift`, and self-harm risk via `selfHarmAversion`. `weaponPriority` survives only as the tiebreak when two weapons score equivalently, which is exactly the "skill levels only modify their weapon-choice" the item asked for. Measured: `avgTtkByCategory.normal` -34 to -42% across the three profiles, guards pass.

- [x] **Map layout rework — rooms pack, corridors vary (2026-08-02).** Reported as "room placement often yields very long corridors; the corridors also barely change and re-use the breakup elements". Both halves traced to two lines: `connectRooms` chained rooms in *parse order* while `tryPlaceRoom` placed each at an *independent uniform-random position*, so the corridor between two consecutive entities was however far apart two unrelated draws landed — a mean of 59 tiles, peaking at 191, against rooms only 4-18 wide. Everything else followed: the breakup pass had to inject **277 near-identical rooms across 17 levels (21% of all floor area)** to keep sightlines down, and map size had to be generous enough for 200 random draws to keep finding gaps, leaving 86-93% of every grid solid rock. `decisions.md` had already named proximity chaining as the fix and deferred it as out of scope; this closed that. Shipped: `tryGrowRoom` (grow beside an already-placed room, anchors ordered by distance to the room `connectRooms` will carve to), `mapSize` derived from the summed room footprint instead of LOC, `connectLoops` (shortcuts on top of the parse-order spine), per-leg corridor width plus a staircase leg shape, and `dressCorridors` replacing the two-element breakup vocabulary with six kinds that never repeat consecutively. Campaign-wide: **leg mean 59 -> 22, max 191 -> 48, longest straight run 34 -> 16, corridor features 277 -> 84 over six kinds, floor-within-bounding-box 22% -> 37%, loops 5 -> 21.** Verified with a before/after anomaly scan on both commits: bot qualification unchanged at 3/3 per profile, total navigation anomalies down 22%. `[notes: map layout rework]`
  - **Three constraints that were not obvious and are worth not rediscovering.** (1) The inter-room *gap* feeds the four `sideCandidates` carvers — secret rooms, vendor depots, switchboards, exception zones all claim untouched rock plus a one-tile margin, so packing rooms flush would have silently deleted four features at once; `verify:campaign` is the gate. (2) A loop shortcut into a private/protected method room *costs a key*, since `placeDoors` locks every corridor mouth — so `connectLoops` verifies doorway counts before and after each carve and undoes it tile by tile, rather than predicting a jittered path geometrically (predicting was tried and rejected almost everything). (3) Once rooms packed, the length-driven breakup pass fired **6 times campaign-wide**, which would have deleted the corridor encounter entirely, because `spawnEdgeCaseEnemies` populates those rooms and nothing else — hence the ornament pass.
  - **Layout quality is not a property any assertion holds.** Every invariant test — reachability, no overlap, no severed corridor, one key per doorway — passed happily throughout the years the levels read as beads on a string. `npm run report:level-maps` (new) renders all 17 floor plans to PNGs and prints the metrics that move when placement or topology changes. Written up in `testing.md` under "what the test suite structurally cannot catch".
  - **Left open deliberately:** `stage06_pipeline.py`'s `healthDrainFrozen` count went 10 -> 53, because packing rooms moved the Exception Zone's 1-tile acid gauntlet from a remote spur onto the main corridor. The shaft geometry is unchanged. It generalises an existing rule (`placeExceptionZones` already excludes the spawn room as an anchor on the grounds that no distance makes "the corridor out of your spawn room is a damage gauntlet" acceptable) — whether that exclusion should widen, or whether it is bot policy since a human sees acid and decides, is a design call and not one to settle inside a layout change.
  - **Two latent bugs surfaced in `verify-campaign-playthrough.mjs`.** Its fixture only ever survived because random placement happened to put spawn and exit 4 tiles apart, with an enemy on the exit room's centre 1.4 tiles from the spawn corner — now a struct, which spawns nothing. And `walkWaypoints`' arrival tolerance (0.15) sat *below* half a sprinting tick's travel (~0.16), so any approach landing in that window orbited the waypoint for the full 30s timeout while visibly moving.
  - **A misattribution worth recording.** `verify:multiplayer-transition` failed on the branch and was attributed to it; `EXIT_CLEARANCE_TILES` shipped as "the fix" and it failed again identically. The signature is the one `verify.yml`'s own comment documents as intermittent *on master* (which is why the job is `continue-on-error`), confirmed by checking master runs as that comment instructs — 2 of the 4 most recent failed, with the runs either side passing. **Read that comment before debugging that job.** The clearance change was kept on its own merits: an enemy is a solid body and only the exact exit tile was ever protected, so one could gate the exit until killed; levels with an enemy within 2.5 tiles of the exit went 5 (pre-rework) / 4 (post) -> 1, with enemy counts unchanged.

- [x] **Exception Zone acid decays after you touch it (2026-08-02).** Idea from watching the bot cross `stage06_pipeline.py`'s `try` gauntlet: it should behave like a Mario falling block. The gauntlet is deliberately unavoidable, but the zone dead-ends, so the toll was being charged twice — in and back out through identical acid at full `HAZARD_DPS`. Acid only (spikes already cycle, a mine is consumed on trigger), exception zones only (`fillHazards` room pools stay permanent terrain), 2.5s from first contact. **Derives the grid from `touchedAt` every tick instead of emitting a tile mutation**: a decay *would* satisfy `pendingGridDelta`'s "every mutation is `{value: 0}`" invariant, unlike Acid Overflow's `0 -> HAZARD_TILE`, but the channel is additive-only, so a guest that mispredicted contact could burn out a tile the host hasn't and never put it back. Deriving retracts as readily as it applies, and is safe for the same reason `acidOverflow.ts`'s is — the tile set is fixed at generation time and disjoint from every other claimer. `gridVersion` is not bumped: acid is walkable either way. Also found: `renderMinimap` painted `GameMap.hazards` with no grid re-check, so a burned-out tile would have kept its marker and routed the player the long way round nothing.

- [x] *(superseded — kept only for the mechanism description, see the corrected entry above)* **oscillation on atan2's ±π branch cut; two fixes attempted, both A/B'd and REVERTED.** *Attempt 2* (`b540533`+`be311c4`, reverted `0f0bcde`): `plainNavTurnSign` pinned the turn direction while the heading sat on the cut. Full protocol, n=12/combo/side: oscillation **+10.9% / +23.4% / +2.6%** (Casual/Gamer/Pro), levelTime +7.1/+4.4/-4.9%, one guard breach (Gamer L1 stuck 0->1). Win condition inverted on 3 of 3. **Method lesson:** it was committed on an n=8 single-combo reading of **-37.4%**; the same combo at n=12 gave **+23.4%**. n=8 on one combo cannot carry a directional claim. *Attempt 1* (`9d49f4d` -> `0fb6f04`): gated `diagonalStrafeKey` on `strafeIsSafe`; findings 520 -> 570 (+9.6%) and inconsistent.

| combo | oscillation ticks/1k dec | levelTimeSec | routeFollowOverhead | guards |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Casual/normal | **+10.9%** | +7.1% | +2.3% | pass |
| Gamer/normal | **+23.4%** | +4.4% | +4.2% | BREACHED — L1 stuck 0->1 |
| Pro/hard | **+2.6%** | -4.9% | -5.6% | pass |

**The registered win condition inverted on 3 of 3 combos.** Pro/hard did improve on route and time, but there is no win on the target metric to weigh that against, so the pre-registered rule applies: revert the stage, do not tune it forward. **Method lesson, the important one:** an earlier n=8 single-combo reading on Gamer/normal showed **-37.4%** and the change was committed on it; the same combo at n=12 gives **+23.4%**. n=8 on one combo cannot carry a directional claim — that is exactly what the 3-combo protocol is for, and labelling the result "directional, not gated" at commit time did not make shipping it correct. **What survives the revert** (kept deliberately, since it is what made the failure visible rather than being part of it): the tick-based anomaly tally in `anomalySummary`, `ticksPerKiloDecision` normalization, `diffAnomalies` in the A/B report, and `CODEENSTEIN_TELEMETRY_TUNING`. **What to try next:** both attempts so far removed or overrode a *symptom* of the flip (attempt 1 the strafe, attempt 2 the turn sign) and both made the detector fire more. That the two opposite interventions both backfire suggests the wobble is load-bearing convergence behaviour rather than waste, and the next attempt should probably target the overshoot that causes the sign to alternate at all (`turnBurstMs` sizing vs the decision window) rather than the sign itself.
